「亲爱的，」西尔弗斯泰尔斯夫人接着说，「记住，你如今可不在美国了。英国是欧洲最虚伪的国家。美国是全世界最虚伪的国家。这就是我们所谓的进步。而法国旧派守旧，倒不苛责于人。我承认，我从前是苛责的。可如今不了。我不苛责了，是因为像这样一种 *état d’âme*（法语：心境），虽然新潮，却有失风度。」

「我是个结了婚的老太婆啦，」这位还不满二十二岁的女士又添一句。「可我要是没结婚——譬如说，要是像你这样，自由自在，独立自主，人又不吓人，要在爱情与婚姻之间挑一样，那我一秒钟都不用犹豫。一秒都不用。」

蕾拉放下茶杯。「你当然用不着犹豫。若能重来一次，你还是会嫁给西尔弗斯泰尔斯，而且是为爱而嫁。这于我已经完了，永远地完了。」

===Original===
Narrowly, out of a corner of an eye, Violet considered her. “He was such a brute, was he?”

“Who? Gulian, do you mean?”

“I suppose so. There has been no other, has there?”

“Violet!”

It was at this juncture, for the fiftieth time, that Lady Silverstairs exclaimed:
===Chinese===
维奥莱特从眼角把她细细打量。「他就那么粗暴，是吗？」

「谁？你是说格里安？」

「我想是吧。此外没有别人了，对不对？」

「维奥莱特！」

正是在这个节骨眼上，西尔弗斯泰尔斯夫人第五十次嚷道：

===Original===
“It is downright mean of you to keep me in the dark. What was it that happened? Make a soiled breast of it. Do!”

For the fiftieth time Leilah protested:

“Don’t ask me. Don’t. He knows and that is enough. As for me I am trying to forget.”

“And you think Barouffski will help you. But has it ever occurred to you that if you were not very rich he might lack the incentive?”
===Chinese===
「你把我蒙在鼓里，实在刻薄。到底出了什么事？把你那桩脏心事一吐为快吧。说吧！」

蕾拉第五十次抗议：

「别问我。别问了。他知道，这就够了。至于我，我在努力忘掉。」

「你以为巴罗夫斯基能帮上你。可你想过没有，若不是你腰缠万贯，他哪来的这股劲头？」

===Original===
To this Leilah assented. “He said he is poor.”

“At least he does not exaggerate. I told Silverstairs that he was after you for your money and he said that was what he married me for. So he did and I married him for his title. It was a fair bargain. Now if we had it to do over I would say﻿—I would say﻿—well, I would say that it is better to have loved your husband than never to have loved at all. But six months hence, if you had it to do over, do you think you could say as much﻿—or as little?”

“At least I could say that I did not marry for a title.”

“Well, hardly, particularly a Polish one, though I daresay even that might be useful in the servants’ hall. But what could you say you married for? It isn’t love?”
===Chinese===
对此蕾拉表示同意。「他说过他很穷。」

「至少他不夸口。我跟西尔弗斯泰尔斯说，他是冲着你的钱来的，他说他娶我图的不也是这个。可不是嘛，我图的是他的头衔。这桩买卖公平合理。如今若能重来一遍，我要说——我要说——唔，我要说：宁可爱过自己的丈夫，也强过从未爱过。可是再过半年，倘若让你重来一遍，你想你还能说出这样一番话——或者这么一点点——吗？」

「至少我可以说，我不是为头衔嫁的。」

「可不是嘛，何况还是个波兰头衔，不过我料想，就连这玩意儿在仆役厅里或许也派得上用场。可你能说你图的是什么？总不是为了爱吧？」

===Original===
“No.”

“Nor position?”

“No.”

“Then what on earth﻿—”
===Chinese===
「不。」

「也不是为了地位？」

「不。」

「那么究竟——」

===Original===
“Violet, how hard you make it for me. Can’t you see that if I do, it will be for protection?”

“For protection! Merciful fathers! You talk like a chorus girl! Protection against what? Against whom? Verplank?”

“No.” Leilah choking down something in her throat, replied: “Against myself.”

“I don’t understand you,” said Violet slowly. But she did or thought she did, and that night told Silverstairs that Leilah was still in love with her ex.
===Chinese===
「维奥莱特，你真叫我为难。难道你看不出来，我若嫁，是为了求个庇护？」

「求庇护！老天爷！你说话像个合唱队女郎！防什么？防谁？防弗普兰克？」

「不。」蕾拉咽下喉头涌上的什么，答道：「防我自己。」

「我不明白你，」维奥莱特缓缓地说。可她明白，或自以为明白。当晚她便告诉西尔弗斯泰尔斯，蕾拉仍旧爱着她的前夫。

===Original===
It was in these circumstances that Leilah listened to the Count Kasimiérz Barouffski, who, in telling her that he was poor, omitted to add that he had resources. These were women and cards. It is a business like another. But even to his nearest friends, to Tyszkiewicz, a compatriot, and Palencia, a Corsican, he did not boast of it. He had therefore some sense of shame, but not of honour, though with humour he was supplied. A man with some sense of humour and no sense of honour may go far. Barouffski intended to. After his volcanic introduction to Leilah, he beheld in her not the woman but the opportunity which chance had sent his way. To grasp that he displayed every art of which the Slav is capable. He did more. He impressed her not with the nobility of his name but of his nature. He was a good actor and though at first unsuccessful he was not discouraged. It was an axiom of his that among the dice of destiny there is always a golden six. It was axiomatic with him also that it is not tossed at once. To deserve it, one must wait. Barouffski waited. Presently fate shook the box before him. The golden six was his.

But not the box.

To Leilah the mere idea of matrimony was abhorrent. Yet she could not stop indefinitely with the Silverstairs. She had no relatives with whom she could reside. She felt that it would be awkward and perhaps equivocal for her to have an establishment of her own. But these considerations were minor beside another﻿—a sense, haunting and constant, that the excursion to Nevada had been inadequate, that the past needed a surer barricade.
===Chinese===
蕾拉就是在这样的境况里，听任卡西米耶日·巴罗夫斯基伯爵倾诉的。此人告诉她自己穷，却忘了补充一句：他自有生财的门路。门路便是女人与牌局。这也是一门行当，同别的行当并无二致。但即便对最亲近的朋友——对同胞蒂什凯维奇（Tyszkiewicz）、对科西嘉人帕伦西亚（Palencia）——他也不夸耀这门营生。可见他还残存几分羞耻之心，只是并无荣誉之心；幽默感倒是备得足足的。一个有几分幽默感而毫无荣誉感的人，前程可以走得很远。巴罗夫斯基正打算走远。在与蕾拉那场火山爆发式的相识之后，他在她身上看到的不是女人，而是机缘——命运顺手送到他眼前的一个机会。为抓住它，他使出了斯拉夫人所能有的一切手段。他还有更进一步的。打动她的，不是他姓氏的高贵，而是他天性的高贵。他是个好演员，起初虽不顺利，却并不气馁。他有一条信条：命运的骰子里，总藏着一枚金六点。他还有一条不言自明的信条：那金六点不会一掷而出。要配得上它，必须等。巴罗夫斯基等着。不久，命运在他面前摇响了骰盅。金六点到手了。

只是骰盅没有到手。

对蕾拉来说，单单是结婚这个念头就令她憎厌。然而她不能在西尔弗斯泰尔斯家无限期地住下去。她没有可以投靠同住的亲戚。她觉得，自己另立门户，未免尴尬，甚而暧昧可疑。但这些考量在另一桩面前都是小事——那是一种纠缠不休、无时或释的感觉：内华达之行并不足以了事，过去需要一道更牢靠的屏障。

===Original===
It was not a husband that she wanted. Peace and security were the fleshpots that she craved. These Barouffski offered or seemed to﻿—and it was these finally and these only that she agreed to accept.

To the implied stipulation Barouffski consented with an air of high chivalry but also with an ambiguous smile. Given the golden six of her income, the box was a detail to him, and it was in these circumstances that over the perhaps insecurely locked door of her past, this mask mounted guard.

The news of the engagement, filtering through the press, was cabled to the States, together with the fact that Leilah was then stopping in the Rue François Premier with Lady Silverstairs, whose portrait, in addition to bogus presentments of the engaged couple, were printed in the minor sheets that circulate from New York to San Francisco.

On arriving from Australia at the latter city, Verplank happened on a belated copy. Since he had gone from Coronado, this, the first news of his wife, was her engagement to another man.
===Chinese===
她要的并不是丈夫。安宁与保障，才是她渴求的埃及肉锅。这两样巴罗夫斯基都肯给，或似乎肯给——而她最终答应接受的，也恰恰只有这两样。

对这项心照不宣的条款，巴罗夫斯基以一副崇高的骑士气概应允下来，嘴角却挂着一丝含混的笑。既然她收入里那枚金六点已经到手，骰盅对他不过是细枝末节。而正是在这样的境况之下，在她那扇或许锁得不甚牢靠的往昔之门上，这张面具登上了岗。

订婚的消息经报端渗漏出去，拍到了美国，连蕾拉正下榻于弗朗索瓦一世街西尔弗斯泰尔斯夫人处的消息也一并拍了去；她的玉照，连同订婚二人拼凑出来的画像，一并印在了从纽约到旧金山流传的小报上。

刚从澳洲抵达旧金山的弗普兰克，碰巧看到一份迟到的报纸。自他离开科罗纳多以来，这关于他妻子的头一条新闻，竟是她的订婚——与另一个男人。

===Original===
In his amazement his thoughts stuttered. Into his mind entered stretches of night. He looked at the sheet without seeing it. But the paragraph and the purport of it, already photographed on the films of the brain, were prompting him unconsciously, and it was without really knowing what he was saying that he exclaimed:

“Leilah! My wife! In Paris! Engaged to another man!”

The names, the words, the meaning of them all, beat on his brain like blows of a hammer.

“Leilah! My wife! In Paris! Engaged!”

Again he looked at the sheet. “What a damned lie!” he ragingly cried, and, rumpling the paper, threw it from him.
===Chinese===
惊愕之中，他的思绪打起结巴。一段段黑夜涌进他的脑海。他望着那报纸，视而不见。但那段文字连同它的含意，早已拍在脑子的底片上，在无意识中驱使着他，于是他并不真正知道自己说了什么，便喊出声来：

「蕾拉！我的妻子！在巴黎！跟别人订了婚！」

那些名字、那些字眼、它们全部的含意，像一记记锤击，砸在他的脑上。

「蕾拉！我的妻子！在巴黎！订婚了！」

他又看了看那报纸。「该死的弥天大谎！」他狂怒地吼道，一把将报纸揉皱，摔了出去。

===Original===
But now, the names, the words, the meaning of them all, well beaten into him, readjusted themselves, presenting a picture perfectly defined and possibly real.

He stooped, gathered the paper, smoothed it, read the account again.

After all, he reflected, it might be that she was in Paris and, if there, it was natural that she would be with Violet Silverstairs. These two items were, therefore, not improbably correct. That view reached, the deduction followed: If they are correct, the other may be. Yet, in that case, he argued, obviously she must think me dead. On the heels of this second deduction an impression trod﻿—the ease and dispatch with which she had become consoled.

Enraged at once, angered already by what he had taken for a lie and then infuriated by what he took for truth, the anterior incidents that had this supreme outrage for climax, leaped at him. At the onslaught the primitive passions flared, and it was with the impulse of the homicide that he determined to seek and overwhelm this woman who accepted men and matters with such entire sans-gêne.

On the morrow he left for New York. Before going he sent a cablegram to the address which the paper had supplied:
===Chinese===
可是此刻，那些名字、字眼及其全部含意，已被好好敲打进他身体里，重新排列成形，呈现出一幅轮廓分明、且可能真实的图画。

他俯身拾起报纸，抹平，又把那段报道读了一遍。

他想，归根到底，她也许真在巴黎；若在巴黎，她和维奥莱特·西尔弗斯泰尔斯待在一起便是顺理成章。如此说来，这两条消息多半不假。这看法一经成立，推论随之而来：倘若这两条是真的，另一条也可能真。然而果真如此，他辩论道，那她分明是当我死了。紧随这第二条推论，一个印象又踏步而来——她得到慰藉，竟如此轻易、如此利落。

他先是被那被他当作谎言的东西激怒，继而又被那被他当作真相的东西激到狂怒，一怒再怒之际，先前种种——以这桩奇耻大辱为顶点——朝他猛扑过来。在这一击之下，原始的激情轰然腾起，于是他怀着杀人犯的冲动，决意去寻出并压倒这个以全然的 *sans-gêne*（法语：毫无拘忌）接纳男人和世事的女人。

次日一早他动身去纽约。临行前，他按报纸提供的地址发了一封电报：

===Original===
> Am just apprised of the studied insult of your engagement to some foreign cad. Leaving for Paris at once.

As he signed it, deeply, beneath the breath, he swore. “That will show her,” he added.

It so happened that it showed her nothing. Leilah was not then in the Rue François Premier, but in the Rue de la Pompe, where the message followed, but only to be received by Barouffski, who read it with a curious smile.

Already he hated Verplank, who had not yet acquired a hatred for him. But though that hatred had not been acquired, it developed tumultuously when, on arriving in New York, he learned that not merely the report of the engagement was true, but that the engagement had since resulted in a marriage, which itself had been preceded by a Nevada divorce.
===Chinese===
> 兹悉尔与某外国无赖订婚、蓄意辱我一事。即刻启程赴巴黎。

签发电报之际，他在呼吸底下狠狠地咒骂了一声。「这会给她点厉害瞧。」他又补了一句。

偏偏这电报什么也没给她看到。蕾拉当时并不在弗朗索瓦一世街，而是在蓬佩街（Rue de la Pompe）；电报追了过去，却落到巴罗夫斯基手里，他带着一种古怪的微笑读了它。

他早已恨着弗普兰克，而后者那时还来不及恨上他。不过这恨意虽未生成，却在弗普兰克抵达纽约、听闻订婚不仅属实，且订婚之后已成连理、而成婚之前还先有一场内华达离婚之时，汹汹然生成爆发了。

===Original===
In comparison to all that had occurred, the divorce seemed at the time almost negligible. It was the crowning infamy of this marriage which, in renewing the primitive passions, aroused in Verplank a determination not merely to seek and overwhelm the woman, but to seek and destroy the man. The marriage, he decided could be but the result of an anterior affair, there was no other explanation of it. The idea that had come to him at Coronado, the possibility that she might have left him because informed of some affair of his own and which since then he had examined again and again, fell utterly away. It was not because of errors of his that she had gone, but for turpitudes of her own. Then to his anger at her was added a hatred of Barouffski, whom he had never seen, and who, without having seen Verplank, hated him also, hated him retrospectively and prospectively, hated him because clearly Leilah had been his and﻿—where women are concerned, all things being possible﻿—might be again.

But though Barouffski hated Verplank actively, he hated him vaguely, as one must when one hates the unknown. It was the cablegram which, in supplying the personal element, made the hate concrete.

“Foreign cad, eh?” he repeated, with a curious smile. “*Eh bien, nous verrons*, we shall see.”

Presently the opportunity occurred. For it was in these circumstances, a fortnight after the receipt of the cablegram, that, directed by the young Baronne de Fresnoy, he turned and saw Verplank entering the room where he stood.
===Chinese===
与已然发生的这一切相比，那场离婚在当时简直无足轻重。真正登峰造极的奇耻是这桩婚姻。它重新点燃原始的激情，在弗普兰克心中唤起的决心，便不只是寻出并压倒那个女人，而是寻出并除掉那个男人。这婚姻，他断定，只能是先前一段奸情的产物，别无其他解释。当初在科罗纳多闪过的那个念头——她可能因为听说了他自己某桩风流韵事而离开他、此后又被他一遍遍推敲过的念头——彻底垮掉了。她的出走并非因为他的过错，而是因为她自己的秽行。于是，对她的怒火之上，又添上了对巴罗夫斯基的仇恨——一个他从未见过的人；而那人也从未见过弗普兰克，却同样恨他，追溯既往地恨，瞻望将来地恨，恨他，因为蕾拉分明曾是他的人，而——在女人的事情上，一切皆有可能——说不定还会再度是。

不过巴罗夫斯基虽恨得积极，却恨得空洞，恨一个素不相识的人，也只能如此。是那封电报补上了个人的因素，把恨凝成了实体。

「外国无赖，嗯？」他带着那丝古怪的微笑重复道。「*Eh bien, nous verrons*（法语：好吧，咱们走着瞧），走着瞧吧。」

不久，机会来了。正是在这样的情势之下——收到电报两周后——经年轻的德·弗雷努瓦男爵夫人（Baronne de Fresnoy）指点，他转过身，看见弗普兰克正走进他站立的那个房间。

## IV / 四

===Original===
With the unerring instinct of the man of the world, Verplank, on entering the crowded salon, divined immediately, among all the women present, the hostess whom he had never seen.
As he bent over her hand, the duchess, who had not an idea how he came there, said in her fluted voice:
“This is really so nice of you. I did not know you were in Paris.”
“Nor did I﻿—until this moment,” answered Verplank, looking as he spoke into the eyes of his hostess who, after the one imperceptible glance with which the mondaine judges and classifies, was wondering in what manner, this man, with his virile face and impeccable presence, had forced Leilah Barouffska to leave him.

===Chinese===
凭着阅世之人那种不会出错的直觉，弗普兰克一走进这间拥挤的沙龙，立刻就在在场的所有女人中间，认出了这位他从未谋面的女主人。
他俯身吻她的手时，公爵夫人——全然不知他是怎么来的——用她那长笛般的嗓音说道：
「您能来，真是太好了。我不知道您在巴黎。」
「我此前也不知道——直到此刻，」弗普兰克答道，一边说，一边望着女主人的眼睛。而她，在交际场中人用来品评和归类的那难以察觉的一瞥之后，正在纳闷：这个男人，凭着他那张刚毅的脸和无懈可击的仪态，是以何种方式逼得蕾拉·巴罗夫斯卡离开他的。

===Original===
“But,” he added, “Monsieur de Joyeuse whom I saw this afternoon told me that you would be at home, and assured me that I might venture to present my homages.”
The duchess displayed her tireless smile. “I am only sorry not to have had them sooner.” She paused. Between her smile, the edges of her teeth showed, false but beautiful. “There is Lady Silverstairs trying to get you to look at her, and very well worth looking at she is.”
Camille de Joyeuse turned for a moment to the reticent young prince who in his diffident way still lingered at her side.
Beyond, at the farther end of the room, notes rippled. Standing near a grand piano, the Romanian with the flowing hair was preluding a fantasy of his own.

===Chinese===
「不过，」他补充道，「我今天下午见到的德·茹瓦耶兹先生告诉我，说您今晚会客，还向我保证，我或许可以冒昧前来致敬。」
公爵夫人展露她那不知疲倦的微笑。「只是遗憾没能早些领受您的敬意。」她顿了顿。在她的微笑之间，露出牙齿的边缘，假而漂亮。「西尔弗斯泰尔斯夫人正在设法让您看她呢，而她也确实很值得看。」
卡米耶·德·茹瓦耶兹转向那位沉默寡言的年轻亲王，他仍以他那种羞怯的样子逡巡在她身旁。
再过去，在房间那一头，音符淙淙作响。那位长发披垂的罗马尼亚人正立在一架大钢琴旁，为他自己的一首幻想曲起着前奏。

===Original===
In the hush that succeeded, Verplank moved to where Violet sat.
Smilingly, without speaking, she gave him her hand and indicated a seat beside her. Then, raising a fan, she whispered:
“Demon! What have you done? Where do you spring from? How long have you been in Paris?”
Verplank, seating himself, answered:

===Chinese===
在随之而来的寂静里，弗普兰克挪到维奥莱特坐的地方。
她微笑着，一言不发，把手伸给他，指了指身边的一个座位。随后，她举起一把折扇，悄声道：
「魔王！你干了什么？你从哪儿冒出来的？来巴黎多久了？」
弗普兰克落了座，答道：

===Original===
“I got here this morning. Why am I a demon?”
From behind the fan, Violet asked:
“What did you do to Leilah? Why did she leave you?”
Verplank folded his gloved hands. “That is what I am here to find out.”

===Chinese===
「今天早上到的。我怎么就成了魔王？」
维奥莱特从扇子后面问：
「你对蕾拉做了什么？她为什么离开你？」
弗普兰克把戴着手套的双手交叠起来。「这正是我来这里要查明的。」

===Original===
“Do you mean to tell me that you don’t know!”
“I have not an idea﻿—unless it was because of this Count Thingumagig.”
Violet Silverstairs furled her fan, looked at him, looked away, looked about the room. At one end her husband, accompanied by de Joyeuse, Tempest, de Fresnoy, and the others, had entered. At the further end the Romanian dominated. Supported *en sourdine* by an accompanist, he massed sounds and dispersed them, concentrating fulgurations of notes from which echoing showers fell. Presently, resuming an abandoned measure, he caressed a largo, infinitely sweet, that swooned in the languors of the finale. At once to a murmur of bravos, the applause of gloved hands and a cry of “Bis!” raising violin and bow above his head, he bent double to the duchess, his flowing hair falling like a veil before him.
“He may play again,” said Violet. “I want to talk to you. Let us go into the next room.”

===Chinese===
「你是要告诉我，你居然不知道！」
「我一点头绪也没有——除非是因为那位『某某伯爵』。」
维奥莱特·西尔弗斯泰尔斯收拢折扇，看看他，又把目光移开，环顾整个房间。房间一头，她丈夫由德·茹瓦耶兹、坦皮斯特、德·弗雷努瓦和其余众人陪着刚刚进来；另一头，那位罗马尼亚人君临一切。在一位伴奏者*en sourdine*（弱音衬托）的支持下，他把音响聚拢又驱散，凝集出一阵阵音符的闪电，闪电后面落下回声的阵雨。片刻之后，他重拾一段搁下的节拍，抚弄出一段广板，甜美无限，在终曲的慵懒中沉沉晕去。随即，在一阵「好」的低语、戴手套的双掌的掌声和一声「Bis!」（再来一遍）中，他把琴和弓高举过头，向公爵夫人深深折腰，长发像一面纱幕垂落在身前。
「他也许还会再演奏，」维奥莱特说。「我想跟你谈谈。我们去隔壁房间吧。」

===Original===
As Verplank rose at her bidding, others who had been seated, rose also. Interrupted conversations were more animatedly resumed. A servant announced additional names. The first salon now was thronged. The second was filled. Verplank and Violet passed on.
Beyond was a gallery. At the entrance stood a woman, her face averted, talking to a man. As the others approached, she turned.
At sight of her and of the man, Violet would have turned also. It was too late.
“Leilah!” Verplank exclaimed.

===Chinese===
弗普兰克应命起身，其他坐着的人也随之起立。被打断的谈话更热络地重新开始。仆人通报着新到的名字。第一间沙龙现在已济济一堂，第二间也满了。弗普兰克和维奥莱特走了过去。
再过去是一条画廊。入口处站着一个女人，脸转向一边，正与一个男人说话。众人走近时，她转过身来。
一看见她和那个男人，维奥莱特本想也转身避开。太迟了。
「蕾拉！」弗普兰克失声喊道。

===Original===
For a second, in tragic silence, two beings whom love had joined and fate had separated, stood, staring into each other’s eyes.
For a second only. At once the man interposed himself between them.
“Monsieur!” he insolently threw out. “My name is Barouffski.”
With superior tact Lady Silverstairs intervened. “Good evening, Count. It never occurred to us that we were interrupting a tête-à-tête.”

===Chinese===
有那么一秒钟，在悲剧性的沉默中，两个曾被爱结合、又被命运拆开的人，站在那里，直视着彼此的眼睛。
只有一秒钟。那男人立刻插到两人中间。
「先生！」他无礼地抛出一句。「我叫巴罗夫斯基。」
西尔弗斯泰尔斯夫人以高人一筹的机敏插了进来。「晚上好，伯爵。我们压根没想到打扰了一场密谈。」

===Original===
She paused. Hostilely the two men were measuring each other. In Verplank’s face there was a threat, in Barouffski’s there was a jeer, in Leilah’s there was an expression of absolute terror. Of the little group Violet alone appeared at ease.
“Leilah,” she added, “don’t forget that you are to have luncheon with me tomorrow. Good night, my dear. Silverstairs and I will be going soon. Good night, Barouffski.”
She smiled, nodded, took Verplank’s arm, took him away. But the arm beneath her hand was shaking and she realised that it shook with rage.
Sympathetically she looked up at him. “I thought they were in the other room and it was just to avoid a thing of this sort that I got you out of it. You won’t do anything, will you?”

===Chinese===
她顿了顿。两个男人敌意地对峙着，彼此打量。弗普兰克的脸上是威胁，巴罗夫斯基的脸上是讥诮，蕾拉的脸上是一种绝对的惊恐。这一小群人里，唯有维奥莱特显得从容。
「蕾拉，」她补了一句，「别忘了明天要来和我共进午餐。晚安，亲爱的。西尔弗斯泰尔斯和我一会儿就走。晚安，巴罗夫斯基。」
她微笑，点头，挽起弗普兰克的手臂，把他带走了。可她手底下那条手臂在颤抖，而且她明白，它是因狂怒而颤抖。
她同情地抬头看他。「我本以为他们在另一间屋子里，正是为了躲开这种场面，我才把你领出来的。你不会做什么吧，会吗？」

===Original===
Verplank now had got control of himself, his arm no longer shook, and it was the smile of a man of the world, the smile of one to whom nothing is important and much absurd, that he answered:
“Why, yes; it was very civil of this chap to introduce himself. I shall leave a card on him. Hello! Here’s Silverstairs! I wonder if he will introduce himself, too.”
The young earl was advancing, his hand outstretched. “I say! I saw a man marching off with the missis, but I had no idea it was you. Where are you stopping? Will you dine with us Tuesday?”
“Yes, do.” Violet threw in. “Rue François Premier at eight.”

===Chinese===
弗普兰克此刻已控制住自己，手臂不再颤抖，他以一种阅世之人的微笑——那种觉得没有一件事要紧、而许多事荒唐的人才有的微笑——答道：
「哎，会的；这位老兄自我介绍，很有礼貌嘛。我会给他留一张名片的。喂！西尔弗斯泰尔斯来了！不知道他会不会也来个自我介绍。」
年轻的伯爵正走过来，手伸在前头。「我说！我看见一个人把夫人带走，可没想到是你。你住在哪儿？星期二来和我们吃饭好吗？」
「来吧，一定。」维奥莱特插了一句。「弗朗索瓦一世街（Rue François Premier），八点。」

===Original===
“I shall be very glad to,” Verplank answered. He turned to Silverstairs. “I am at the Ritz. Stop by there tomorrow noon, won’t you, and let me take you somewhere for luncheon?”
Lady Silverstairs laughed and employing a darkyism, said: “You don’t say turkey to me. There!” she exclaimed as Verplank was about to protest. “I could not anyway.”
From the salon beyond came a woman’s voice, clear and rich, rendering, in a lascive contralto, a song of love and passion.
The Silverstairs and Verplank approached. Meanwhile, from the diva’s mouth, notes darted like serpents on fire. In mounting fervour the aria developed, trailing, as it climbed, words such as *amore*, *speranza*, *morir*. A breath of brutality passed. The atmosphere became charged with emanations in which the perfume of women mingled with the desires of men. Still the aria mounted, it coloured the air, projecting, like a magic lantern, visions of delight, imperial and archaic, that ascended in glittering scales.

===Chinese===
「我很乐意，」弗普兰克答道。他转向西尔弗斯泰尔斯。「我住在里兹（the Ritz）。明天中午到那儿来找我，有空吗？让我带你去哪儿吃顿午饭。」
西尔弗斯泰尔斯夫人笑了，用了一句美国黑人的俗话：「你可别拿火鸡哄我。」「得啦！」弗普兰克正要抗议，她叫道。「反正我也去不成。」
从那边沙龙里传来一个女人的嗓音，清亮而丰润，用一副妖冶的女低音，演唱着一首爱与激情的歌。
西尔弗斯泰尔斯夫妇和弗普兰克走近前去。与此同时，从那位名伶的口中，音符像着火的蛇一样窜出。热忱步步高涨，咏叹调层层展开，一路攀升，拖曳着诸如*amore*、*speranza*、*morir*（爱、希望、死亡）这样的词。一阵残暴的气息掠过。空气变得充盈着种种气味，女人的香水和男人的欲望在其中交混。咏叹调还在攀升，它给空气着了色，像一盏魔术灯，投射出种种欢愉的幻象，帝王的、太古的，沿着闪闪发光的音阶冉冉上升。

===Original===
Verplank, detaching himself from the Silverstairs, felt his dumb rage renewed. At the moment he conceived an insane idea of going below, waiting without until Barouffski and Leilah appeared and he saw himself, confronting the man, tearing the woman from him, carrying her off and making her his own.
The impulse fell from him. The rage that he felt at the man deflected into rage at this woman who had made his life a vacant house and for what, good God! And why?
In a cascade of flowers and flames the song was ending. There was new applause, the discreet approbation of worldly people, easily pleased, as easily bored and with but one sure creed: Not too much of anything.
Verplank must also have had enough. When presently the Silverstairs looked about for him he had gone.

===Chinese===
弗普兰克从西尔弗斯泰尔斯夫妇身边脱开，感到他无言的狂怒又涌了上来。此刻他生出一个疯狂的念头：下楼去，在外面等着，等巴罗夫斯基和蕾拉出来；他仿佛看见自己迎住那个男人，把女人从他手里夺过来，把她带走，让她归于自己。
这股冲动从他身上落下去了。他对那个男人的怒火，转而变成对这个女人的怒火——她把他的生活变成了一座空屋子，又是为了什么，天哪！到底为什么？
在一阵花与火焰的瀑布中，歌声临近尾声。掌声再度响起，那是世俗之人有分寸的赞许——他们轻易地满足，也轻易地厌倦，只有一条确凿的信条：任何东西都不要太多。
弗普兰克想必也受够了。过了一会儿，西尔弗斯泰尔斯夫妇环顾找他时，他已经走了。

===Original===
Already Violet had summarised the situation to her lord. Now, perplexed at Verplank’s abrupt disappearance, she said:
“You don’t suppose that anything will happen, do you?”
Silverstairs, bored by the entertainment, anxious only to get away where he could have a quiet drink, tugged at his moustache and with unconscious reminiscence answered:
“I don’t know and I don’t care. I don’t care what happens as long as it doesn’t happen to me.”

===Chinese===
维奥莱特早已向她的夫君概括了局势。此刻，她对弗普兰克的突然消失感到困惑，说：
「你不至于会觉得会发生什么事吧？」
西尔弗斯泰尔斯被这番消遣弄得无聊，只想脱身去寻一个能安静喝一杯的地方。他捻着胡须，带着不自知的旧调重弹，答道：
「我不知道，也不在乎。只要不落到我头上，发生什么我都不在乎。」

## V / 五

===Original===
“There are too many of us,” Verplank, the day following, found himself saying to Silverstairs.
The two men were lunching at Voisin’s.
The charming resort which, since the passing of Véry, of Véfour and the Maison Dorée, has become the ultimate refuge of the high gastronomic muse of Savarin and of Brisse was, on this forenoon, filled with its usual clientele:﻿—old men with pink cheeks, young women with ravishing hats, cosmopolitan sportsmen, ladies of both worlds, assortments of what Paris calls High Life and pronounces Hig Leaf.
Without, a fog draped the windows, blurred the movement of the street, transforming it into a cinematograph of misty silhouettes. But within, the brilliant damask, the glittering service, the studied excellence of everything, produced an atmosphere of wealth and ease.
===Chinese===
“我们这样的人太多了。”第二天，弗普兰克发觉自己对西尔弗斯泰尔斯这样说。
两人正在瓦赞餐厅（Voisin’s）用午餐。
自从韦里（Véry）、韦富尔（Véfour）和金屋餐厅（Maison Dorée）相继谢幕之后，这家迷人的去处便成了萨瓦兰（Savarin）与布里斯（Brisse）那崇高的美食缪斯的最后庇护所。这个上午，店里照例坐满了它的老主顾：——粉颊的老头儿，帽子摄人心魄的年轻女人，世界主义的运动家，两个世界里的太太们，还有巴黎人所谓的 High Life（上流社会）、念出来却成了 Hig Leaf 的那一路人物。
外面，雾气蒙住窗子，把街上的动静晕成一片，化作一台放映着雾影幢幢人形的电影机。里面，鲜亮的锦缎、熠熠的银器、处处刻意经营的精美，营造出一种富足而安适的气氛。

===Original===
Silverstairs, after swallowing a glass of Chablis, meditatively lit a cigar. But meditation was not his forte. The twentieth of his name, he was tall and robust. He had straw-coloured hair, blue eyes, a skin of brick, and an appearance of simple placidity. At the moment he was mentally fondling certain reminiscences of the Isis and certain bouts with bargees there.
“You know,” he said at last, “if I were you I would just march up to him and knock him down.”
Verplank nodded. “I dare say. But not if he had taken your wife.”
The suggestion, penetrating the earl’s placidity, punctured it. He threw back his head. “By George! If he had, I’d kill him.”
“There, you see!”
Silverstairs puffed at his cigar. His placidity now was reforming itself.
===Chinese===
西尔弗斯泰尔斯灌下一杯夏布利（Chablis），若有所思地点起一支雪茄。可沉思本非他的长项。他是这个姓氏的第二十代传人，高壮结实，稻草色头发，蓝眼睛，砖红色的皮肤，一副憨直平和的相貌。此刻他心里正回味着艾西斯河（the Isis）上的旧事，以及当年在河上同驳船夫的几场交手。
“要知道，”他终于开口，“我要是你，就径直走上去，一拳把他撂倒。”
弗普兰克点头。“说得是。可要是抢走的是你老婆，就不一样了。”
这话刺穿了伯爵的平和，把那层平和捅了个窟窿。他猛地仰起头。“老天！他要是敢，我就杀了他。”
“瞧见没有！”
西尔弗斯泰尔斯抽着雪茄。他的平和此刻正重新聚拢成形。

===Original===
“Yes,” he answered. “But then in taking yours, he did it after she was divorced. You can’t have him out for that.”
“All the same there are one too many of us.”
Silverstairs filled his mouth with smoke. Longly, with an air of considering the situation he expelled it. Then he said:
“It is what I call damned awkward. But what the deuce can you do?”
“What can I do?” Verplank with an uplift of the chin repeated. “Why, if only for the manner in which he acted last night﻿—”
“I know,” Silverstairs interrupted. “The missis told me. He behaved like a fidgety Frenchman. I grant you that. But there were no words, nothing that you could put a finger on.”
===Chinese===
“是，”他答道，“可话又说回来，他得到你那位，是在她离婚之后。为这个你不能约他决斗。”
“饶是这样，我们中间总是多出了一个人。”
西尔弗斯泰尔斯含了满口的烟，慢条斯理、一副权衡局势的样子把它吐出来。然后他说：
“这就是我所谓的该死的尴尬。可你究竟能怎么办呢？”
“我能怎么办？”弗普兰克扬了扬下巴，重复道。“嗨，单凭他昨晚的做派——”
“我知道，”西尔弗斯泰尔斯打断他，“我太太跟我说了。他表现得像个坐立不安的法国人。这个我认。可没说破一个字，没有什么你能指得出来的。”

===Original===
Through an adjacent door a man strolled in. He had his hat on and in one gloved hand he held a thin umbrella of which the handle was studded with gold nails. With the other hand he smoothed a black moustache. Through a monocle he was surveying the room. He looked careless and cynical.
Deferentially a maître d’hôtel addressed him. Ignoring the man he waved his umbrella at Silverstairs.
Silverstairs waved his hand. He turned to Verplank. “Here’s de Fresnoy. He can put us straight. Let’s ask him to join us.”
Rising, he greeted the Parisian, invited him to the table, introduced Verplank, speaking as he did so in French, with an accent frankly barbarous which de Fresnoy seemed to enjoy.
The latter raised his hat to Verplank, confided it to the maître d’hôtel, gave him the umbrella also, while another waiter drew up for him a chair.
“Thanks,” he said in an interval of these operations. “I see you have breakfasted. If you don’t mind my eating while you smoke﻿—”
===Chinese===
一扇侧门里踱进来一个男人。他戴着帽子，一只戴手套的手里拈一把细伞，伞柄上钉着金钉；另一只手捻着黑髭。他透过单片眼镜扫视餐厅，一副漫不经心、玩世不恭的样子。
一名 maître d’hôtel（侍者领班）恭敬地上前招呼他。他不理那人，朝西尔弗斯泰尔斯挥了挥伞。
西尔弗斯泰尔斯挥手作答，转向弗普兰克。“是德·弗雷努瓦。他能让咱们把是非摆正。请他过来坐吧。”
他起身迎接这位巴黎人，请他入座，把弗普兰克介绍给他，说话用的是法语，口音直白得近乎野蛮，德·弗雷努瓦似乎还听得津津有味。
后者向弗普兰克举了举帽，把帽子托付给侍者领班，伞也一并交了，另一名侍者则为他拉过一把椅子。
“多谢，”他在这一连串动作的间隙说，“看得出二位已经用过了。你们抽烟、我吃东西，不介意吧——”

===Original===
Seating himself he turned to the waiter, a man short and stout, completely bald, with large dyed whiskers and an air of repressed satisfaction.
“Listen, Léopold, and note well what I say. To begin with do not attempt to tell me what you wish me to eat. You have heard? Good! Listen again. A dozen Ostendes, an omelette, a pear. Nothing else. Not a crumb. Yes, some Eau de Vals. *Allez!*”
Léopold bowed. “Perfectly, monsieur le baron. I shall have the honour of serving monsieur le baron with what he has been good enough to be willing to desire.”
Again the waiter bowed. But behind the oleaginousness of his speech a severity had entered, one which intimated that in this preserve of gastronomics such an order was unworthy.
“These gentlemen?” he added, his eyes moving from Verplank to Silverstairs. “Some coffee? A liqueur?”
But now, in fluent French, Verplank was addressing de Fresnoy. “Silverstairs and I have been having an argument. In your quality of Parisian, will you tell us whether a man can have another out for looking impertinently at him?”
===Chinese===
他落了座，转向那侍者——一个矮胖汉子，头全秃了，两颊蓄着染黑的大连鬓胡，一副按捺着的得意。
“听着，莱奥波德（Léopold），把我的话记牢。第一，别打算告诉我该吃什么。听见没有？好！再听着。一打奥斯滕德牡蛎（Ostendes），一份煎蛋卷，一只梨。别的不要。一星面包屑也不要。对，再来点瓦尔泉水（Eau de Vals）。*Allez!*（快去！）
莱奥波德躬身。“遵命，男爵先生。能侍奉男爵先生、上男爵先生屈尊愿意想用的东西，是在下的荣幸。”
侍者又躬了一躬。可在那套油滑的客气话背后透出一种严峻，暗示在这片美食家的禁苑里，这样的点法实在有失身份。
“这两位先生呢？”他又补一句，眼睛从弗普兰克移到西尔弗斯泰尔斯，“咖啡？利口酒？”
这时弗普兰克已用流利的法语对德·弗雷努瓦说起话来。“西尔弗斯泰尔斯和我正争一件事。以您巴黎人的资格，可否告诉我们：一个人因别人无礼地盯着他看，就能约人决斗吗？”

===Original===
De Fresnoy adjusted his collar, patted his neckcloth. “But certainly, most assuredly. To look impertinently at a man constitutes an attack on his self esteem, which in itself is an integral part of his moral wealth. To omit to return a man’s bow, to neglect to take his proffered hand, to regard him in an offensive manner, are one and all so many assaults on his dignity.”
Verplank, pleased with this view of things, smiled. “Thanks. Mine has been assailed and I was in doubt how to rebuke the aggressor.”
“It is simple as ‘Good day.’ You have only to select two representatives and get them to put themselves in communication with him. If then he refuses to have friends of his meet yours, or if, afterward, he will neither apologise or fight, he is outlawed.”
De Fresnoy, as he spoke, made a gesture, a wide movement of the arm which indicated, or was intended to indicate, the uttermost limits of the world.
“It is Barouffski,” Silverstairs, with some idea that de Fresnoy might be aware of the anterior complication, threw out.
“Barouffski!” de Fresnoy repeated, his head held appreciatively a little to one side. “In a bout he is very clever. Barring d’Arcy, Helley-Quetgen”﻿—and myself he was about to add, but throwing the veil he desisted﻿—“I don’t know his equal. How he is on the field, personally I cannot say. But there, the absence of buttons, the absence of masks, the inevitable emotion, the sight of the other man, the consciousness of an injury to be maintained or avenged, the consciousness too of the definite character of any thrust you may give and particularly of any thrust you may receive, these things have such an effect that often the cleverest acts like a fool. On the boards, fencing is an exercise, it is an amusement. On the field, it is another man’s blood﻿—or yours. Though, after all, one is rarely killed except by one’s seconds.”
===Chinese===
德·弗雷努瓦正了正衣领，拍了拍领巾。“当然可以，千真万确。无礼地打量一个人，即是对他自尊心的攻击，而自尊心本身乃是他精神财富不可分割的一部分。不回人家的招呼，不接人家伸来的手，以冒犯的姿态注视人家——桩桩件件，都是对他尊严的侵犯。”
弗普兰克对这套道理颇为受用，笑了。“多谢。我的尊严受了侵犯，正不知如何回敬这位冒犯者。”
“这事简单得就像说一声‘日安’。您只消选出两位代表，让他们去与对方接洽。若他拒绝让他的朋友与您的朋友见面，或者事后既不肯道歉又不肯应战，他就在法外了。”
德·弗雷努瓦说着做了个手势，手臂大大地一挥，划出——或意在划出——这世界的最远边界。
“是巴罗夫斯基。”西尔弗斯泰尔斯说道，心想德·弗雷努瓦兴许知道先前的那段过节。
“巴罗夫斯基！”德·弗雷努瓦重复道，脑袋赏识地微微一偏。“论交锋，他非常厉害。除去达尔西（d’Arcy）、埃利-凯特根（Helley-Quetgen）”——“还有我”他差点补上一句，但话到嘴边又咽了回去——“我不知道谁堪与他匹敌。至于真到了场上如何，我个人无从说起。不过嘛，没有护胸钮，没有面罩，那份免不了的情绪，看得见对手这个人，意识到有一笔屈辱要撑住、要雪耻，还意识到你刺出的每一剑、尤其是你挨的每一剑都有着定生死的分量——这些凑在一起，效力之大，往往最灵巧的人也会打得像个笨蛋。在剑台上，击剑是一项运动、一种消遣。到了场上，那就是另一个人的血——或者你的血。不过说到底，能杀死一个人的，很少是别人，多半是他自己的证人。

===Original===
He turned to Verplank. “You fence? Or is it that you shoot?”
Verplank leaned back in his chair. “Oh, I suppose I can fire a gun.”
Silverstairs laughed. “I say now! You are too modest by half.” He looked at de Fresnoy. “Verplank is one of the crack shots of America.”
De Fresnoy turned again to Verplank. “You should demand pistols then. Barouffski draws well, but at twenty paces he is less sure of himself. Have you selected your seconds?”
“I suppose I may count on Silverstairs for one﻿—”
The young earl nodded. “That’s of course, and perhaps you, de Fresnoy, will act with me.”
===Chinese===
他转向弗普兰克。“您使剑？还是使枪？”
弗普兰克往椅背上一靠。“哦，我想我还能放响一杆枪。”
西尔弗斯泰尔斯大笑。“瞧你说的！你也太谦虚了一半。”他看向德·弗雷努瓦。“弗普兰克可是美国数得着的神枪手之一。”
德·弗雷努瓦又转向弗普兰克。“那您就该要求用手枪。巴罗夫斯基出剑漂亮，可到了二十步开外，他就不那么有把握了。您的证人选好了吗？”
“我想西尔弗斯泰尔斯可以算一个——”
年轻的伯爵点头。“这是当然的。也许您，德·弗雷努瓦，愿意同我一起出面。”

===Original===
The Parisian smoothed his moustache. “I shall be much honoured. In that case, however, as necessary preliminary, I shall have to ask to be made acquainted with all the circumstances.”
But now Léopold, bearing a dish on which were oysters green as stagnant scum, approached and with an air of infinite tenderness, much as though it were a baby, placed it before de Fresnoy.
Leisurely he began to eat.
Verplank, who had been looking out of the window, leaned forward. “The circumstances are evangelical in their simplicity. Last evening I was about to speak to Madame Barouffska when he put himself between us and eyed me in the manner which I have described.”
De Fresnoy, considering him over an oyster, said:
“You were at the Joyeuses then?”
===Chinese===
巴黎人捻了捻髭。“那将是我莫大的荣幸。不过那样的话，作为必要的预备手续，我就不得不请二位把全部情由讲给我听了。”
这时莱奥波德端着一盘牡蛎上来了，壳绿得像死水上的浮沫，他带着无限的温柔，仿佛捧的是个婴孩，把它安放在德·弗雷努瓦面前。
他不慌不忙地吃了起来。
弗普兰克本来一直望着窗外，这时俯身向前。“情由简单得像福音书一样。昨晚我正要同巴罗夫斯卡夫人说话，他插到我们中间，用我刚才描述的那种眼神打量我。”
德·弗雷努瓦就着一只牡蛎打量他，说：
“那么说，您当时在茹瓦耶兹家？”

===Original===
Verplank nodded.
“And there Barouffski objected to your speaking to his wife?”
“Yes.”
De Fresnoy swallowed the oyster. “In that case he was guilty not only of a grave offense to you, but to Madame de Joyeuse as well. The duke would be the first to resent it.”
With an idea of making it all very clear, Silverstairs put an oar in: “Madame Barouffska, you know, was formerly Madame Verplank.”
De Fresnoy bent a little. It may be that because of Silverstairs’ ultra English accent he had not understood. “Pardon?”
===Chinese===
弗普兰克点头。
“在那儿，巴罗夫斯基不容您同他妻子说话？”
“是。”
德·弗雷努瓦咽下牡蛎。“那样的话，他得罪的就不止是您，还有德·茹瓦耶兹夫人。头一个咽不下这口气的就该是公爵。”
西尔弗斯泰尔斯想把事情说个明白，插了一杠子：“要知道，巴罗夫斯卡夫人从前是弗普兰克夫人。”
德·弗雷努瓦微微一躬。也许是西尔弗斯泰尔斯那口过分英国腔的法语他没听懂。“请您再说一遍？”

===Original===
But here Verplank intervened. “This lady had been divorced from me before she married Barouffski.”
De Fresnoy, over another oyster, turned to him again. Yet any surprise he may have experienced he was too civil to display.
“Ah, indeed!” he replied. He looked as though he were about to add something, but refraining, he paused.
Verplank helped him out. “You are thinking perhaps that there may have been circumstances that rendered further acquaintance between us inadmissible. I may assure you that there are none and, without wishing to intrude my private affairs, I may assure you also that to this hour I am unaware why the divorce was obtained. This lady had no grievance of any kind against me and I had none whatever against her.”
Pontifically, in his deepest note, Silverstairs threw out: “In the States they give you a divorce for a Yes or a No.”
“For married people,” de Fresnoy remarked, yet so pleasantly that the sarcasm was lost, “America is the coming country.”
===Chinese===
弗普兰克这时插了进来。“这位夫人同我离了婚之后，才嫁的巴罗夫斯基。”
德·弗雷努瓦又就着另一只牡蛎转向他。即便心里有什么惊讶，他也太有教养，不肯流露。
“啊，是这样！”他答道。他看上去还想添一句什么，可又忍住了，顿在那里。
弗普兰克替他把话接了过去。“您也许在想，当年或许有什么情由，使我们不便再续来往。我尽可以向您保证，并没有那样的事；我也无意拿私事搅扰您，但我同样可以保证，直到今天我也不明白那场离婚究竟为的是什么。这位夫人对我没有任何一桩不满，我对她也绝无一丝怨怼。”
西尔弗斯泰尔斯一副主教做派，压着最低的嗓音抛出一句：“在美国，一句‘是’一句‘不’就能给你办出离婚来。”
“对于结了婚的人，”德·弗雷努瓦说——说得那样和悦，讥讽竟被磨掉了，“美国是未来之国。”

===Original===
As he spoke, the fat waiter, after supervising the removal of the first dish, produced, with the air of a conjurer, another. It was an omelette, golden without, frothy within.
De Fresnoy glanced up. “Countermand the pear. Instead, bring me paper and ink.”
“Perfectly, monsieur le baron.”
Slowly de Fresnoy attacked the food. After a mouthful he said to Silverstairs:
“When the writing materials come we can get off a note to Barouffski. If he has any explanation he can advance it. Otherwise﻿—on guard!”
After another mouthful he said to Verplank:
===Chinese===
说话间，那胖侍者督着撤下第一道菜，又变戏法似的端上另一道。是一份煎蛋卷，外皮金黄，里面松泡。
德·弗雷努瓦抬眼。“梨取消了。改给我拿纸和墨水来。”
“遵命，男爵先生。”
德·弗雷努瓦慢慢对食物开刀。吃了一口后，他对西尔弗斯泰尔斯说：
“等纸墨到了，我们就可以给巴罗夫斯基发封信。他若有什么解释，尽可以提出来。否则——预备，开战！”
又吃了一口，他对弗普兰克说：

===Original===
“You have fought before?”
“I have not had the occasion.”
“Nor I,” interjected Silverstairs. “It is against the law in England.”
Gravely, as though he were receiving valuable information de Fresnoy bowed. “So it is here. But with us it is custom that rules, not law. No jury would convict an honourable man for fighting a fair fight. Besides, dueling is in our blood. It will not disappear as chivalry has. It will last as long as there are French men﻿—and French women. And yet, in saying that chivalry has disappeared, I am in error. Not later than the week before last a cousin of mine, a young man truly charming, married a monster.”
He pushed aside his plate. “Well, then, Léopold, am I to sit here the entire day?”
Serviceably, a *buvard* in his hand, the waiter approached. “I have subventioned a new pen for the use of monsieur le baron.”
===Chinese===
“您从前决斗过？”
“我没有这个机会。”
“我也没有，”西尔弗斯泰尔斯插嘴，“在英国这是违法的。”
德·弗雷努瓦郑重地躬了躬身，仿佛领受了宝贵的信息。“这里也违法。但在我辈中间，主宰事情的是习俗，不是法律。体面人为一场公平的决斗打官司，没有哪个陪审团会定他的罪。何况决斗在我们的血里。它不会像骑士精神那样消亡。只要还有法国男人——和法国女人，它就会延续下去。不过，我说骑士精神已经消亡，这话错了。就在上上个星期，我的一位表亲，一位当真迷人的青年，娶了一个怪物。”
他推开盘子。“喂，莱奥波德，要我在这儿坐一整天吗？”
侍者殷勤地凑上前来，手里拿着 *buvard*（吸墨纸）。“我为男爵先生弄来了一支新笔。”

===Original===
“There, Léopold, your sins are remitted. See at once if the chasseur is free.”
De Fresnoy looked at Silverstairs. “With your permission, in our joint names, I write.”
He looked at Verplank. “Will you pardon me if I ask how your name is spelled?”
Verplank, getting at his case, extracted a card.
De Fresnoy glanced at it. Then, taking that new pen, he read, as he wrote, aloud.
===Chinese===
“好，莱奥波德，你的罪赦免了。快去看看那位 chasseur（门房侍者）得不得空。”
德·弗雷努瓦看向西尔弗斯泰尔斯。“承你们允许，我以我们双方的名义来写。”
他又看向弗普兰克。“恕我冒昧，您的名字怎么拼？”
弗普兰克摸出名片盒，抽出一张名片。
德·弗雷努瓦扫了一眼，随后提起那支新笔，一边写一边念出声来。

===Original===

> Monsieur: M. Verplank has requested the Earl of Silverstairs and myself to arrive at an understanding with two of your friends concerning an incident which occurred last evening in the Avenue Cours la Reine.
> Lord Silverstairs and I will be obliged if, as soon as possible, you will ask one of your friends to appoint a meeting at which we may deliberate.
> Receive, Monsieur, the expression of my distinguished sentiments.
> Baron de Fresnoy

===Chinese===

> 先生：M. 弗普兰克已委托西尔弗斯泰尔斯伯爵与鄙人，就昨日晚间在王后大道（Avenue Cours la Reine）发生的一桩事件，与您的两位友人达成谅解。
> 倘蒙您尽快约请一位友人为双方指定会面、共商其事，西尔弗斯泰尔斯伯爵与鄙人将不胜感激。
> 先生，请接受鄙人崇高敬意的表白。
> 德·弗雷努瓦男爵（Baron de Fresnoy）


===Original===
He looked over at Silverstairs. “Is that to your liking? Good! We will send it to the Little Club where the answer is to be left and we will have a reply today. *En attendant*, there are matters that claim me.”
With a movement of the chin he summoned the waiter.
A little byplay followed; the presentation of the bill, the click of gold on porcelain, the carelessly gathered change, the meagre tip, the reappearance of the hat, the bowing waiters, the craning necks, and the departure of de Fresnoy, an umbrella under his arm, a cigar between his teeth.
Verplank, emptying a glass of Chablis, looked out of the window. A panorama was forming. He saw the room at Coronado, Leilah as she told him of her love, his brief absence, his harrowing return, the hunt for her that had extended over half the globe, a hunt that divorce had not terminated, which her remarriage had not stopped and which, had he not at last discovered her, nothing could have stayed save his death or hers or the reason of the implacable Why. An obstacle to the Why or, it might be, the incarnation of it, was Barouffski, and Verplank saw himself standing somewhere with Barouffski before him. There was a command, the call of numbers, a detonation and the sight of Barouffski turning, swaying, falling down.
The panorama faded. A picture had appeared. Before the window, arrested by a congestion of traffic, a motor was stopping. In it and the mist was Leilah.
Verplank sprang to his feet. With the idea of going out to her there and forcing an explanation, he looked about for his hat.
===Chinese===
他望向西尔弗斯泰尔斯。“这样写合您意吗？好！我们把它送到小俱乐部（the Little Club），回信就留在那里，今天就能有答复。*En attendant*（在此期间），我还有几件事等着我。”
他下巴一扬，唤来侍者。
接着是一出小小的过场戏：账单呈上，金币在瓷碟上叮当一响，找头随手一拢，小费少得可怜，帽子再度现身，侍者们躬身送客，颈项纷纷伸长，然后德·弗雷努瓦告辞而去，臂下夹着伞，齿间咬着雪茄。
弗普兰克干了杯中的夏布利，望向窗外。一幅全景画正在成形。他看见科罗纳多的那个房间，蕾拉向他诉说着她的爱；他短暂的外出；他撕心裂肺的归来；随后是寻她的追踪，追遍了半个地球——离婚没能叫它收场，她再嫁也没能把它拦住，若不是他终于找到了她，能叫它停下的只有他的死、她的死，或那不肯罢休的「为什么」的缘由。而这「为什么」的障碍——或者说它的化身——便是巴罗夫斯基。弗普兰克看见自己站在某个地方，巴罗夫斯基立在他面前。一声口令，报数的呼号，一声爆响，巴罗夫斯基转身、摇晃、倒下。
全景画淡去。一幅画面出现了。窗前，被堵塞的车流截住，一辆汽车正停着。车里，在雾气中，是蕾拉。
弗普兰克霍地站起。想就地追出去、逼她把话说个明白，他四下找他的帽子。

===Original===
Silverstairs also got up. He had not seen. He too was looking for his hat. Placidly he remarked:
“I have an appointment with a chap named Tempest. Will you come with me?”
But now, the congestion relieved, the motor shot on. Verplank had the spectacle of a face fading instantly in the fog and the future.
“Will you?” Silverstairs repeated.
“Will I what?”
===Chinese===
西尔弗斯泰尔斯也站了起来。他没有看见。他也在找自己的帽子。他心平气和地说：
“我约了一个叫坦皮斯特（Tempest）的家伙。你跟我一起去吗？”
可就在这时，车流疏开，汽车一窜而去。弗普兰克眼睁睁看着一张脸，霎时消失在雾里，消失在未来里。
“去不去？”西尔弗斯泰尔斯又问。
“去干什么？”

===Original===
“I have to see a man about a horse. He lives just off the Bois de Boulogne, in the Rue de la Pompe. Will you come up there with me?”
“Yes, if you will go on foot. In that case I’ll leave you there and walk back. I need the exercise. I feel like what you described as a fidgety Frenchman.”
Silverstairs pulled at his moustache. “It’s no end of a walk. But no matter, I’ll go with you.”
===Chinese===
“我要去见一个人，为一匹马的事。他就住在布洛涅森林（Bois de Boulogne）边上，蓬佩街（Rue de la Pompe）。你陪我走一趟吗？”
“好，要是你肯走路去。那样我把你送到那儿就自己走回来。我正需要走走。我觉得自己就像你刚才说的那种坐立不安的法国人了。”
西尔弗斯泰尔斯捻着髭。“这一路可不近。不过没关系，我陪你走。”

## VI / 六

===Original===
That morning Leilah had two appointments, one with a modiste, the other with Violet Silverstairs. She did not feel equal to either. The episode of the previous evening had been to her like the supreme torture which medieval legislation devised. It was all she could bear﻿—and more!

When, abruptly, she found herself face to face with Verplank, it was as though she were confronted by the dead. The sense of it numbed her, and the numbness was heightened by a horror that has no name. Into the seats of thought there entered the realisation that, in spite of all, she still loved him, that in spite of all he still loved her. In the core of these convictions fear entered, fear of him, fear of herself, a sensation of common peril and mutual perdition so blinding that Barouffski’s rudeness she barely noticed, and it was with a look the damned may have that she saw Verplank turn with Violet Silverstairs, and go.

As they passed, Barouffski, with the air of one commenting on a triviality, remarked:

“How odd it is that the Joyeuses should care to hobnob with *demi-castors*. Shall we go?”

That *demi-castors* meant bounders generally, and, in this instance, specifically, she would, ordinarily, have been insufficiently familiar with the slang of the boulevards to know. But she did not hear. Moreover, the remark required no reply. Even otherwise she was unable to speak, and it was not until Barouffski reiterated his suggestion that mechanically she acceded to it with a movement of the head.
===Chinese===
那天上午蕾拉有两个约会：一个与女裁缝，一个与维奥莱特·西尔弗斯泰尔斯。两个她都无心赴。前一晚的那场遭遇，于她如同中世纪刑律所设计的极刑。那是她所能承受的极限——还不止！

当她猝然发现自己与弗普兰克面对面时，那感觉如同撞见了死者。这感觉使她麻木，而无以名状的恐怖又加深了这麻木。思绪的深处进来了这样的认识：不顾一切，她仍然爱他；不顾一切，他仍然爱她。在这两重确信的核心里，恐惧钻了进来——怕他，怕自己，一种同临险境、共赴沉沦之感，夺目蚀心到这般地步，以致巴罗夫斯基的无礼她几乎没有察觉；她目送弗普兰克随维奥莱特·西尔弗斯泰尔斯转身而去，眼神一如受诅咒者所有的那种。

两人走过时，巴罗夫斯基一副评说琐事的姿态，开口道：

「茹瓦耶兹家的人居然乐意同 *demi-castors*（法语：暴发户之流）厮混，多奇怪呀。咱们走吧？」

*demi-castors* 泛指下等暴发户，而在此处则特有所指——按理说，她素来不大熟悉林荫大道上的切口，本不会知道这个。可她根本没有听。再说，那话也不需要回答。即便不然，她也无法开口；直到巴罗夫斯基把建议重复了一遍，她才机械地以一点头应承下来。

===Original===
Her demeanor then in traversing the salons, her leave-taking of the duchess, her bearing in descending the stairs, were as mechanical as her reply to Barouffski, and it was not until after the motor had dropped him, as he had asked that it should, at the door of the Little Club, that, at last alone, the mental anchylosis fell by.

At once in a sort of retrograde vision, she relived the past. There had been the flight from Coronado, the halt at Salt Lake, the descent into Nevada, the divorce, the journey abroad, the platonic marriage to Barouffski. These﻿—the succeeding episodes in the drama of her life﻿—were so many hostages to joy, barricades thrown one after another between Verplank and herself, and unavailingly thrown, since, with but a look, they were almost destroyed.

They had seemed wholly impregnable, but she knew then that unless reinforced by surer bars, they would one and all collapse. At the foreknowledge of that she appreciated what the heroines in the old tragedies endured, when circled by the seven-times-twisted coil of fate. Yet, though they had yielded, she would not yield, and it was with this determination that she alighted in the Rue de la Pompe.

The house there had a church for neighbour, and stood between a court and a garden. Before the court was a high, white wall. The garden extended back to the parallel street, where, also, was a wall. The entrance to the court was a double doorway, the entrance to the garden was an iron gate. Between the gate and the house were large urns, a marble bench, a marble chair, most noticeably the kennels of two mastiffs, pets of Barouffski who, at whatever hour he returned at night, had them loosed. They were, he declared, a great protection, as indeed they were﻿—for him. Apart from the occasional barking of these dogs, barring also occasional music from the church, usually the garden was quiet. But that was in the order of things. It lacked both stable and garage. These had been secured elsewhere.
===Chinese===
随后她穿过一间间客厅的神态，向公爵夫人告辞的样子，下楼时的仪态，都同她答复巴罗夫斯基时一样机械；直到汽车按他先前的吩咐，把他丢在小俱乐部（the Little Club）门口，她终于独自一人，那精神的僵直症才蜕落下去。

顿时，在一种倒叙的幻象中，她把往昔重新过了一遍：逃离科罗纳多，滞留盐湖城，深入内华达，离婚，远走海外，与巴罗夫斯基那桩柏拉图式的婚姻。这些——她生命戏剧里接连上演的各幕——是一个个交给欢乐的人质，是她与弗普兰克之间接连筑起的一道道壁垒；而筑得徒劳，因为只消一个眼色，它们便几乎尽毁。

它们本来看上去固若金汤，可她那时明白了：若不用更牢靠的横闩加固，它们终将一个个垮塌。预见到这一点，她领会了旧式悲剧里的女主角们被命运那七重绞缠的罗网围住时，受的是什么熬煎。然而她们屈服了，她却不肯屈服；她正是揣着这份决心，在蓬佩街下的车。

蓬佩街的宅子以一座教堂为邻，夹在一方庭院与一座花园之间。庭院前面是一堵高高的白墙。花园向后延伸到平行的那条街，那里也是一堵墙。庭院的入口是一道双扇大门，花园的入口是一扇铁门。铁门与宅子之间摆着几只大瓮、一张大理石长凳、一把大理石椅，最惹眼的是两座獒犬舍——那是巴罗夫斯基的爱畜，无论夜里何时归来，他都要把它们放出来。他宣称，它们是了不起的护卫，它们也确实是——对他而言。除了这两条狗偶尔的吠声，再除去教堂里偶尔传来的音乐，花园通常是安静的。但这也是自然之理。花园既没有马厩也没有车库，这两样都另在别处安置了。

===Original===
Except for that detail, the arrangements generally were satisfactory. The house was commodious, agreeably furnished. On the ground floor were the usual offices, beneath which the servants slept. On the floor above were the salons and dining hall. Above these were the bedrooms. On this upper floor the apartment which Barouffski occupied gave on the street, while Leilah’s overlooked the garden.

Adjacent to her suite was a stairway designed for servants, but which, because of its convenience, she occasionally used. It led directly to the dining hall, and from there she could descend into the garden.

It had a superior advantage. It enabled her to avoid the hazards of the main stairway, which was used by Barouffski, whom nearer acquaintance had discovered to her without the mask﻿—without one mask, that is﻿—for histrion that he was, he had many, but the best, the feigned nobility of noble pride, the assumed parage, had gone.

In its place was a smile, constant, equivocal, ambiguous, a smile such as the consciously damned may display. It gave Leilah little creeps. She dreaded it, dreaded him, dreaded both, what is worse she dreaded instinctively, without knowing why. The man was amiable, serviceable, gallant. He wore his domino not faultlessly perhaps but with the fine air of a bravo who, when the time comes, will knife you, yes, but who in so doing will rather require that you admire the chasing on the handle of the blade. As yet the knife was concealed. But Leilah felt that it was there. He knew it was. Occasionally he fingered the point.
===Chinese===
除开这一处细节，总的安排还算称心。宅子宽敞，陈设雅致。底层是些惯常的杂用间，仆人便睡在其下。上面一层是客厅和饭厅。再往上是卧室。在这一层，巴罗夫斯基住的套间临街，蕾拉的则俯瞰花园。

与她的套间相邻有一道供仆人用的楼梯，但因它方便，她偶尔也走。它直通饭厅，从那里她可以下到花园里去。

这楼梯还有一大好处：让她避开正楼梯的风险。正楼梯是巴罗夫斯基用的，而进一步的相识已让她见到他不戴面具的样子——确切说，是不戴其中一副面具的样子；他本是演员一流，面具多得很，只是最好的那一副——假装高贵傲骨、假托名门 parage（法语：门第）的那副——已经卸下了。

取而代之的是一抹微笑，恒常、暧昧、含混，一种自知沉沦者才会露出的微笑。它让蕾拉起一阵阵细小的寒栗。她怕那微笑，怕那个人，两样都怕；更糟的是，她本能地怕，却不知为何。这人和气、勤快、殷勤。他的假面（domino）戴得或许并非无可挑剔，却自有刺客的漂亮风度——时候一到，他会给你一刀，是的一定会的，但动手之际，他更要求你欣赏刀柄上的雕花。刀还藏着。但蕾拉感觉得到它在。他知道它在。偶尔，他还用指尖试试刀尖。

===Original===
Hitherto he had lived by expedients. A golden six had been tossed him. He had pocketed it. For him the economic problem of life was solved. He asked little else, merely that the solution should endure and that his dignity, of which he had a humorous conception, be outwardly preserved. In addition to his dignity, or to his idea of it, he had another attribute. He was not exacting. It is a great charm in anyone. But with him it did not extend to money. Freely he demanded it, freely she gave and it was precisely when he demanded it that she felt, and he felt, too, the point of the knife.

On this evening when, after the usual din at the doors, the motor entered the court and she alighted at the perron, two footmen busied themselves in aiding her.

Leilah passed through the dining room to the garden where for a while she walked along the path that led from the house to the gate.

The garden was cloistered, the night serene. The influences of both affected her. The darkness put her thoughts into relief, the solitude relaxed the tension of her nerves.
===Chinese===
迄今为止，他一直靠权宜之计过活。一枚金六点掷到了他手里。他把它装进了口袋。对他来说，人生的经济问题已告解决。他别无所求，只求这解决能够长久，只求他的尊严——他对尊严自有一副打趣的见解——在表面上得以保全。除了尊严，或者说他对尊严的观念之外，他还有一样品性：他不苛求。这在任何人身上都是了不起的魅力。但在他身上，这魅力不适用于钱。他要得爽快，她给得爽快；而恰恰在他伸手要钱的时刻，她感到了——他也感到了——那刀尖。

这天晚上，大门照例一阵喧响之后，汽车驶进庭院，她在阶前下车，两名男仆赶忙上前搀扶。

蕾拉穿过饭厅走进花园，在从宅子通往铁门的小径上徘徊了一阵。

花园幽闭如修道院的回廊，夜色澄宁。两者的气息都感染了她。黑暗让她的思绪浮凸出来，清寂松开了她神经的紧张。

===Original===
Another thing was helpful, the determination which she had reached, though for that determination to be maintained there must, she saw, be further hostages, new barricades. But what further hostages could she give she wondered, what firmer barricades was it possible to erect? Barring flight or an appeal to Verplank, some message begging him to leave Paris, she could not imagine any. Flight she had already tried, but not flight to some one of the world’s faraway places where anyone may be lost forever. It was a miserably dismal thing to do, she reflected, a thing so dismal and so miserable that she doubted her ability to do it.

As she thought it over she wondered if in some former existence she could have injured Verplank and whether it were by way of retribution that he had the power to tempt and torture her now. Tenets of this character the Vidya advanced and as she had told Tempest, she had come to believe in that Scripture as many do in the Bible, though as many also do without being able to accept it entirely, without being able to accept for instance stories such as that of Jonah and the whale which none the less all would accept were it known how profound is the symbolism behind them. With like reservations, Leilah accepted the Vidya. She was very ignorant as women in her station generally are and the reservations were due to that ignorance and also to the demand which the doctrine made on her imagination. But though she was ignorant she was conscious of it and consciousness of ignorance is usually the condition precedent to enlightenment.

Now, in considering the episode of the evening, she asked herself whether she was warranted in accepting this creed of past lives. At the Joyeuses, during the announcements of resonant names, Tempest had said that unless we swallow the ridiculous dogma of a soul specially created at every birth and unless too we are indecent enough to fancy the Deity waiting for that purpose on the passions and caprices of man, we have to accept it, have to accept with it the corollary of past actions and their consequences, have to accept, too, the deduction that, in accordance with our past actions, it is we who reward or punish ourselves, we who become avenging furies or angels of light.

Leilah wished that she could have discussed the matter more fully with Tempest yet she felt that what he had said was logical, but if it were true, then the parallel doctrine that all misdeeds and with them all misfortunes spring from desire must be true also, in which case, before their consequences can be effaced, all misdeeds must be atoned. But how can they be atoned? she asked herself. Presently she remembered. According to the Vidya, any desire no matter what, desire for pleasure, for gain, for attainments, for honours, even the desire for spiritual perfection, even the desire for the lack of desire, must be extinguished before old scores are paid. That was the way she saw, the only way. The debtor must sacrifice himself to himself.
===Chinese===
另一样东西也有助益，那就是她已下定的决心，尽管她明白，要维持这决心，还得交出新人质、筑起新壁垒。可是还能交出什么人质呢，她纳闷，还能筑起什么更牢的壁垒呢？——除了出逃，或者向弗普兰克求告、捎个信求他离开巴黎，她想象不出别的。出逃她已试过，但那不是逃到世界某个谁都能一去无踪的远方。她寻思，那是桩凄惨得可怜的事，凄惨可怜到她怀疑自己做得来做不来。

再三思量之际，她不禁想：莫非自己在某一世伤害过弗普兰克，如今他这般有本领诱惑她、折磨她，莫非正是报偿？这一类的教理，the Vidya 是主张的；正如她曾对坦皮斯特说过的，她已信了那部经典，像许多人信《圣经》那样信——但也像许多人一样，不能尽数接受，比如就无法接受约拿与鲸鱼那类故事；可倘若人们知道那些故事背后的象征何等深邃，人人也都会接受的。怀着同样的保留，蕾拉接受了 the Vidya。她非常无知，如同她那阶层的女子通常那样；这些保留既源于无知，也源于那教义对想象力提出的要求。不过她虽无知，却自知其无知，而自知无知，通常是开悟的先决条件。

此刻，回味着前一晚的那一幕，她问自己：接受这套前世今生的信条，是否有凭有据？在茹瓦耶兹家的晚宴上，在一连串响亮名号的通报声中，坦皮斯特说过：除非我们咽下那套可笑的教义——说灵魂在每一次出生时被特地创造；也除非我们下流到竟以为神会为此守候在人类的情欲与任性旁——否则我们就只能接受它，只能连带接受前世作为及其后果这条推论，还得接受那个结论：依照我们前世的作为，奖赏我们、惩罚我们的正是我们自己，我们自己也正是那复仇的怒妇，或光明的天使。

蕾拉但愿能同坦皮斯特把这问题谈个透彻，不过她也感到他说的合乎逻辑。而倘若这是真的，那么与之平行的那条教义——一切罪愆以及随之一切不幸皆源于欲望——也必然是真的；如此说来，在罪愆的后果被抹去之前，一切罪愆都必须赎清。可怎么赎清呢？她问自己。随即她想起来了。照 the Vidya 所说，任何欲望——不论什么欲望，求欢愉、求财利、求造诣、求荣名的欲望，乃至求灵性圆满的欲望，乃至求「无欲」的欲望——都必须在旧账偿清之前熄灭。她看到的路便是这一条，唯一的一条：债务人必须把自己献祭给自己。

===Original===
But, uncertain still, she went over the matter again, putting to it little tests, passably naive yet serviceable to her. She had ardently desired to marry Verplank, then, desiring as ardently a barricade against him, she had married Barouffski. In the one case the result had been catastrophic; in the other, calamitous. Doubtless she had sinned in the past and these disasters, brought about by her own desires, were her punishment. There were other things that she had desired. She had wanted to be loved, she had wanted to be thought a beauty, and not only her love had shamed her but soon she might be ashamed to show her face. At the thought of these things she realised anew and more profoundly than ever that selfish desire is the root of evil and that only in its extirpation may peace be had. But coincidently she realised also that any such extirpation was beyond her. Heredity, environment, the circumstances of her life, had given an impetus to desire which she could not arrest. She liked wealth, ease, pretty clothes, becoming hats, the society of agreeable people. She liked the world and in liking it she feared that she liked also the flesh, it might be even that she liked, too, the devil. Yet, she must not, she knew.

In telling herself that, she thought of the Church. The Church was so much more comfortable. There you were not asked impossibilities, the one requirement was to throw yourself in her arms and repent. As the facile process occurred to her she recalled George Moore’s story of Evelyn Innes. That masterwork seemed to tell her to do as the heroine had done and go in a convent.

Perhaps she might, she thought. Perhaps she must.

Several times already she had crossed and recrossed the garden. Now she found herself at the farther end facing the iron gate. Leilah opened it, walked to the corner and returned.
===Chinese===
但她仍不笃定，又把这事掂量了一遍，给它出了几道小小的测验题，天真得可以，对她却颇管用。她曾热切地想嫁给弗普兰克；随后又热切地想要一道抵御他的壁垒，于是嫁了巴罗夫斯基。前一桩的结局是灾难，后一桩是祸殃。无疑她过去造了罪，这些由她自己的欲望招来的灾祸，便是她的惩罚。她还有过别的欲求。她曾想被人爱，曾想被人视作美人；如今不但她的爱已使她蒙羞，恐怕不久她连露面都要羞于了。想到这些，她再度认识到——比以往任何时候都更深地认识到——自私的欲望是罪恶之根，唯有将它连根拔除，方得安宁。可与此同时她也认识到，这样的拔除非她力所能及。遗传、环境、她一生的境遇，给了欲望一股她刹不住的冲力。她爱财富，爱安逸，爱漂亮衣裳、衬人的帽子，爱与令人愉快的人交往。她爱这个世界，而在爱着它的时候，她恐怕连肉体也爱了，说不定连魔鬼也爱了。然而她知道，万万不可。

这样告诉自己时，她想到了教会。教会可舒服得多。在那里没人要求你做不可能的事，唯一的要求是投入她的怀抱，忏悔。这省事的法子浮上心头时，她记起了乔治·穆尔（George Moore）写伊芙琳·英尼斯（Evelyn Innes）的那部小说。那部杰作似乎在告诉她：照女主角的样子做，进修道院去。

也许可以试试，她想。也许非如此不可。

她已在花园里来回走了好几趟。此刻她发现自己到了尽头那端，正对着铁门。蕾拉推开它，走到街角，又折了回来。

===Original===
The little tentative evasion had been successful. At any time, unseen even by a servant, she could leave the house, disappear utterly, be forever engulfed. But the knowledge that she could escape into darkness and be lost there, offered little more than a choice between tears. It presented a form of suicide which was superior only to actual death. She hoped she might be spared it. She hoped an appeal to Verplank would suffice, though in what manner it could best reach him, or, for that matter, reach him at all, she found it difficult to decide. To make it personally was impossible. To attempt it through Violet Silverstairs would involve an explanation and that was impossible also. The idea of employing one of her women occurred to her. There were manifest objections to such a course, though the particular woman whom she had in view she trusted entirely.

Slowly she returned to the house and went to her room. There, when at last the servants had gone and she was alone, she knelt on a prie-dieu and, to the Watchers of the Seven Spheres, prayed for the earthly peace of her soul and of his. She knew that no prayer could affect them, she knew that they are not to be propitiated or coerced, but it soothed her as prayer, in raising the vibrations, does soothe the distressed. The prayer concluded she began another. She prayed that sometime she might be somewhere, on some plane, where all things broken are made complete and found again things vanished.

Then, the solace of it still upon her, suddenly she saw by what the prayer had been induced. The consciousness confused and presently, in the melancholy sotto-voce of thought, she told herself that to extinguish that desire, she would have to be in Dharmakaya﻿—the mystic state where there is oblivion of all things here.

“Here!” she caught herself repeating. For, at once, a passage from the Upanishads prompting, she remembered that here means Myalba, which is hell, the greatest of all hells and, for those of this evolution, the only hell there is.
===Chinese===
这一场小小的试探性出逃是成功的。任何时候，哪怕不被一个仆人看见，她都能离开这宅子，彻底消失，永远被吞没。可是「可以遁入黑暗、埋没于黑暗」这个认知，给人的无非是在泪水与泪水之间做选择。那是一种自杀的形式，只比真正的死亡可取些。她希望自己能免于此途。她希望向弗普兰克求告一声便已足够，尽管这求告怎样才能最妥帖地送达他——或者说到底能不能送达他——她觉得难以决断。亲自去说是万万不能的。托维奥莱特·西尔弗斯泰尔斯转达又须解释缘由，那也是万万不能的。雇她的一个女仆去传话的念头也闪过脑海。这条路子显然多有不便，尽管她心里想定的那一个女仆，她是全然信得过的。

她缓缓回到宅里，回到自己房中。在那里，待仆人们终于退尽、只剩她一人时，她跪在一张祈祷凳上，向七域的守望者们（the Watchers of the Seven Spheres）祈求她与他灵魂的尘世安宁。她知道任何祷告都打动不了他们，她知道他们不受安抚也不受胁迫；但这祷告抚慰了她，一如祷告在提升振动之时，确能抚慰愁苦之人。这一篇祷毕，她又开始了另一篇。她祈求，但愿有朝一日，她能在某处、在某个层面上——在那里，一切破碎的得以完满，一切消逝的得以重逢。

随后，那份慰藉犹在身上，她忽然看清了这祷告由何而起。这意识搅乱了心绪；不多时，她便在思绪那忧郁的低语中告诉自己：要熄灭那个欲望，她就得进入法身（Dharmakaya）——那个此世万物俱遭遗忘的神秘境地。

「此世！」她发觉自己反复念着这个词。因为《奥义书》的一段文字随即涌上心头，让她想起：此世者，Myalba（缪亚尔巴）也——那是地狱，一切地狱中最宏大的地狱，而且对这一演化期里的众生而言，是唯一实有的地狱。

## VII / 七

===Original===
It was on the morning succeeding these incidents that Leilah felt unequal for the appointments she had made. But however she felt, she always did what she had planned. In this instance nature punished her. On the way to the first appointment, a malaise overtook her, enveloped her, beat at her and although, gradually, it fell by, she was still conscious of it when, in the Rue Cambon, the motor stopped at the modiste’s door.
“The fitting of madame la comtesse Barouffska!” a fair young girl in black immediately and authoritatively announced.
Before landscapes of silk, in the delight of new modes, customers were sunning themselves. At the announcement they turned, while Leilah, conducted by another girl who had advanced to meet her, crossed the laboratory of enchantments and entered an adjoining room.
But, for the moment, the fitting was delayed. The première was elsewhere occupied. When presently she appeared she excitedly exclaimed:
“I hope I have not detained madame. I am desolated if I have. But! But! If madame knew! One is literally torn to pieces! All day long it is nothing but Ernestine that dress! Ernestine, that robe! Ernestine, that costume! Ernestine this! Ernestine that! Truly madame, there are moments when I say I die! I go crazy!”
===Chinese===
这些事发生的第二天早晨，蕾拉觉得浑身没力气，应付不了自己约下的那些约会。可无论感觉如何，她向来是照计划行事的。这一回，老天惩罚了她。在赴第一个约会的路上，一阵不适袭来，裹住她，捶打她；虽说它渐渐退了，等汽车在康朋街（Rue Cambon）一家 modiste（女帽时装店）门前停下时，她仍觉着它还在。
“巴罗夫斯卡伯爵夫人（madame la comtesse Barouffska）试衣！”一个身穿黑衣、金发白皙的年轻姑娘当即以权威的口吻通报。
主顾们正在一匹匹丝绸的风景前，沉浸在新款式的喜悦里晒着暖。通报声起，她们纷纷转头，蕾拉则由迎上来的另一位姑娘引着，穿过这间施了魔法的工坊，走进旁边的一间屋。
不过试衣暂时耽搁了。首席裁缝（la première）在别处忙着。不多时她露面了，激动地嚷道：
“但愿没有让夫人久等。若是让夫人等了，我真是过意不去。可是！可是！夫人哪里知道！人家简直要被撕成碎片了！成天到晚，不是『欧内斯蒂娜（Ernestine），这件连衣裙！』就是『欧内斯蒂娜，那件长袍！欧内斯蒂娜，那套行头！欧内斯蒂娜这！欧内斯蒂娜那！』说真的夫人，有好几个瞬间我都觉得自己要死了！要疯了！”

===Original===
Abruptly dropping her voice, she added: “But pardon, I monologue.”
At once, indicating a gown which an assistant had brought, she exclaimed again:
“It will ravishingly become madame.”
The gown, a work of the best Parisian art, suggested something of the immateriality of a moonbeam, and as the assistant, a girl with a tired face and circled eyes, held it for inspection, it gleamed.
Leilah looked at it, wondering the while where she would wear it, whether indeed she would wear it at all. Then, before a sheet that had been placed on the floor and on which the assistant arranged the gown in a circle she proceeded to undress.
To the amateur in feminine beauty, there are few spectacles more attractive than that of an attractive woman clothed in lingerie and a hat. This spectacle Leilah presented.
===Chinese===
她陡然压低嗓音，补了一句：“不过恕罪，我一个人唱起独角戏来了。”
随即，她指着一名助手捧来的连身裙，又嚷道：
“夫人穿上它，一定美得迷人。”
那件连身裙是巴黎顶级手艺的作品，隐隐透着一缕月光般的非物质之美；那助手——一个面色疲倦、眼圈发黑的姑娘——把它举起来供人细看时，裙上泛起微光。
蕾拉看着它，一边思忖自己会到哪里去穿它，究竟还会不会穿它。随后，在铺于地板的一幅布单前——助手已把裙子在布单上摊成一圈——她动手宽衣。
在女性之美的鉴赏家看来，少有比一个美丽女人只着衬裙、头戴帽子更好看的景致了。蕾拉此刻呈现的正是这幅景致。

===Original===
The première exclaimed at it. “Madame la comtesse has a figure truly divine. But! But! Who could have laced her?”
“I was not very well this morning,” Leilah replied. “I told my women not to make me too tight. But you can take me in I think about an inch.”
“Marguerite,” said the première, “draw the stays a little closer.”
The girl with the tired face undid the corset and pulled at the strings. But she pulled awkwardly, perhaps too suddenly.
Leilah gasped, turned, sat down and fell forward. The première hurried to her. She had fainted.
“The smelling salts!” the première cried. “The smelling salts! Cognac! Get some cognac!”
===Chinese===
首席裁缝见了赞叹出声。“伯爵夫人的身段真是天仙一般。可是！可是！是谁给您束的腰？”
“我今天早晨不大舒服，”蕾拉答道，“吩咐了我的女仆们别束得太紧。不过我想，您还可以再收我一英寸左右。”
“玛格丽特（Marguerite），”首席裁缝说，“把系带再收紧一点。”
那个倦容的姑娘解开胸衣，去拉系带。可她拉得笨拙，也许太猛了。
蕾拉倒抽一口气，转过身，坐下，向前栽倒。首席裁缝急忙扑过去。她晕过去了。
“嗅盐！”首席裁缝喊道，“嗅盐！白兰地！去拿白兰地！”

===Original===
With one hand she was supporting Leilah, with the other she gesticulated at Marguerite who, hurriedly from the mantel, fetched a vinaigrette which Ernestine then took and sniffed at.
“She’s coming to,” said the assistant.
Ernestine waved the vinaigrette. “The gods be praised!”
For Leilah now had opened her eyes. Wearily she looked about, straightened herself and sighed.
“I must have fainted.”
“It is nothing madame,” Ernestine anxiously protested. “Truly nothing and yet so modish. Yesterday the Princesse de Solférino fainted. The day before it was the turn of the young Duchesse de Malakoff. Such a good augury for these ladies! Like them madame is perhaps﻿—”
===Chinese===
她一手扶着蕾拉，一手朝玛格丽特比划；玛格丽特慌忙从壁炉架上取来一只香盐瓶，欧内斯蒂娜接过去给她嗅。
“她缓过来了。”助手说。
欧内斯蒂娜晃着香盐瓶。“谢天谢地！”
蕾拉这时睁开了眼。她倦倦地环顾四周，坐直身子，叹了口气。
“我大概是晕过去了。”
“没什么，夫人，”欧内斯蒂娜急切地分辩，“真的没什么，而且多时髦呀。昨天索尔费里诺王妃（Princesse de Solférino）晕倒了。前天轮到年轻的马拉科夫公爵夫人（Duchesse de Malakoff）。对这几位夫人是多好的兆头！夫人或许也和她们一样——”

===Original===
But Leilah now was making an effort to rise.
Abandoning the vinaigrette Ernestine aided her.
“Madame will perhaps wish the fitting postponed. Yes, is it not? It might further fatigue madame. Tomorrow﻿—no, tomorrow I regret but in the afternoon I have three appointments and in the morning there is the trousseau of Miss Smith of New York who is to marry an English lord. Marguerite!” she interrupted herself to exclaim. “The costume of madame!”
Then, as the assistant also assisted Leilah, reflectively the première resumed:
“I hear that every New York young lady loves a lord. But﻿—”
She hesitated. Visibly the vision evoked, confused. Yet, after a second’s pause, rallying, she continued.
===Chinese===
可蕾拉这时正挣着要起身。
欧内斯蒂娜放下香盐瓶，上前搀她。
“夫人也许想把试衣改期。是吧，是不是？免得更累着夫人。明天——不，明天很抱歉，下午我有三个预约，上午还要赶纽约的史密斯小姐（Miss Smith）的嫁妆，她要嫁一位英国勋爵。玛格丽特！”她打断自己，喊道，“夫人的那套行头！”
接着，助手也来搀蕾拉，首席裁缝若有所思地续道：
“我听说纽约的年轻小姐个个都爱勋爵。可是——”
她迟疑了。那唤起的幻象显然令她犯糊涂。不过，顿了一秒之后，她回过神来，接着说：

===Original===
“Perhaps it is not every New York young lady who has a lord to love. Perhaps many of them love the same lord.”
Discreetly she smiled. “And that must be so nice for him!”
Considering Leilah, she concluded:
“But another day﻿—”
Of it all, Leilah heard but that. “Yes,” she answered, “another day.”
Then, presently, after more attentions, the première accompanied her to the door.
===Chinese===
“也许并不是每个纽约小姐都有一位勋爵可爱。也许是许多位爱着同一位勋爵。”
她得体地微笑。“那他可真受用！”
她端详着蕾拉，作结道：
“不过改天吧——”
这一整篇话里，蕾拉只听进了这一句。“好，”她答道，“改天。”
随后，又殷勤照应了一阵，首席裁缝把她送到门口。

===Original===
“Rue François Premier,” Leilah told the groom.
The machine shot ahead. Arrested shortly by a congestion of traffic, it halted before a window behind which Verplank and Silverstairs sat.
Leilah, unconscious of their presence, gazed at the murky cinematograph of the street, filled at this hour with faces sordid, petulant, indifferent, or frankly gay; with the passing forms of workmen, idlers, shopgirls, vagabonds; the swarming Parisian crowd which did not, she believed, contain one soul as miserable as her own.
The congestion relieved, the motor shot on. Leilah leaned back. It was not so long ago that she was on her way from New York to Coronado. She was happy then, happy with a happiness so perfect that it lifted her into the ultimate ecstasies which love and life comport. It was not so long ago, only six short months, only that brief eternity of sorrow which, unended yet, had been the damning penalty of that joy.
“In this life ye shall have tribulation,” the Christ had said, and truly said, and as she rememorated the significant menace, she wondered whether for such as she, tribulation ended here. But her creed assured her. From the Vidya she had acquired faith in fate, the belief rather that we make our own destiny, that it is by our own hands that our lives are cast in places pleasant or the reverse, that our conduct in one life creates the conditions of our existence in another, that anything experienced now is the effect of a cause set going in the past, that happiness is the recompense of beneficence, deformity the result of cruelty, melancholy the penalty of evil thoughts. But whether retribution pursued its victim into future planes or abandoned them when they died, depended, she also believed, on how they faced it here, and it was in this idea that, during the unended sorrow, she had found the strength to bear its coils.
The motor stopped. She told the groom to wait. Presently she was among the subdued tints and harmonised furnishings of the drawing room of her friend.
===Chinese===
“弗朗索瓦一世街（Rue François Premier）。”蕾拉告诉司机。
汽车蹿了出去。不多时被堵塞的车流截住，停在一扇窗前——窗后坐着的正是弗普兰克和西尔弗斯泰尔斯。
蕾拉没有察觉他们在场，只望着街上那浑浊的映画：此刻街上满是或鄙俗、或乖戾、或淡漠、或坦荡荡寻欢的脸；走过一个个工人、闲人、女店员、流浪汉的身影；熙熙攘攘的巴黎人群——她相信，这里面没有一个灵魂像她自己的这样苦。
车流疏开，汽车又蹿了出去。蕾拉靠上椅背。就在不久之前，她还在从纽约去科罗纳多的路上。那时她是幸福的，幸福得那样圆满，把她举进了爱与人生所能容纳的至高的狂喜。不过是不久之前——只隔了短短六个月，只隔了那一场尚无尽头、短暂的永恒的悲伤——那欢乐注定的刑罚。
「在世上你们有苦难」，基督曾这样说，说得真切；她默想着这句意味深长的警诫，不知道对她这样的人来说，苦难是否到此为止。可她的信条给了她保证。从 the Vidya（梵语「明／智」）中她获得了对命运的信仰——毋宁说是这样的信念：命运是我们自己造的，我们的生活被我们自己的手安放进乐土或其反面；我们此生的行止造出我们彼生的境遇；如今经历的一切，都是过去种下的因所结的果；幸福是善行的报偿，畸形是残忍的结果，忧郁是恶念的刑罚。只是，报应究竟是追着它的牺牲品进入来世的诸界，还是在他们死时便弃之而去——她同样相信——这取决于他们在此间如何面对它；正是靠着这个念头，在那场无尽的悲伤里，她才找见了承受其缠绕的力气。
汽车停了。她让司机等着。不多时，她已置身于她朋友那客厅的柔淡色调与调和的陈设之间。

===Original===
At once, clearly in her limpid voice, considering her with brilliant eyes, Violet Silverstairs aimed and fired.
“You’re a liar!”
At the shot Leilah attempted to smile, and though she failed, it was not because she fancied there could be any reproach in the term, but because latterly she had been unable to smile at all.
“You’re a liar,” Violet repeated. “Also, you are late.”
“I know I am late and I am sorry,” Leilah withdrawing her gloves, replied. “But how am I a liar?”
“Come to luncheon and you will precious soon find out. I had some eggs for you, eggs *à l’Aurore Boréale*. I had a sweetbread. I had﻿—I have forgotten what else. Now I have nothing. Everything is spoiled.”
===Chinese===
维奥莱特·西尔弗斯泰尔斯当即以清亮的嗓音、闪亮的眼睛盯住她，瞄准，开火。
“你撒谎！”
枪声响处，蕾拉试图微笑；虽说没笑成，倒不是她觉得这个词里有什么可责备之处，而是因为近来她压根就笑不出来了。
“你撒谎，”维奥莱特重复，“而且，你迟到了。”
“我知道我迟到了，很抱歉，”蕾拉一面脱手套一面答道，“可我怎么就撒谎了？”
“留下吃午饭，你很快就会明白的。我给你备了鸡蛋，*à l’Aurore Boréale*（「北极光」风味）的鸡蛋。还备了牛胸腺。还有——别的我忘了。现在我什么都没有了。全都糟蹋了。”

===Original===
Violet Silverstairs was perhaps imaginative. There were eggs, very good eggs too, though whether prepared in the Aurora Borealis fashion is perhaps beside the issue. Moreover there was a sweetbread, one that had been germinated on salt meadows and which was not spoiled in the least. In addition there were the other things which she had forgotten and all of them appetising in the extreme. It was an excellent luncheon, perfectly served in a beautiful room. But it was a luncheon for Sybarites, not for the suffering. After the first morsel Leilah was unable to eat.
“Where is Silverstairs?” she asked when that morsel had been consumed.
“With your ex.”
Leilah put down her fork. “With Gulian?”
Violet laughed. “Have you more than one? But it was just through him that your lie cropped out. Last night he swore by bell, book and candle that you had never told him why you cut and ran.”
It was at this juncture that Leilah found herself unable to eat. Instantly her mind shot back. She was at Coronado again, in the sunshine and frippery of her sitting room. She could see Verplank as he left it, see the letters that had been brought, see herself as she opened one of them, that one which with its enclosures she had redirected and left for him. The possibility never before conjectured, that he had not received it girdled her with a zone of ice. For a moment she looked fixedly at one of the windows through which the pale daylight fell. In the beautiful room, companioned by her nearest friend, she felt that sense of utter loneliness which in the great crises of life is experienced by all. Yet was it true?
===Chinese===
维奥莱特·西尔弗斯泰尔斯也许想象力太丰富。鸡蛋是有的，还是顶好的鸡蛋，至于是否照北极光的法子做的，恐怕无关宏旨。牛胸腺也有，那是盐泽牧场上长成的，一点没糟蹋。此外还有那些她忘了的东西，样样都开胃到了极点。那是一顿极好的午餐，在一间美丽的房间里伺候得妥妥帖帖。可那是给锡巴里斯人（Sybarites，奢靡成性的享乐之徒）备的午餐，不是给受苦人的。头一口下去，蕾拉就吃不动了。
“西尔弗斯泰尔斯呢？”那一口咽下去后，她问。
“跟你前夫在一起。”
蕾拉放下叉子。“跟格里安？”
维奥莱特笑了。“难道你不止一个前夫？可你的谎恰恰就是从他嘴里露的馅。昨晚他指着钟声、经书和蜡烛对我起誓，说你从来没告诉过他，你为什么拔腿就走。”
正是在这个当口，蕾拉发觉自己一口也咽不下去了。她的思绪霎时倒卷回去。她又回到了科罗纳多，坐在那间洒满阳光、摆满小玩意的起居室里。她看见弗普兰克走出去的样子，看见送来的那些信，看见自己拆开其中一封——就是那一封，她连同里面的附件重新封好、留给了他的那一封。他从没收到它——这个她从未设想过的可能，像一条冰带把她箍住了。有一瞬，她死死盯着透进苍白天光的一扇窗。在这间美丽的屋子里，最亲密的朋友相伴在侧，她却感到了那种彻底的孤独——人生的大危机里人人都会尝到的那种。可这是真的吗？

===Original===
“Violet!” she cried. “You are jesting.”
But the lady, determined then or never to learn the truth, cocked an eye at her. “I am not, nor was he.”
At that, Leilah felt the girdle of ice sending its shivers through her. The plan she had made must, she saw, be foregone. If Verplank did not know why she had separated from him, never would he leave Paris until he did. But what must he have thought, she agonisedly reflected, and what must he think!
Violet, who had been watching her, said:
“Why don’t you tell me?”
Leilah taking up her fork again, tried for countenance sake, to affect to eat. The effort was beyond her. She put it down.
===Chinese===
“维奥莱特！”她喊道，“你在说笑。”
可这位夫人打定主意此时不问就再没机会了，斜着眼瞅她。“我没说笑，他也没有。”
这话一出，蕾拉只觉那条冰带的寒颤传遍全身。她看出，自己定下的计划必须放弃了。倘若弗普兰克不知道她为什么离开他，那他不问明白是决不会离开巴黎的。而他一直以来是怎么想的，往后又会怎么想——她痛苦地思量着。
一直在盯着她看的维奥莱特说：
“你为什么不告诉我？”
蕾拉重新拿起叉子，为了体面，装着吃。这份力气她拿不出。她又放下了。

===Original===
“I can’t,” she at last replied.
Violet, her brilliant eye still cocked, almost winked.
“Yes, you said that before. But you see, don’t you know, that whether you can or cannot tell me, you will have to tell him and, in the circumstances, would it not be best to have me do it for you? To be sure, if you had taken my advice and omitted to marry Barouffski, I would say, have it out with him yourself. But your marriage does not seem to have simplified matters, which, so far as I can make out, are now pretty thoroughly mixed.”
The lady spoke better than she knew. Matters were complicated though how profoundly she had no idea, nor was Leilah aware that the situation, already tortuous, was to become even more intricately labyrinthine.
“Of course,” Violet, in her bell-like voice, threw out, “after running away, getting a divorce and marrying another man, I can fancy that you don’t much want to see him. But, really, you owe it to yourself to give the reason, particularly as it is he who is to blame.”
At this, Leilah, who had been looking down into her prison, looked up. “I never said so.”
===Chinese===
“我说不出口。”她终于答道。
维奥莱特那只亮眼睛仍斜着，几乎要眨一下。
“是啊，这话你先前就说过了。可你明白的，对不对，不管你说不说得出口，你总得说给他听；处在这个境地，由我替你去说，岂不是最好？当然啦，你要是当初听了我的劝、没嫁给巴罗夫斯基，我就会说，你自己去跟他摊牌。可你这场婚事似乎并没把事情弄简单——依我看，如今是搅得相当彻底了。”
这位夫人是无心之言却道着了实处。事情确实复杂，只是她不知道有多深；蕾拉也不知道，本已曲折的局面，还会变得更加盘根错节，如迷宫一般。
“当然啦，”维奥莱特用她铃铛似的嗓音抛出一句，“又是出走，又是离婚，又嫁了别人，我料想你不太想见他。可是说真的，你欠自己一个交代，总得把缘由说出口——何况错的是他。”
听了这话，一直低头望着自己这座牢狱的蕾拉抬起了眼。“我从没这么说过。”

===Original===
“No, but was it necessary? Even nowadays, even in the States, a woman does not cut and run because butter won’t melt in her husband’s mouth. She does so because she has, or thinks she has, a grievance and the man, if he is a man, ought to be given an opportunity to apologise, however imaginary the grievance may be.”
Leilah shook her head. “There can be no apology here.”
Violet laughed. “That is just what I would say if I had gone and done it. Then it would be for Silverstairs to try on his knees to get me to listen to one﻿—provided, of course, that in the interim I had not taken over another man, for in that case I verily believe he would wring my neck. But you need fear nothing of the sort from Verplank. He seemed anxious only to wring Barouffski’s.”
Leilah made another futile effort with her fork. Absently she answered:
“I don’t believe he knew who he was.”
“You don’t! After his telling him! But, apropos, what became of d’Arcy? I thought you and he were safely tucked away in a corner, otherwise never in the world would I have marched your Number One up to your Number Two.”
===Chinese===
“是没说过，可这还用说吗？即便在今天，即便在美国，也没有哪个女人会因为丈夫装得一脸无辜就卷铺盖出走。她出走，是因为她受了委屈，或者自认为受了委屈；而那男人，只要他还算个男人，就该给他一个道歉的机会——不管那委屈有多么子虚乌有。”
蕾拉摇头。“这里头没有什么道歉可言。”
维奥莱特笑了。“我要是也这么干了，说的就会是一模一样的话。接下来就该轮到西尔弗斯泰尔斯跪下来求我听他道一次歉——当然啦，前提是这中间我没把另一个男人接上手；真要是那样，我笃信他会拧断我的脖子。不过这种事你不必怕弗普兰克。他看上去一心只想拧巴罗夫斯基的脖子。”
蕾拉又徒劳地动了动叉子。她心不在焉地答道：
“我不信他知道那人是谁。”
“你不信！人家都当面告诉他了！对了，说句闲话，达尔西（d’Arcy）后来怎么样了？我原以为你和他稳稳当当缩在哪个角落里呢，不然我打死也不会把你的「一号」径直领到你的「二号」跟前去。”

===Original===
“D’Arcy!” Leilah repeated. She had barely heard. She scarcely knew what she was saying, still less what was being said.
“Yes, le beau d’Arcy. Marie de Fresnoy told me that the other day at the races he was about to pay a ragamuffin of a girl for a flower, when she said: ‘I’d rather you kissed me.’ Fancy that! She told me too that a man who had a husband’s reasons for wanting to kill him, was afraid to say a word. It appears he is a dead shot. But it appears also that your lovely Barouffski is one of the best swordsmen here. Verplank had better look out. To return though to our Chablis Moutonne. What will you do?”
Leilah, her thoughts afar, made no reply.
“What will you do?” Violet repeated.
From afar the question floated, descended, trod among the tender places of Leilah’s soul. At the pain of it she winced. “God help me, I do not know.”
Violet, cocking an eye again, insinuated: “Let me take a hand.” She paused, then, for clincher, threw out: “He dines here tomorrow.”
===Chinese===
“达尔西！”蕾拉重复道。她几乎没有听。她几乎不知道自己在说什么，更不知道人家在说什么。
“是啊，le beau d’Arcy（美男子达尔西）。玛丽·德·弗雷努瓦（Marie de Fresnoy）告诉我，前两天在赛马会上，他要付钱给一个野丫头买一朵花，那丫头却说：『我宁愿你亲我一下。』你想想！她还告诉我，有个男人明明有充分的做丈夫的理由想杀他，却吓得一句话不敢说。据说他是个神枪手。可据说你那位漂亮的巴罗夫斯基也是此地数一数二的剑客。弗普兰克最好当心点。不过话再说回来，我们的夏布利·穆托纳（Chablis Moutonne）——你打算怎么办？”
蕾拉思绪在远处，没有作答。
“你打算怎么办？”维奥莱特又问。
那问话从远处飘来，落下来，踩进蕾拉灵魂的柔软处。她疼得一缩。“上帝帮帮我，我不知道。”
维奥莱特又斜起眼睛，委婉地进言：“让我来插一手。”她顿了顿，随后抛出一句定乾坤的话：“他明天来这儿吃晚饭。”

===Original===
“Here!” Leilah exclaimed, half rising, fearful now that at any moment he might appear. “Here! With you?”
Violet nodded. “Why yes. Why not? If I can’t confess you, perhaps I can him. At any rate I can try. You can’t blame me for wanting to, either. You abandoned him on your honeymoon. You won’t tell me why and he says he don’t know. But he must suspect. He must have concluded that you left him for this, that or the other. I want to find out what his this, that and the other are and then make my own selection. It is true he did say that it was because of Barouffski. But that’s all gammon. You never saw Barouffski until you got here. There is something else and what that is I want to find out. No, you can’t blame me. It is the instinct of self-preservation. If I don’t get at the bottom of it soon, I shall simply go mad.”
A laugh, clear and musical, wound up the lady’s chatter. She had no more idea of going mad than she had of jumping out of the window. But she wanted to know and that was only human.
But now, Leilah, who a moment before had half risen, stood up. “Violet, I am not well, you must let me go. Yes,” she added as the lady remarked that on the morrow she might appear in the Rue de la Pompe. “Yes, yes.”
She would have said yes to anything. Hurriedly she got away.
Without the motor waited.
===Chinese===
“这儿！”蕾拉惊呼，半站起身，此刻生怕他随时会出现。“这儿！跟你们？”
维奥莱特点头。“是啊，为什么不？我要是没法替你办告解，兴许能替他办。至少我可以试试。我想这么做，你也怪不得我。你在蜜月里抛下了他。你不肯告诉我为什么，他又说他不知道。可他必定在犯疑。他必定早就断定你是为了这个、那个或别的什么离开他的。我就想弄清他的这个、那个和别的什么都是些什么，然后自己挑一个。他确实说过是因为巴罗夫斯基。可那全是鬼话。你到这儿之前根本没见过巴罗夫斯基。另有隐情，我要弄清那隐情是什么。不，你怪不得我。这是自我保全的本能。再不把这事挖到根儿上，我就要疯了。”
一声清脆如乐的笑，给这位夫人的絮叨收了尾。她要去疯，正如她要跳窗，一样都没想过。可她想知道，这不过是人之常情。
可就在这时，方才半起身的蕾拉站了起来。“维奥莱特，我不舒服，你得让我走。好，”这位夫人插话说她明天不妨到蓬佩街（Rue de la Pompe）去一趟，她应了一声，“好，好。”
叫她说什么都行。她匆匆脱了身。
外面，汽车候着。

===Original===
“Home,” she told the groom.
A little before she had thought herself the most miserable of beings. But however deep the hell, there is always a deeper one. Add uncertainty to distress and the sum of it is sorrow multiplied by the infinite. That hell, that sorrow was or seemed to be, hers. She did not know where to go, what to do, to whom to turn.
The pitiable plan of flight returned to her. Again she put it aside. She could not adopt it now. Besides, though she owed a duty to herself, she owed another to Verplank. In what manner he had failed to receive the letter, it was impossible for her to imagine, but the fact that he had not received it, hurt her doubly, hurt her for herself, hurt her for him. Had it reached him, both would have been spared this pass. But it had not reached him and since then what must he have thought of her? What!
The query, which kept repeating itself, tortured her and on that torture was superposed the precarious problem of his enlightenment. See him she could not. To write was beyond her ability. For there are things no pen should write as there are others no tongue should tell. None the less the truth she knew must reach him and would do so best, she thought, through some channel similar to that from which the letter had proceeded, from a source either indifferent or inimical to them both.
At the autosuggestion, her thoughts fluttered, scattered, grouped, then suddenly regrouping, produced a name. Beneath her breath she uttered it.
“Barouffski!”
===Chinese===
“回家。”她对司机说。
片刻之前，她还自认为是天下最苦的人。可地狱无论多深，总有更深的一层。在苦难之上再加上不确定，其总和便是悲伤乘以无穷。那个地狱、那份悲伤，是她的——或者说，似乎是她的。她不知道去哪里、做什么、向谁求助。
那个可怜的出走计划又回来了。她又把它推开。如今她不能用它了。况且，她对自己负有一份责任，对弗普兰克也负有一份。那封信是以什么方式没能到他手里，她无从想象；可它没到他手里这个事实，双重地伤了她——为她自己伤，也为他伤。信若到了，两人都不至于落到这一步。可它没到，从那以后，他必定把她想成什么样了？什么样！
这个问题反复自问，折磨着她；在这折磨之上，又叠着一个悬而未决的难题：如何让他知道真相。见他，她做不到。写信，她力不能及。因为有些事笔不该写，正如有些事口不该讲。然而她知道，真相必须传到他那里，而传得最好的路子——她想——莫过于当初那封信所自出的那条渠道：一个对他们两人都无所谓、或者干脆怀有敌意的源头。
这个自我暗示之下，她的思绪扑动、散开、聚拢，随后猛地重新列队，给出了一个名字。她低声吐出它。
“巴罗夫斯基！”

===Original===
It was not in provision of this that she had married him. At the time no such possibility had even impossibly loomed.
