But she had married him precisely as she had obtained a divorce, in order to barricade the future from the past; in order also for the fleshpots which she craved﻿—peace and security. She had not had much of either. None the less, the primary object which she had sought had, in its accomplishment, persisted. He was a barricade. He was her official and paid protector.
For the task therefore which she could not perform, he seemed naturally indicated. What alone gave her pause was the certainty that he would enjoy it. She could see him, see his ambiguous smile, see his green eyes aglow, his cruel and sensual mouth distended.
From the picture she turned. Beyond was a church, the frontal draped with black. The motor had stopped. It had reached the house in the Rue de la Pompe and pending the opening of the doors, whirred as it blocked the sidewalk.
It was then that she turned. Beside her, arrested by the car, stood Verplank.
After walking up from Voisin’s with Silverstairs he had left him a moment earlier at Tempest’s.
But the great doors had opened. Before Verplank could speak, the machine slid in. As it entered the court, the doors closed noisily.
===Chinese===
她嫁他，原本并不是为这个预备的。当初，这样的可能性连不可能的边都没沾过。可她嫁他，正如她办离婚一样，为的是把未来与过去隔开；也为的她渴求的那些肥甘——安宁与保障。两样她都没得到多少。尽管如此，她当初谋求的首要目标，在实现之后，依然立在那里。他是一道栅栏。他是她名正言顺、拿了钱的保护人。
因此，这桩她无力履行的差事，他似乎天然就是人选。唯一让她踌躇的，是确信他会从中取乐。她看得见他，看得见他那暧昧的笑，那双燃着光的绿眼睛，那张残忍而肉感的嘴咧开来。
她从这幅画面上转过脸。前方是一座教堂，正面垂着黑幔。汽车停了。已经到了蓬佩街的宅子门前，等大门开的工夫，机器嗡嗡响着，挡在人行道边。
就在这时她转过身。车旁，被汽车截住的，站着弗普兰克。
他和西尔弗斯泰尔斯从瓦赞餐厅一路走上来，片刻之前刚把他留在坦皮斯特家。
可大门开了。弗普兰克还没来得及开口，汽车已经滑了进去。它驶进院子，两扇门轰然合拢。

## VIII / 八

===Original===
On alighting at the perron, Leilah had as always to endure the ceremonial of two footmen assiduously assisting her.
“Emmanuel,” she said to one of them. “Is Monsieur Barouffski at home?”
“No, madame la comtesse.”
Leilah passed on and up. For a moment, in the hall above, she hesitated. Then, pushing a portière aside, she entered a salon, went to the window, and looked out. Crossing the court was Verplank.
===Chinese===
蕾拉在门廊台阶下车，照例得经受两名男仆殷勤搀扶的仪节。
“埃马纽埃尔（Emmanuel），”她对其中一个说，“巴罗夫斯基先生在家吗？”
“不在，伯爵夫人。”
蕾拉径自往里走，上了楼。片刻，她在楼上厅堂里犹豫。随后，她掀开一道门帘，走进一间客厅，走到窗前望出去。正穿过院子的，是弗普兰克。

===Original===
Fear and the fear of it, the throttling sensation which children know when pursued, enveloped her. With an idea of telling the servants that she was out, that she was ill, that she could see no one, she turned. On a table near the entrance was a service of Sevrès. Its tender hues were repeated on the ceiling. Beneath was the mirror of a waxed and polished floor. On the glistening woodwork her foot slipped. She staggered, recovered herself, got to the door.
Already Verplank had entered. She could hear him. He was not asking, he was demanding to see her. The form of the order mounted violently.
“Tell your mistress that I am here.”
Even then, with the idea that she might still deny herself, Leilah drew back into the room. Mentally she was framing a phrase when Emmanuel entered.
With that air domestics have when tidying something objectionable, the footman reconstructed Verplank’s command:
“There is a monsieur who inquires whether madame la comtesse receives?”
===Chinese===
恐惧，以及对这恐惧的恐惧——孩子被追赶时知道的那种被扼住喉咙的感觉——裹住了她。她想吩咐仆人，就说她出门了、病了、谁也不见，一面转过身。入口近旁一张桌上摆着一套塞夫勒（Sèvres）瓷器。它那柔嫩的色泽在天花板上重复着。脚下是打过蜡、擦得锃亮的地板，一面镜子。她在光溜溜的木地板上滑了一下，踉跄，稳住，到了门边。
弗普兰克已经进来了。她听得见他。他不是在求见，是在勒令见她。命令的调门陡然升高。
“告诉你们夫人，我在这儿。”
即便那时，想着兴许还能闭门不见，蕾拉退回房里。她心里正拼凑一句话，埃马纽埃尔进来了。
带着仆人们收拾什么碍眼东西时惯有的神情，那男仆把弗普兰克的命令改装了一遍：
“有一位先生问，伯爵夫人是否见客？”

===Original===
“Tell him﻿—”
But the injunction, as yet not wholly formed, was never completed. Verplank, brushing the man aside, strode in.
Leilah, retreating before him, motioned at Emmanuel, and the servant, with an affronted air of personal grievance, vacated this room that was charged now with the vibrations of hostilities begun.
Retreating yet farther, her eyes on the foe, Leilah stared at him, and, as she retreated, Verplank, staring, too, advanced. In his stare were threats so voluble that she thought: “He will kill me.” At the thought, there appeared before her Death’s liberating face, the mysteriously consoling visage which it reveals to those alone who have reached the depth of human woe.
Beyond, from the church, came the music of an organ. A requiem was being held. Leilah felt as though it were her own.
Verplank, his hands clenched, the look of an executioner about him, threw at her:
===Chinese===
“告诉他——”
可这句还没成形的吩咐终究没有说完。弗普兰克把那人拨到一边，大步走了进来。
蕾拉在他面前节节后退，朝埃马纽埃尔摆手；仆人带着一副受了人身冒犯的神气退出房间——此刻屋里已充满了开战的震颤。
她退得更远，眼睛盯着这仇敌；她退，弗普兰克也盯着，往前逼。他的盯视里满是滔滔的威胁，她想：“他会杀了我的。”这念头一起，死亡那张解脱的面孔便出现在她面前——那张神秘的、安慰人的脸，只向独自抵达人间苦难最深处的人显现。
外面，从教堂传来管风琴的乐声。一场安魂弥撒正在进行。蕾拉觉得那仿佛是给她自己做的。
弗普兰克双手攥紧，周身一副刽子手的神气，向她抛来：

===Original===
“For six months I have been looking for you. I am come to have you tell me why I have had to look at all.”
“*Dies irae, dies illa*,” admirably, in a clear contralto, a woman’s voice rang out.
Neither heard it. At the menace of the man, Leilah shrank, and in an effort at defense cried pitiably:
“Gulian! I left a letter for you.”
Angrily he tossed his head.
“I received none, nor did I need any to tell me that there are women on the street, others in jail, that are less vile than you.”
===Chinese===
“六个月来我一直在找你。我来，就是要你告诉我，我凭什么要找。”
“*Dies irae, dies illa*（震怒之日，那一天），”一个女人的嗓音以清亮的女低音，出色地响起。
两人都没有听见。在那男人的威逼下，蕾拉瑟缩着，试图防卫，可怜地喊道：
“格里安！我给你留了一封信。”
他愤然一甩头。
“我一封也没收到，也不需要谁来告诉我：街上的暗娼，牢里的女犯，都比你干净。”

===Original===
“*Teste David cum Sibylla*,” clearly and beautifully the voice resumed.
“Gulian!” Leilah cried again.
With whips in his words, he added:
“No harlot could have acted more infamously than you.”
At the lash of the outrage, Leilah, joining her hands, held them to him. “Gulian! You are killing me!”
“It is what you deserve. There are no penalties now for such turpitudes as yours. But, when there were, women like you were beaten with rods, they were lapidated, stoned to death, and death was too good for them; they should have been made to go about, as they afterward were, as you should be, in a yellow wig, in a yellow gown, that even children might point and cry: ‘Shame!’ ”
===Chinese===
“*Teste David cum Sibylla*（大卫与西比拉可为见证），”那嗓音清美地续道。
“格里安！”蕾拉又喊。
他的话里带着鞭子，又添一句：
“就是娼妇也做不出比你更下作的行事。”
这一记凌辱的鞭子抽下来，蕾拉合起双手，向他伸过去。“格里安！你在杀我！”
“这是你应得的。如今对你这等的丑行再没有刑罚了。可从前有的时候，你这样的女人是要挨鞭笞的，是要被绑去受石刑、被石头砸死的，而且死刑对她们都嫌太便宜；本该让她们——后来也确实让了——像你该受的那样，戴着黄色假发、穿着黄裙子游街，好连小孩子都能指着她喊：‘可耻！’”

===Original===
The words, which he tore from his mouth, he hurled at her. She cowered before them. On a chair nearby she had put her bag. Her wrap had fallen from her. In the church now the hymn had ceased. The ringing of the Elevation was beginning.
“Gulian! As if shame had not cried at me! Gulian, I have been scourged, I have been stoned. If I live, it is to implore of you mercy.”
Her hands, still joined, were still extended, and in her face was an expression of absolute despair. But this martyr attitude seemed to him the most abominable of hypocrisies, and it was with anger refreshed that he lashed her again.
“Mercy? Yes, you want mercy, you, who were merciless in your treachery to me. A sweep would have had more decency, a scullion more heart. I put in your hands my trust, my love, my honour, and you who want mercy dragged them in dirt.”
“Gulian!” Within her now was that invincible need of justice which impels the weakest to protest against the savagery of wrong. “Gulian! When you know!”
“I do know. I know you and your lies, and the infamy of them too well. At Coronado﻿—”
===Chinese===
这些话他从嘴里撕下来，掷到她身上。她在话前蜷缩。近旁一张椅子上放着她的小提包。她的披肩从身上滑落了。教堂里的圣咏这时已停。举扬圣体的铃声正开始鸣响。
“格里安！好像『可耻』没有冲我喊过似的！格里安，我挨过鞭笞，挨过石头。我若还活着，只为求你一点怜悯。”
她的双手仍合着、仍伸着，脸上是一副彻底绝望的神情。可这副殉道者的姿态在他看来是虚伪中最可憎的，于是他带着续了新的怒气，又鞭了她一记。
“怜悯？是啊，你要怜悯——你，对我的背信里那样残忍的你。一个扫烟囱的都比你知廉耻，一个洗碗工都比你有心肝。我把我的信托、我的爱、我的名誉放进你手里，而你这个要怜悯的人，把它们拖进了泥里。”
“格里安！”此刻她心里升起那股不可战胜的、对公道的需要——它驱策最弱者起而抗议冤屈的暴虐。“格里安！等你知道了！”
“我知道。我太知道你、太知道你的谎、也太知道那些谎的下作了。在科罗纳多——”

===Original===
“Gulian! You are not killing me merely, you torture my very soul.”
He sneered.
“Do I? Do I, indeed! No, you compliment yourself. It is what I want to do, but you cheat me even there. No woman with a soul could have done this soulless thing.”
The brutality of the arraignment shook her. She leaned against the chair for support. She felt hopeless, helpless, defenseless, and it was because the need for justice still impelled her, that she protested anew.
“Gulian, if only you knew! If only you had had that letter! Had it reached you, you would know that there was no deceit, that I left you for your sake as well as my own. Gulian, if I had not gone you would have seen and made me tell you, and then it may be you would have taken me and thrown me with you from the yacht.”
There were tears in her words. With one hand she held to the chair, the other she raised to her head. It pained her. She felt bruised and looked it.
===Chinese===
“格里安！你不只是在杀我，你在拷问我的灵魂。”
他冷笑。
“是吗？真是吗！不，你可真抬举自己。这是我想做的，可连这个你也骗了我。有灵魂的女人做不出这种没有灵魂的事。”
这番控诉的残暴震得她发颤。她靠住椅子支撑着。她感到无望、无助、无防，可正是那股对公道的需要仍在驱策她，使她再度抗辩。
“格里安，你要是知道就好了！你要是收到那封信就好了！它若到了你手里，你就会知道这里没有欺骗，知道我离开你，既为我自己，也为你自己。格里安，我若不走，你就会看出来、逼我说出来，那时说不定你会拉着我和你一起从游艇上跳进海里。”
她的话里带着泪。一只手扶着椅子，另一只手举到头上。头在痛。她觉得自己遍体鳞伤，看上去也确实如此。

===Original===

> “Ecce panis Angelorum
 

 Factus cibus viatorum﻿—”

===Chinese===

> 「看哪，天使的食粮（Ecce panis Angelorum）
 

 而成为旅途者的食粮（Factus cibus viatorum）——」


===Original===
Beyond, sustained by the arpeggios of the organ, the voice of a singer mounted sheerly like a thread of gold. It lowered and heightened. Presently, on a note, as if abruptly snapped, it ceased. The organ continued. It renewed the canticle. It projected a scale that ascended slowly, as though upward and onward, over the limitless steps of eternity, it were lifting the soul of the dead.
Leilah wished it were her own. Sadly she added:
“God knows it would have been better. Anything would be better than that you should speak to me as you do.”
There is an innocence that appeals, a sincerity that disarms, a candour that outfaces every proof, and Verplank, who had been bent on overwhelming this woman with a contempt which he felt wholly deserved, was impressed, in spite of himself, by the evident ingenuousness, by the evident wretchedness, too, of her words.
He moved back.
“You say I would have made you tell me?”
===Chinese===
外面，一个歌者的嗓音倚着管风琴的琶音，像一根金线笔直攀升。它低下去，又扬起来。不多时，在一个音上，仿佛骤然绷断，它停了。管风琴继续着。它重续那支颂歌，铺出一段缓缓上升的音阶，仿佛沿着永恒那没有尽头的台阶，向上、向前，托举着死者的灵魂。
蕾拉但愿那是自己的灵魂。她凄然续道：
“上帝知道，那样倒好。什么都比你现在这样对我说话强。”
世上有一种叫人心软的无辜，一种解除武装的诚恳，一种能直面一切证据的坦白；弗普兰克本打算用他认定她全然应得的轻蔑把这女人压垮，却不由自主地被她话中那显而易见的真挚、还有那显而易见的凄楚打动了。
他退了一步。
“你是说，我会逼你说出来？”

===Original===
“Yes. Yes. You would have.”
“But made you tell me what?”
Leilah, still holding one hand to her head, raised the other from the chair, and with it made a gesture slight, yet desolate.
“What was it?” he asked.
Before replying, she looked away.
“What I hid from you rather than repeat.”
===Chinese===
“会。会。你会的。”
“可逼你说什么？”
蕾拉仍把一只手按在头上，另一只手从椅子上抬起，做了个手势——轻微，却荒凉。
“是什么？”他问。
回答之前，她把目光移开。
“那件事我宁可瞒着你，也不愿重述。”

===Original===
“But repeat what?”
Her face still turned from him, she answered:
“Something, my﻿—something Mr. Ogston sent me.”
“Mr. Ogston!” Verplank exclaimed. The formality of the statement astounded him. “Do you mean your father? What did he send you?”
But Leilah would not or could not speak. Her mouth contracted as though she were choking, and she put a hand to her throat.
“Tell me,” he insisted.
===Chinese===
“可重述什么？”
她仍把脸背着他，答道：
“一件事，我的——一样奥格斯顿先生（Mr. Ogston）寄给我的东西。”
“奥格斯顿先生！”弗普兰克失声。这称呼的正式程度令他骇然。“你是说你父亲？他寄给你什么？”
可蕾拉不肯说，也说不出。她的嘴抽紧，仿佛透不过气，一只手按住喉咙。
“告诉我。”他坚持。

===Original===
She turned, and beseechingly she looked at him.
“Gulian, I cannot.”
At that Verplank moved nearer, and so dominatingly that again she extended her hands.
“Gulian, I will get someone else to tell you. I had intended to. Believe me, it is better so.”
“It concerns me?”
“Yes, you.”
===Chinese===
她转过身，哀恳地望着他。
“格里安，我说不出口。”
这话一出，弗普兰克逼近过来，那样咄咄逼人，她又伸出了双手。
“格里安，我会找别人告诉你。我本就打算这样。相信我，这样更好。”
“与我有关？”
“是，与你。”

===Original===
“And you?”
“Yes, both of us.”
“Then you shall tell me, and tell me now. Do you hear?”
“Gulian!” she cried. She raised her clasped hands to him. “Gulian!”
But Verplank, his jaw ominously square, confronted her.
“I say you shall.”
===Chinese===
“也与你？”
“是，与我们两个。”
“那你就得告诉我，现在就告诉我。听见没有？”
“格里安！”她喊。她把合着的双手举向他。“格里安！”
可弗普兰克下颌绷成不祥的方角，与她对面而立。
“我说了，你就得说。”

===Original===
“Don’t look at me then,” she pleaded. “Bend your head, bend it lower. One second, then I will. One second﻿—one. Ah, God! I cannot.”
Verplank, who at her bidding had stooped, straightened himself, and caught at her.
“I say you shall.”
“Gulian, a moment. Give me a moment. Now bend your head again. One moment, Gulian; your father, your father﻿—My mother loved him.”
“Your mother loved my father!”
“Gulian, I am his daughter.”
===Chinese===
“那你就别看着我，”她哀求，“低下头，再低些。一秒钟，然后我就说。一秒钟——就一。啊，上帝！我说不出口。”
依着她的话俯下头去的弗普兰克直起身，一把抓住她。
“我说了，你就得说。”
“格里安，等一下。给我一刻。现在再低下头。一刻，格里安；你的父亲，你的父亲——我的母亲爱过他。”
“你的母亲爱过我的父亲！”
“格里安，我是他的女儿。”

===Original===
“You are what?”
“I am your sister.”
As she whispered it, she covered her face. Verplank started, straightened again, raised his arm, and, with a gesture wide, elemental, absurd, and human, struck at the empty air.
Savagely he turned to her.
“And you believe this?”
Leilah, her head bowed, her face covered, shook with sobs.
===Chinese===
“你是什么？”
“我是你的妹妹。”
她低声说出这话，双手掩面。弗普兰克一个激灵，再度直起身，抬起手臂，做了一个大大的、原始的、荒唐而又人性的手势，朝空处击去。
他狂暴地转向她。
“而这个你信？”
蕾拉低着头，掩着脸，抽泣得直颤。

===Original===
“You believe it?” he repeated.
“There were letters,” she stammered. “Three letters. No one could read them and not﻿—and not﻿—”
“And it was for this you left me?”
A fresh access seized her. He could not see her tears, he heard them.
“And it was for this you got a divorce?”
On the chair beside her was her bag. She felt in it, and got out a handkerchief.
===Chinese===
“你信这个？”他重复。
“有几封信，”她讷讷地说，“三封信。谁读了都不能不——不能不——”
“你就为这个离开我？”
新一阵恸哭攫住了她。他看不见她的泪，却听得见。
“你就为这个办了离婚？”
她身旁的椅子上放着提包。她伸手进去，摸出一方手帕。

===Original===
“And it was for this you took that cad?”
Slowly, with infinite hesitations, the bit of cambric held to a face that was wet and white, she turned to him.
“I thought you would forget. I thought you would marry. I thought you would be happy. I hoped so that you would. But my leaving you, the divorce, the marriage, these things were done with no idea of happiness. They were to serve as barriers between us.”
Impotently he stamped a foot. He was furious still. But his anger had deflected. He was enraged less at her than at circumstances.
“Rubbish! That’s what your barriers are.”
Leilah, wiping her eyes, turned from him. The barriers, however fragile, were not rubbish to her.
===Chinese===
“你就为这个嫁了那个下流胚？”
她举着那方细麻纱帕，贴着一张湿而苍白的脸，带着无穷的迟疑，慢慢转向他。
“我以为你会忘掉。我以为你会另娶。我以为你会幸福。我多盼着你会。可我离开你、离婚、再嫁，这些事没有一件是冲着幸福去的。它们原是要在我们中间充当壁垒。”
他颓然跺了跺脚。他还在盛怒之中，可怒火已经偏转。他恼的与其说是她，不如说是这个局面。
“胡扯！你的壁垒就是这么回事。”
蕾拉拭着眼睛，把脸从他那边转开。那壁垒，再怎么脆弱，对她可不是胡扯。

===Original===
Violently he continued:
“As for Barouffski﻿—”
But Leilah, turning to him again, interrupted:
“Gulian, let me tell you. Last night I planned to have someone ask you, for my sake, to go away. Gulian, I thought you would, but I determined if you would not that I would go.”
Verplank moved back.
“Go! Go where?”
===Chinese===
他怒气不减地继续：
“至于巴罗夫斯基——”
可蕾拉又转向他，打断道：
“格里安，让我告诉你。昨天夜里我打定主意，要托人求你看在我的份上走开。格里安，我以为你会的；可我也下了决心，你若不走，我就走。”
弗普兰克退了一步。
“走！走去哪儿？”

===Original===
“Ah! God knows! Anywhere. Wherever I could hide myself. Wherever I could hide my love for you.”
Her eyes had been raised to his. At the confession they lowered of themselves. Then again she looked him in the face.
“Gulian, it is that which cried shame at me. It is that which scourged me with rods bitterer than those of which you spoke. You say the barriers are nothing. Gulian, you are wrong. To me they are eternal.”
“Yes,” he angrily retorted. “Yes, if your story were true. But it isn’t. It’s arrant nonsense.”
In miserable protest, she half raised a hand.
“Gulian, when I read those letters my youth died in me. Never since they reached me have I had the heart to smile. If you had seen them you would have felt the truth in every line.”
===Chinese===
“啊！上帝知道！哪儿都行。随便哪个我能藏住自己的地方。随便哪个我能藏住我对你的爱的地方。”
她的眼睛原是抬着望他的。这句自白出口，眼睛自己垂了下去。随后她重又直视他的脸。
“格里安，冲我喊『可耻』的正是它。用比你说的那些更苦的鞭子抽我的，正是它。你说壁垒算不了什么。格里安，你错了。于我，它们是永恒的。”
“是啊，”他愤然反唇相讥，“是啊，倘若你这套话是真的。可它不是。这是彻头彻尾的胡说。”
她惨然抗议似的半举起一只手。
“格里安，读那些信的时候，我的青春就在我心里死了。从它们到我手里那天起，我再没心思笑过。你若见过它们，你会觉出每一行里的真实。”

===Original===
“I would have felt nothing of the kind; the fact that you still care for me ought to show you that they are false.”
“Gulian, I tried to think that, too; but even in trying I felt that I was pleading for myself.”
“Then, for the love of God, stop pleading and act! Look at yourself, look at me! We could not be more unlike if we came from different planets.”
She was making an effort to answer. He stopped her.
“Listen to this. If you can’t act, I shall. My mother is in London. Tomorrow she is to be here. Probably she can tell me the truth. If not, I will go to the States. There I will see your father. When I return it will be with proofs. I will bring them if I have to drag that old scoundrel with me.”
He paused. Though angry still, her story had pacified him. He felt it to be false, none the less she had believed it and the fact that she had, absolved her of much that she had done. However she had erred, she had at least tried to do right. He closed and opened a hand, looked at it and from it looked at her.
===Chinese===
“我什么也觉不出；你如今还惦着我，这一点本该让你明白那些信是假的。”
“格里安，我也想这样想；可想的时候我就觉得，我是在替自己求情。”
“那么，看在上帝份上，别再求情了，行动起来！看看你自己，看看我！我们就算来自两个星球，也不能更不相像了。”
她正要勉力作答。他止住了她。
“听好。你若不动手，我就动手。我母亲在伦敦。明天她就到这儿。多半她能告诉我真相。若不能，我就回美国去。我要去见你父亲。等我回来，手里就会带着证据。哪怕得把那老恶棍亲手拖来，我也会把证据带来。”
他顿住。怒气虽在，她这套话已让他平静了些。他觉得那是假的，可她信了；而她信了这一事实，便为她所做的许多事开了脱。无论她怎么错，她至少努力想做对的。他一只手握拢又张开，看看它，又从它看到她。

===Original===
“But first I will see my mother. In any case I will be here tomorrow. Yes, that’s what I’ll do. Why shouldn’t I come. Why not?”
Leilah did not answer. She did not believe he would come, except to cause fresh agony to them both there was no reason why he should do so. The horror which she had told him and to which incredulously he had listened was gospel to her, an evil gospel, yes, but none the less a true one. Besides if he did come as, in any case, he said he would, he might meet Barouffski, and affrightedly she foresaw blows, afterwards a duel﻿—one which she was unaware was then impending.
“Why not?” Verplank repeated, fumbling her as he spoke with suspicious eyes and appearing to divine and to resent her forecast.
She caught at a straw. Usually, between four and seven, Barouffski was tabled at baccarat, gambling with her money. That straw she produced.
“Come at five.”
Verplank, appeased, nodded. “Very good, at five then.”
===Chinese===
“可我先得见我母亲。不管怎样，我明天会来。对，就这么办。我为什么不能来。为什么不能？”
蕾拉没有回答。她不信他会来：除了给他们两人再添一层新的苦痛，他没有来的理由。她讲给他听、而他疑信参半地听下的那个恐怖，于她是福音——一卷恶的福音，是的，却仍是一卷真的。况且他若真来——他反正说了要来——兴许会撞见巴罗夫斯基，她惊恐地预见到拳脚，随后是一场决斗——她还不知道，那决斗当时已迫在眉睫。
“为什么不能？”弗普兰克重复，一面用狐疑的目光在她身上搜来索去，仿佛窥破了她的盘算，并为之心生不快。
她抓住了一根稻草。往常，四点到七点，巴罗夫斯基总坐在巴卡拉（baccarat）赌桌前，赌的是她的钱。她递出了这根稻草。
“五点来吧。”
弗普兰克消了气，点头。“很好，那就五点。”

===Original===
But at once she realised that other safeguards were needful. She hesitated, looked about her, looked at Verplank, gave him his hat, motioned to him. Then, preceding him, she passed into an adjoining salon, entered the dining room and moved from it to the garden below.
Passably mystified, he followed.
The air, freighted with fragrance, stirred by music from the church, the dogs, at sight of him, charged suddenly with menaces. Straining at their chains, viciously they clamoured.
Indifferently Verplank glanced from them to the gate beyond, to which Leilah was leading him.
When both reached it, she opened it and said: “Come this way tomorrow, will you?”
For a second he considered her. Her face was as a book in which he could read the reason. In view of many things, particularly of the duel, it seemed to him all very puerile.
===Chinese===
可她立刻明白还需要别的防范。她迟疑着，环顾四周，看看弗普兰克，把帽子递给他，朝他示意。随后她引着他，穿过隔壁一间客厅，进了饭厅，又从饭厅下到花园。
他半是懵懂地跟了去。
空气载着芬芳，被教堂的乐声搅动；那群狗一看见他，便挟着威胁猛冲过来，绷紧锁链，恶狠狠地吠成一片。
弗普兰克淡淡地扫了它们一眼，又看向更远处那扇门——蕾拉正引他往那儿去。
两人到了门边，她推开它，说：“明天从这边进来，好吗？”
有几秒他端详她。她的脸像一本书，缘由他一读便知。念及种种，尤其是那场决斗，这一切在他看来未免太孩子气。

===Original===
But, replacing his hat, grimly he nodded. “Before then I have rather an idea that there may be a deficit among us.”
This expression, in itself perhaps overprecise, was too much for her and the fact that it was showed itself in her eyes.
Without heeding their inquiry he nodded again. “I will come this way but only that together we may leave by the other.”
Again he nodded. In a moment he had gone.
Leilah, closing the gate behind him watched him go. It was, she felt, her last earthly sight of him. There would be no going away together. He would never come back. Never. His mother, if she knew the truth, could only substantiate it. If she did not, another would. Helplessly she held at the gate. A vagrant passed, she did not see. A hawker called, she did not hear. She was not only helpless, she was hopeless. She wished that death really were, that it could beneficently come, take her, shroud her in blankness, in endless oblivion of what was and of what might have been. Long since the dogs, mollified by Verplank’s exit, had ceased to bark. Shrilly now from the church came boys’ fresh voices. The music of them stirred her a little and she turned.
Before her, framed in a window of the dining room, Barouffski stood. At sight of him she started. Amiably he smiled. When she looked again he had vanished.
===Chinese===
可他把帽子扣回头上，冷硬地点头。“在那之前，我倒隐隐觉得，我们中间或许要出一笔亏空。”
这个说法——本身也许过于确切了——让她受不住，这一点在她眼睛里显了出来。
他不理会她们眼中的诘问，又点了点头。“我从这边来，只为让我们能一起从另一边离开。”
他再点点头。转眼人已走了。
蕾拉在他身后关上大门，望着他走远。这是她在这世上见他的最后一面，她感觉到了。不会再有双双出走。他再也不会回来。永远不会。他母亲若知道真相，只能证实它；若不知道，另一个人也会知道。她无望地扶着门。一个流浪汉走过，她没有看见。一个小贩叫卖，她没有听见。她不只是无助，她是无望。她但愿死亡真的存在，能仁慈地来，带她走，把她裹进一片空无，裹进对已然的和本可然的万事的无尽遗忘。狗早在弗普兰克出去时就安静了。此刻，教堂里尖亮地响起男童们清新的嗓音。那乐声轻轻搅动了她，她转过身。
她面前，饭厅的一扇窗框里，站着巴罗夫斯基。一见他她一惊。他和蔼地笑。她再看时，他已不见了。

===Original===
But, in a moment, in the doorway beneath, smiling still, he reappeared.
“What a beautiful day, is it not?” Oilily he rubbed his hands. “You have been having visitors, *cara mia*?”
As he spoke he moved toward her. Urbanely he continued! “And what did they have to say?”
He was quite near her now and, with his head held a trifle to one side he was regarding her with affectionate indulgence, much as one would regard a child.
“They told you nothing new, *cara mia*?”
Without looking at him, Leilah shook her head. “Nothing. Nothing at least that I did not know.”
===Chinese===
可不多时，在下面门道里，他仍笑着，重新现身。
“多美的一天，是不是？”他油滑地搓着手。“家里来了客人吧，*cara mia*（亲爱的）？”
他一面说一面向她走来，温文尔雅地续道！“他们都说些什么来着？”
他已离她很近，头微微偏向一侧，用一种宠溺的温存打量她，像打量一个孩子。
“他们没跟你说什么新鲜的吧，*cara mia*？”
蕾拉不看他，摇了摇头。“没什么。至少没有一件是我本来不知道的。”

===Original===
Smiling still, indulgent as before, Barouffski plucked at his pointed beard. “And what is that, *cara mia*?”
Remotely, in a voice without colour, as though speaking not to him at all but only to herself, she answered:
“That I am the most miserable woman in the world.”
Barouffski’s smile broadened. “Bah! They exaggerated, *cara mia*. It is the way of the world. *Mon Dieu, à qui se fier?* You are not at all what they said. You are﻿—how shall I put it﻿—perhaps a bit indiscreet. That is it, a bit indiscreet.” He pointed to the bench. “Will you not seat yourself?”
He was still smiling, but the smile wholly muscular, was one in which the eyes have no part. The “visitors” whom he affected to ridicule, alarmed him. They were, he knew, quite capable of taking Leilah away. Her presence or absence was quite one to him. Only if she departed, so would her purse.
“Will you not?” he repeated.
===Chinese===
巴罗夫斯基仍笑着，仍那样宠溺，捻着他的尖胡须。“那是些什么呢，*cara mia*？”
她远远地、用一个没有颜色的声音，仿佛根本不是对他说、只是对自己说，答道：
“我是世上最苦的女人。”
巴罗夫斯基的笑意宽了。“呸！他们夸大其词了，*cara mia*。世道就是这样。*Mon Dieu, à qui se fier?*（我的天，还能信谁呢？）您根本不是他们说的那种人。您是——该怎么说呢——也许有点欠审慎。就是这么回事，有点欠审慎。”他指着长凳。“您不坐坐吗？”
他还在微笑，可那笑纯然是肌肉的事，眼睛在里面没有份。他佯作讥嘲的那些「访客」令他惊慌。他知道，他们完全有可能把蕾拉带走。她在与不在，对他本无二致。只是她若走，她的钱袋也会跟着走。
“您不坐吗？”他重复。

===Original===
“I am going in.”
“Certainly, *cara mia*. It is as it pleases you. But﻿—”
At this Leilah, who had passed him, turned.
“Well, what?”
“You see, *cara mia*, supposing I had visitors. Supposing rather I had a visitor. We are only supposing, are we not? *Bon!* Supposing this visitor happened to be what we call an *ancienne*, an old flame, an inamorata of mine. Supposing that were so. Do you know what you could do?”
“No,” Leilah from over her shoulder answered. “Nor do I care.”
===Chinese===
“我进去了。”
“当然，*cara mia*。悉听尊便。不过——”
这话让已经走过他的蕾拉转过身。
“嗯，什么？”
“您瞧，*cara mia*，假设我有了访客。不如说，假设我有一位访客。我们只是假设，对不对？*Bon!*（好！）假设这位访客碰巧是我们所谓的 *ancienne*（旧相好）、一段旧情、我从前的一位恋人。假设如此。您知道您能做什么吗？”
“不知道，”蕾拉越过肩头答道。“也不想知道。”

===Original===
“Forgive me, *cara mia*. You mean that you do not care to be informed. Yet you should know, for you could if you wished have me fined. Yes, that is what you could do. You could have me fined.
“But I,” he resumed. “Do you know in similar circumstances what I could do? Do you know rather what the law says I may do? Do you, *cara mia*? Do you? For really you ought to.”
But Leilah now was approaching the entrance.
“What!” Barouffski exclaimed. “You are not interested? You are really going?”
As he spoke, he bowed. “*Bon, à ce soir*, *cara mia*. And a last word. If I may advise, do not be led into indiscretions.”
“Do not,” he repeated, while shrilly from the adjacent church came the voices of boys chanting the final phrase of the Pater Noster:
===Chinese===
“恕我直言，*cara mia*。您是说您不想听人告知。可您应该知道，因为只要您愿意，您可以让我吃罚金。对，那就是您能做的。您可以让我吃罚金。
“可我呢，”他续道，“您知道在同样的情形下，我能做什么吗？不如说，您知道法律说我可以做什么吗？您知道吗，*cara mia*？知道吗？说真的，您应该知道。”
可蕾拉这时已快走到入口。
“怎么！”巴罗夫斯基叫道，“您不感兴趣？您真要走？”
他一面说一面躬身。“*Bon, à ce soir*（好，晚上见），*cara mia*。还有一句最后的话。恕我进言，别被引入轻率之举。”
“别，”他重复道。与此同时，毗邻教堂里尖亮地传来男童们诵念《天主经》（Pater Noster）末句的嗓音：

===Original===
“*Sed libera nos a malo.*”
“That is it,” he called at Leilah’s retreating back. “Pray rather to be delivered of them. Otherwise﻿—”
But Leilah now had entered the house.
“Otherwise,” he continued to himself and moving to the kennels, patted the dogs, “otherwise a sojourn in Poland may improve you.”
===Chinese===
“*Sed libera nos a malo*（但救我们免于凶恶）。”
“正是这句，”他冲着蕾拉离去的背影喊，“不如求告，让你们得脱于它们。否则——”
可蕾拉已经进了屋。
“否则，”他自顾自继续说，一面走向狗舍拍拍那些狗，“否则，去波兰小住一阵，兴许能把您调理好。”

## IX / 九

===Original===
“The strawberries were delicious,” Violet, the following day, remarked to Leilah.

The two women were seated in the garden of the house in the Rue de la Pompe. It was just after luncheon and between them was a table on which coffee had been served. From without came the whirr of passing motors, the cries of those hawkers who are never still. But the garden itself was quiet, scented too and the day superb.

Violet, patting a yawn, resumed: “One never really gets strawberries except in Paris. They are so big! And so expensive, aren’t they? I know that in a restaurant a man gave one to the waiter for a tip.”

She looked about her. “But, mercy! What can have become of Aurelia? She was to have stopped for me.”

“Don’t you think it unwise to let her go on the stage?” Leilah, with an air of talking for talk’s sake, inquired.
===Chinese===
「草莓真是可口，」第二天，维奥莱特对蕾拉说。

两位女子坐在蓬佩街宅子的花园里。刚吃过午饭，两人中间摆着一张桌子，咖啡已经端上。墙外传来驶过的汽车的嗡鸣，还有那些永不停歇的小贩的叫卖。但花园本身很安静，还带着香气，天气也极好。

维奥莱特掩着一个哈欠，接着说：「只有巴黎才吃得到真正的草莓。个头多大呀！还多贵，是不是？我知道有家馆子里，一位先生拿一颗草莓当小费给了侍者。」

她环顾四周。「哎呀，天哪！奥蕾莉亚怎么还不来？她说好要来接我的。」

「你不觉得让她去登台演戏不明智吗？」蕾拉带着一副为说话而说话的神气问道。

===Original===
“Let her! But she’s got her head. I can’t prevent her. She’ll never come to any harm though. She isn’t the kind to want to do anything she thought was wrong. No indeed. She would never think anything wrong that she wanted to do. But tell me. I could not very well ask before the servants. What are you going to do?”

For a moment Leilah did not answer. Then, a bit resignedly, she folded her hands.

“I do not see that I have the ability to do anything.”

The pause, the gesture, the reply, angered Violet. She bristled.

“Don’t be so modest, you make me nervous!”
===Chinese===
「让！她自有个主意。我拦不住她。不过她绝不会吃亏的。她不是那种想做错事的人。才不呢。凡是她想做的事，她从来不觉得是错的。倒是告诉我——当着仆人我不便问——你打算怎么办？」

蕾拉一时没有作答。随后，有点听天由命似的，她交叠起双手。

「我不觉得自己还有能力做什么。」

这沉默、这手势、这回答，惹恼了维奥莱特。她炸了毛。

「别这么谦虚了，看得我着急！」

===Original===
For a moment she also paused. Then, ruminantly, as though in self communion, the lady uttered these cryptic words:

“But perhaps﻿—”

Leilah, who had turned away, turned to her. “Perhaps what?”

But Violet, compressing her lips, assumed the appearance which a very worldly, exquisitely gowned and beautiful Sphinx might present.

“Perhaps what?” Leilah, puzzled by the attitude, repeated.
===Chinese===
她也停了片刻。随后，若有所思，像在自言自语，这位女士吐出一句没头没尾的话：

「不过也许——」

蕾拉本已转过身去，又转了回来。「也许什么？」

可维奥莱特抿紧嘴唇，摆出一副深谙世事、衣装绝美、漂亮至极的斯芬克斯（Sphinx）才有的神情。

「也许什么？」蕾拉被这姿态弄得摸不着头脑，又问了一遍。

===Original===
“Oh, nothing. That is, nothing in particular. I was merely thinking from battle, murder and sudden death, Good Lord deliver us! And yet﻿—”

“Well?”

Violet looked her over. “I know I ought not to tell and for that very reason I will. Your two husbands are to fight today. They may be at it now﻿—”

Abruptly she made a face, dropped her voice and threw out:

“No such luck.”

In the doorway Barouffski stood.
===Chinese===
「哦，没什么。就是说，没什么特别的。我不过在想：战祸、凶杀与暴死，求好上帝救我们脱离！不过呢——」

「不过什么？」

维奥莱特把她上下打量。「我知道我不该说，可正因为不该说，我偏要说。你那两位丈夫今天要决斗。说不定这会儿已经动上手了——」

她突然做了个鬼脸，压低嗓门，脱口道：

「没那么好的运气。」

门口站着巴罗夫斯基。

===Original===
Leilah had not seen. Inwardly she had shrivelled. To the sudden knowledge that the two men were to fight, fear, as suddenly, superposed the conviction that Verplank would be killed. It stirred in her a wholly animal longing to get away from herself; to be rid, however transiently, of that sense of horror and helplessness which only the tortured know. In an effort to shut out the pain of it, she closed her eyes.

When she opened them, Barouffski was before her. Affably he was addressing her friend.

“Beautiful day, Lady Silverstairs. In London you do not often have such weather. I hope Lord Silverstairs is planning to keep you here a very long time.”

“What? What?” Violet in a crescendo of surprise, exclaimed.

Affably, smilingly, unperturbedly, Barouffski reiterated the expression of his hope.
===Chinese===
蕾拉没有看见。她心里已经缩成了一团。两个男人要决斗——这突如其来的认知之上，又骤然叠加了一层确信：弗普兰克会被杀死。这在她心里激起一种全然兽性的渴望：想逃离她自己；想摆脱——哪怕只摆脱片刻——那唯有受刑的人才懂的恐怖与无助。为了把这份痛关在外面，她闭上了眼睛。

睁开眼时，巴罗夫斯基已站在她面前。他正和颜悦色地同她的朋友寒暄。

「多好的天气，西尔弗斯泰尔斯夫人。伦敦可不常有这样的天。我希望西尔弗斯泰尔斯勋爵打算把您在这里长长久久地留住。」

「什么？什么？」维奥莱特的惊讶一声高过一声。

巴罗夫斯基和颜悦色、笑吟吟、不动声色地把他这番祝愿又表述了一遍。

===Original===
Icily Violet cut him short. “With us it is the woman who makes and unmakes plans.”

Barouffski, unabashed and smiling, plucked at his beard. “A most excellent custom. Yes. For when has reason governed the world? It is only by the heartstrings that men can be led and women alone can lead them.”

But now Violet with the air of an empress had risen. “Leilah, my motor is at the door. Let me take you for a turn in the Bois.”

“Do,” Barouffski exclaimed, looking as he spoke at Leilah. “You are a trifle pale, *cara mia*. A turn or two now in the Bois﻿—”

With a gesture he signified, that is what you need.
===Chinese===
维奥莱特冷冷地打断他。「在我们家，定计划、改计划的是女人。」

巴罗夫斯基毫不窘迫，笑吟吟地捻着胡须。「好一个绝妙的风俗。可不是。理性几时统治过世界？人只有靠心弦才能被牵动，而能牵动心弦的，唯有女人。」

可这时维奥莱特已带着女皇的气派站起身来。「蕾拉，我的汽车就在门口。让我带你去布洛涅森林兜一圈。」

「去呀，」巴罗夫斯基嚷道，说话间眼睛望着蕾拉。「你脸色有点白呢，*cara mia*（意大利语：我亲爱的）。这就去布洛涅森林转上一两圈——」

他做了个手势，意思是：那正是你需要的。

===Original===
Turning to Violet he added: “So thoughtful of you, Lady Silverstairs.”

In speaking he bowed for Violet now was vacating the garden and Leilah who had risen was following her.

Barouffski bowed again. “*Cara mia*, a pleasant drive to you.”

But, when both women had entered the house, he sighed, sighed with relief, looked about him, consulted his watch, looked again about him, moved to the entrance, touched a bell which presently a footman answered.

Barouffski indicated the table and chairs. “Get all that out of here.”
===Chinese===
他又转向维奥莱特：「您想得真周到，西尔弗斯泰尔斯夫人。」

说这话时他躬身行礼，因为维奥莱特正走出花园，蕾拉也已起身跟了上去。

巴罗夫斯基又是一躬。「*Cara mia*，祝你们兜风愉快。」

可是等两位女子都进了屋，他叹了口气——松了一口气似的叹了口气——环顾四周，看了看表，又环顾四周，走到大门口，按了按铃，一个男仆随即应声而来。

巴罗夫斯基指了指桌椅。「把这些统统搬出去。」

===Original===
“Perfectly, monsieur le comte,” the man, with marked deference, answered and started to do as bidden.

Barouffski checked him. “In five or ten minutes some gentlemen will come by the main entrance. Show them in the reception room. About the same time others will come by the gate. When they do, see that I am notified at once.”

“Perfectly, monsieur le comte.”

“Afterward, when they are gone, come back here and tidy up.”

“Perfectly, monsieur le comte.”
===Chinese===
「遵命，伯爵先生，」仆人必恭必敬地应道，动手照办。

巴罗夫斯基拦住他。「五到十分钟后，有几位先生会从正门来。把他们领进会客室。差不多同时，另有几位会从花园门来。他们一到，立刻通报我。」

「遵命，伯爵先生。」

「事后，等他们走了，再回到这里收拾干净。」

「遵命，伯爵先生。」

===Original===
But now Barouffski had turned, he was entering the house. The man stuck his tongue out at him. “*Canaille, va!*” he muttered. Raising his arms, he added: “Tidy up, eh? Tidy up what? The remains of your conversation, no doubt. Bah! That won’t be much.” He laughed, took first the table, then the chairs, vanished with them and reappeared.

A bell at the gate had sounded, he hurried there and bowing, admitted Aurelia and that young person’s young man.

The girl made straight for the kennels. “Parsnips!” she delightedly exclaimed. “Aren’t those two big brutes simply dear?”

Swiftly Emmanuel intervened. “Pardon, they are very savage.” Then, as the girl hesitated he added: “Will mademoiselle give herself the trouble to pass into the salon?”
===Chinese===
可这时的巴罗夫斯基已经转身进屋去了。仆人朝他背后吐了吐舌头。「*Canaille, va*（法语：好一个恶棍）！」他咕哝道。随即举起双臂，又补一句：「收拾，嗯？收拾什么？收拾你们谈话的残局吧，八成是。呸！那能有多少。」他笑了笑，先搬桌子，再搬椅子，搬得没了影，又现出身来。

花园门上的铃响了，他急忙赶过去，躬身放进来奥蕾莉亚和这位少女的年轻男友。

姑娘径直朝犬舍奔去。「欧防风（Parsnips）！」她喜不自胜地嚷道。「这两条大畜生难道不可爱死了？」

埃马纽埃尔敏捷地挡在中间。「对不起，它们凶得很。」见姑娘犹豫，他又添一句：「小姐可否劳步到客厅去？」

===Original===
Aurelia tossed her pretty head. “No, I like it here. Besides I hate suggestions. Tell Madame Barouffska that I have come on a most unimportant matter which will probably detain me a very long time.”

“Yes,” her companion rejoined as the footman retreated. “Yes, I often think that it is only unimportant matters that are really momentous.” In his hand was a stick which negligently he twirled. “What is this one, if I may ask?”

“I have forgotten.”

“Perhaps then it was really important.”

Aurelia, who, with her delicious face and delicate garments, looked like a wayward angel, lifted a finger.
===Chinese===
奥蕾莉亚把她那漂亮的脑袋一扬。「不，我就喜欢在这儿。再说，我最恨人家给我出主意。告诉巴罗夫斯卡夫人，我来的是一件顶顶无关紧要的事，多半要耽搁很久很久。」

「是呀，」男仆退下时，她的同伴应道，「是呀，我常想，真正要紧的，从来都只有那些无关紧要的事。」他手里拿着一根手杖，漫不经心地转着。「这一件是什么事，容我问一句？」

「我忘了。」

「那它八成真是件要紧事。」

奥蕾莉亚那张讨人喜欢的脸、那一身轻柔的衣裙，让她活像个任性的天使；她竖起一根手指。

===Original===
“So it was! So it was! I remember now I wanted to ask her how she likes matrimony.”

“Caesar!” the youth exclaimed. “You are not collecting data on the subject, are you?”

Meekly, with a treacherously innocent air, the girl surveyed him. “You wouldn’t wish me to take leaps in the dark, would you?”

“Certainly I would. Certainly I do﻿—since you are to take them with me.”

With the same wicked look, Aurelia moistened her lips. “What a beautiful nature you have!”
===Chinese===
「正是！正是！我想起来了，我是要问问她，婚姻的滋味如何。」

「天哪！」这少年嚷道。「你该不会是在为这件事收集资料吧？」

姑娘温顺地、带着一副不怀好意的天真打量他。「你总不至于希望我在黑暗里瞎跳吧？」

「我当然希望。我当然乐意——既然你要跳，也是同我一起跳。」

奥蕾莉亚带着同样促狭的神情舔了舔嘴唇。「你这人性情多美呀！」

===Original===
Pleased at this, the little lord nodded.

“I’ll tell you what matrimony is, two souls with but a single thought﻿—”

“Yes,” Aurelia interrupted. “Two souls with half a thought apiece.” Rapturously she sighed. “There is real bliss!”

Buttercups snarled. “Oh come, now! If you turn everything into ridicule﻿—”

Dreamily Aurelia continued. “I asked the duchess, and she said﻿—”
===Chinese===
这话听得小爵爷直点头。

「我告诉你婚姻是什么：两个灵魂，共有同一个念头——」

「是呀，」奥蕾莉亚打断他，「两个灵魂，一人分得半个念头。」她心醉神迷地叹道。「这才叫真幸福！」

巴特卡普斯咆哮起来。「哎呀，行了！你把什么都变成挖苦——」

奥蕾莉亚恍恍惚惚地继续说。「我问过公爵夫人，她说——」

===Original===
“The old harridan!”

“You know her manner”﻿—a manner which Aurelia instantly made her own. “My dear, matrimony is three months of adoration, three months of introspection, thirty years of toleration﻿—with the children to begin it all over.”

Buttercups frowned. “A rather voluminous definition.”

“Rather luminous, I should call it.”

Frowning still, Buttercups threw out.
===Chinese===
「那老泼妇！」

「你知道她那个做派」——奥蕾莉亚当即把这做派学了个十足。「『亲爱的，婚姻么，就是三个月的倾慕，三个月的自省，三十年的容忍——再添上几个孩子，从头再来一遍。』」

巴特卡普斯皱起眉。「好一个臃肿的定义。」

「要我说，是好一个透亮的定义。」

巴特卡普斯仍旧皱着眉，脱口道：

===Original===
“While you were at it, it’s a pity you did not ask her what love is.”

But the sarcasm, if sarcasm it were, convulsed Aurelia. “Parsnips!” she delightedly exclaimed. “You’ll never believe it! She asked me!”

“Mistook you for an expert,” Buttercups, glowering at the beautiful, laughing girl snapped back. “What did you say?”

Aurelia, her eyes sparkling, her little white teeth visible, her little pink tongue also, looked about her, turned, went to the bench, got up on it and there, solemnly now as though on a platform, coughed.

“I said, that while from studies and statistics I was inclined to believe that, theoretically, love is a fermentation of the molecules of the imagination, actually it is the affection of somebody else.”
===Chinese===
「你既然问到那儿了，可惜没顺便问问她爱情是什么。」

可这句讥诮——如果算得上讥诮的话——笑坏了奥蕾莉亚。「欧防风！」她喜不自胜地嚷道。「你永远猜不到！是她问我来着！」

「把你当成行家了，」巴特卡普斯瞪着这个笑靥如花的姑娘，回敬道。「你怎么说的？」

奥蕾莉亚双眼发亮，露出一口小白牙，还有粉色的小舌尖；她环顾四周，转过身，走向长凳，蹬了上去，站在那里，此刻郑重其事，仿佛站在讲坛上，咳了一声。

「我说，根据研究和统计，我倾向于认为：在理论上，爱情是想象力的分子的一场发酵；而在实际上，它是另有所钟的情感。」

===Original===
Blankly Buttercups stared. “I don’t understand that.”

Aurelia coughed again. “I added that from the same studies and statistics I was also inclined to believe that love is the tragedy of those who lack it, the boredom of those who don’t.”

“Eh?” Buttercups whined. “I don’t understand that either.”

“I further stated that love is a specific emotion, more or less exclusive in selection, more﻿—or less﻿—permanent in duration and due to a mental disturbance, in itself caused by a law of attraction which somebody or other said was the myth of happiness, invented by the devil for man’s despair.”

Helplessly Buttercups groaned. “I don’t understand that at all.”
===Chinese===
巴特卡普斯木然瞪着她。「这个我听不懂。」

奥蕾莉亚又咳了一声。「我还补充说，根据同样的研究和统计，我也倾向于认为：爱情是缺爱的人的悲剧，是不缺的人的无聊。」

「嗯？」巴特卡普斯哀声问。「这个我也听不懂。」

「我进一步指出：爱情是一种特殊的情感，对象的选择多少具有排他性，持续时间或长——或短——起因于心智的一种扰动，而扰动本身又源于一条引力定律；这条定律，用某位什么人的话说，乃是幸福的神话，是魔鬼发明出来让人绝望的。」

巴特卡普斯无可奈何地呻吟。「这个我就完全听不懂了。」

===Original===
With birdlike ease Aurelia hopped from the bench and with consoling delicacy nodded:

“Violet said she didn’t either.”

Buttercups brightened. “Now there’s a woman of sense.”

Very sweetly Aurelia nodded again. “Leilah Barouffska said she did understand, so I may suppose that she is stupid.”
===Chinese===
奥蕾莉亚小鸟似的轻巧一蹦，跳下长凳，带着安慰人的纤巧点了点头：

「维奥莱特说她也不懂。」

巴特卡普斯精神一振。「瞧，这才是个有头脑的女人。」

奥蕾莉亚又甜甜地点头。「蕾拉·巴罗夫斯卡说她懂，所以嘛，我可以断定她蠢。」

===Original===
At the shot﻿—which missed him﻿—Buttercups tormented the tip of his nose.

“No doubt, she does seem to have made a mess of things. Why now did she leave her first husband?”

Aurelia looked down and away.

“It is not a thing I could mention.”

Buttercups gave a little jump. “What?”
===Chinese===
这一枪——没打中他——打得巴特卡普斯直揉鼻尖。

「可不，她那些事确实办得够糟的。她到底为什么离开头一位丈夫？」

奥蕾莉亚垂下眼睛，望向别处。

「那可不是我所能提的事。」

巴特卡普斯小小一惊。「什么？」

===Original===
Perversely, her lovely eyes still lowered, Aurelia added:

“She caught him in the act.”

Buttercups jumped again.

Aurelia blushed or rather appeared to do so. “With her own eyes she saw him eating fish with his knife.”

But Buttercups had rallied. “Now, Aurelia,” he protested, “I have heard too many lies about myself, too many confounded lies, to believe any such story.”
===Chinese===
奥蕾莉亚执拗地、依然低垂着那双可爱的眼睛，又添一句：

「她当场逮住了他。」

巴特卡普斯又惊得一动。

奥蕾莉亚红了脸——确切说，是显出红了脸的样子。「她亲眼看见他用刀子吃鱼。」

可巴特卡普斯已经缓过来了。「我说，奥蕾莉亚，」他抗议道，「关于我自己的谎话我听得太多了，太多混账的谎话，这样的话我是不信的。」

===Original===
Superciliously, her delicate nose in the air, Aurelia looked him over. “Ah, indeed! But then you see sensible people never object to the lies that are told about them. What we do object to is the truth. Now when we are married﻿—if we ever are﻿—”

“Aurelia,” the poor devil pathetically interrupted, “you never say when we are married without adding if we ever are!”

“That’s to teach you not to take things for granted. I have been engaged before﻿—and may be again.”

“B-before!” the flustered Buttercups stuttered. “A-again!”

Frostily this ingénue considered the youth. “Parsnips, don’t look at me in that fashion, you inflame me.”
===Chinese===
奥蕾莉亚傲气地翘起她那秀气的鼻子，把他上上下下打量一遍。「啊，是嘛！不过你要明白，明白人从不计较人家编排自己的谎话。我们计较的偏偏是真话。等咱们结了婚——倘若真结得成的话——」

「奥蕾莉亚，」这可怜虫可怜巴巴地插嘴，「你说『咱们结婚』，从来不肯漏掉一句『倘若真结得成』！」

那是要教你别事事想当然。我从前订过婚——往后保不住还会再订。

「订、订过！」慌了神的巴特卡普斯结巴起来。「再、再订！」

这位天真少女冷冰冰地打量这少年。「欧防风，别那样瞅我，你要把我点着了。」

===Original===
She cocked an ear. “What’s that?”

At the gate the bell was ringing and unperceived by either Emmanuel had reappeared. The footman was descending the garden. Midway he stopped.

“I have the honour to inform mademoiselle that madame la comtesse is momentarily awaited.”

He bowed, moved on, opened the gate through which then a brief procession passed:﻿—Silverstairs, a green bag under his arm; de Fresnoy, a stick under his; an old man with a small valise; finally Verplank.
===Chinese===
她侧耳一听。「那是什么？」

花园门上的铃在响，而埃马纽埃尔已经去而复返，两人都没察觉。男仆正往花园里走。走到半路，他停下。

「小人谨禀小姐：伯爵夫人即刻便到。」

他一躬身，继续往前，打开园门；随即一支短短的队伍鱼贯而入：——西尔弗斯泰尔斯，腋下夹着一只绿袋；德·弗雷努瓦，腋下夹着一根手杖；一个提着小提包的老人；最后是弗普兰克。

===Original===
Verplank, raising his hat, approached Aurelia. De Fresnoy, after saluting the young woman, addressed the old man.

But Silverstairs, sidling up to Buttercups and indicating Aurelia, whispered:

“Get her away, there’s to be a fight.”

“The deuce there is!” Buttercups exclaimed.

For a moment he looked helplessly about and made a little futile gesture. “If I ask her to go she’ll stay.”
===Chinese===
弗普兰克举帽致意，向奥蕾莉亚走去。德·弗雷努瓦向这位少女行过礼，便去招呼那位老人。

西尔弗斯泰尔斯却侧身凑近巴特卡普斯，朝奥蕾莉亚一努嘴，低声道：

「把她带走，这儿要有一场决斗。」

「居然有事！」巴特卡普斯失声道。

他无助地环顾片刻，做了个无用的小手势。「我若请她走，她偏要留下。」

===Original===
Silverstairs pulled at his moustache. “Then tell her to stay and she’ll go.”

But such strategy was needless. Aurelia had no intention of loitering among these men, not one of whom interested her remotely. With a glimpse of her pretty teeth to Verplank and a nod at the others, she passed, followed by Buttercups, through the gate which the footman held open.

Meanwhile the dogs were barking and, from the house beyond, Barouffski appeared. With him were friends of his, Palencia, Tyszkiewicz; also a young man with a serious face who, like the old man, had with him a case which gingerly he put on the ground.

Verplank glanced at them, went to the bench and began removing his coat.

The night before he had dreamed pleasurably, as the great beasts of the jungles dream, of blood and the joy of killing. He had dreamed also and less agreeably that Leilah’s story was true. However he had denied it, he did not know but that it might be. None the less he doubted. He doubted it for the most human of reasons, because he wanted to. He doubted it for another and a better reason, because his intuitions so prompted. He had yet another reason, one less valid perhaps but cogent, the dissimilarity between Leilah and himself. The contrast was so marked that they might have come of alien races, from different zones.
===Chinese===
西尔弗斯泰尔斯捻着髭须。「那就叫她留下，她自会走。」

其实这等计谋全属多余。奥蕾莉亚压根无意在这些男人中间流连——他们说起来没一个让她有半分兴致。她朝弗普兰克露了露那口漂亮的牙齿，又朝其他人点点头，便由巴特卡普斯跟着，从男仆拉开的园门里出去了。

与此同时，狗在吠；从屋子那头，巴罗夫斯基露了面。同他一起的是他的朋友们：帕伦西亚、蒂什凯维奇，还有一个神色严肃的年轻人；同那老人一样，他也带着一只箱子，正小心翼翼地放到地上。

弗普兰克朝他们扫了一眼，走向长凳，动手脱外衣。

前一夜，他做了个快意的梦，像丛林里的巨兽做的那种梦，梦见血，梦见杀戮的快乐。他还做了一个不那么愉快的梦：梦见蕾拉讲的事是真的。任凭他怎样否认，他也不知道那会不会是真的。然而他还是怀疑。他怀疑它，是出于最人性的理由——因为他愿意怀疑。他怀疑它，还有一个更好的理由——因为他的直觉如此怂恿。他还有第三个理由，或许不太站得住，却很有力：蕾拉与他本人之间的悬殊。那反差鲜明到他们仿佛出自异种，来自异带。

===Original===
On leaving her house other differences had occurred to him, differences not physical but moral. It is ridiculous, he had told himself. None the less he dreamed that the story was true.

Meanwhile the parliamentaries had not been entirely successful. The note dispatched from Voisins had resulted that evening in a conference between Verplank’s seconds and Barouffski’s. These latter, Tyszkiewicz and Palencia, had begun by insisting that it was their principal who was aggrieved, that Verplank, in attempting to address a lady whom he knew did not wish to speak to him, had been wholly at fault and was deprived in consequence of the choice of weapons.

To this de Fresnoy had objected that Verplank knew nothing of the kind, that in addressing or in attempting to address the lady, he had acted in accordance with the usages of the world: moreover assuming him to have been in error in thinking that the lady did not object to being addressed, her slightest indication to the contrary would have been super-sufficient to make him desist, the result being that Barouffski’s intervention reflected on his good-breeding and was therefore an insult.

This view of the matter Barouffski’s seconds refused to accept. They represented that it were difficult for the lady to have more punctiliously informed Verplank of her disinclination to be addressed by him than she had already done in obtaining a divorce.

At the easy logic de Fresnoy laughed. According to him, all that was beside the issue. He declared that many divorced couples were better friends afterward than they had found it possible to be before. In support of the statement he cited history: he cited the case of Henri IV and the Reine Margot. He did more than cite, he quoted the chronicles of Pierre l’Estoile, and he insisted that if his view were not accepted the conference must dissolve and an arbiter be convened.
===Chinese===
离开她家的那天，他又想到了别的一些差异——不是体质上的，是心性上的。多荒唐，他那时对自己说。可他还是梦见那件事是真的。

与此同时，两边「议会使节」的斡旋并未全然成功。从瓦赞餐厅（Voisins）送出的那张便条，当晚促成了弗普兰克的副手们与巴罗夫斯基的副手们的一次会谈。后两位，蒂什凯维奇和帕伦西亚，一上来就咬定受屈的一方是他们的委托人：弗普兰克向一位明知不愿搭理他的女士攀谈，错处全在他，因此理应丧失挑选武器的权利。

对此，德·弗雷努瓦反驳说，弗普兰克全然不知此情；他向那位女士致意——或试图致意——是合乎上流社会惯例的；退一万步，就算他误以为女士不介意被攀谈，她只消最轻微地示意相反的意思，便足以让他住口；因此，巴罗夫斯基的插手有损他的教养，故而构成一桩侮辱。

巴罗夫斯基的副手们拒不接受这套说法。他们声言：女士既已办妥离婚，她对「不愿受弗普兰克攀谈」这一点，已不可能表达得比这更一丝不苟了。

听了这套省事的逻辑，德·弗雷努瓦笑了。照他说来，这一切都文不对题。他宣称，多少离异的夫妻事后反成了朋友，比婚前做得到的还要亲。为支持这论点，他援引历史：他援引了亨利四世（Henri IV）与玛戈王后（Reine Margot）的先例。他不止于援引，还朗诵了皮埃尔·莱斯图瓦勒（Pierre l’Estoile）的编年史；并且声言，倘若他的见解不被接受，会谈即告解散，须召集仲裁人。

===Original===
In face of these arguments advanced to provincials by a Parisian, advanced too with that tone of authority which only a man sure of his ground or of his assurance may maintain and advanced, moreover, to men not over sure of their own, the latter hesitated.

Then, as though to demonstrate the truth of the paradox of which de Fresnoy had delivered himself at Voisins, the quip that a man is rarely killed except by his seconds, Silverstairs who, thus far throughout the conference, had smoked in placid silence, suddenly stuck his oar in.

“Why not toss for it?”

Tyszkiewicz and Palencia, hesitating still, agreed. A coin was flipped, heads for Verplank, tails for Barouffski. Tails it was. Barouffski was accorded the choice of arms, foils were designated by his seconds and the meeting was arranged to be held in his garden, at two the next day.
===Chinese===
一个巴黎人向外省人抛出的这一套论证——还带着那份唯有对自家道理十拿九稳的人、或者干脆对一切都有把握的人才拿得出的权威口吻，何况抛给的又是两位对自己肚里的货色并不很有数的人——后两位迟疑了。

这时，仿佛要坐实德·弗雷努瓦在瓦赞餐厅发过的那句怪论——说人很少死于敌手，多半死于自己的副手——迄此在会谈中一直安安静静抽着烟的西尔弗斯泰尔斯，突然横插了一杠子。

「何不掷硬币定夺？」

蒂什凯维奇和帕伦西亚还迟疑着，也就同意了。一枚硬币抛上半空：正面归弗普兰克，反面归巴罗夫斯基。落下来是反面。巴罗夫斯基得了挑兵器的权利，他的副手们指定了花剑，场地定在他家花园，时间定在次日下午两点。

===Original===
Verplank would have preferred pistols. But, informed of the result, he dreamed pleasurably. The encounter was the main thing. Presently sleep sank him deeper. Life and death ceased to be. He became part of the inchoate and primordial. Then, from the voids in which he lay, lightly, delicately, imperceptibly, an artery reached and drew him. But his scattered selves, the objective, subjective, superjective, satisfied with their temporary decentralisation, resisted. In the subtle struggle a memory, catalogued Leilah, was aroused. The syllables of the name resounded remotely, like a damp drum beaten obscurely behind the shelves of thought. They conveyed no meaning and, before they could suggest any, they passed, drifted by the currents of unconsciousness. But at once, those currents, barred by assembling ideas, broke to the murmur of the vocables﻿—Leilah! Leilah! Other memories, incidents, possibilities eddied among them and the sleeper, awakening, found himself confronted by the tragic mystery which the name revived.

Immediately the picture that had formed itself before him at Voisins returned. From it the Why had gone but the obstacle remained, and, as he got from the bed, he promised himself to demolish it.

Now, in the hostile enclosure, as the dogs barked and Barouffski appeared, Verplank removed his coat, undid his collar, rolled up his sleeves.
===Chinese===
弗普兰克本会更喜欢手枪。不过听了这结果，他做起了快意的梦。交手才是正经。不多时，睡眠把他沉得更深。生与死都不复存在。他化入了那混沌未凿的原初之物。随后，从他躺卧的虚空里，一条动脉轻轻地、悄悄地、不知不觉地伸来，把他吸引过去。可他那些四散的自我——客体的、主体的、超主体的——对这暂时的离散状态颇为受用，竟抗拒不从。在这场微妙的拉锯里，一份编目为「蕾拉」的记忆被唤醒了。名字的音节远远地回响，像一面受潮的鼓，在思绪的排架后面闷闷地敲着。它们没有传达出任何意义，还没等提示出什么，就被无意识的潜流漂走了。但顷刻间，那些潜流被聚拢来的念头拦住，碎成那几个字眼的低语——蕾拉！蕾拉！别的记忆、旧事、种种可能打着旋汇入其中，睡着的人醒了过来，发现自己正面对这名字复活的悲剧之谜。

霎时间，那晚在瓦赞餐厅在他面前凝成的画面又回来了。画面上的「为什么」已经褪去，障碍却还在；他一边下床，一边向自己许诺：要把它砸碎。

此刻，在这剑拔弩张的围场里，狗吠着，巴罗夫斯基出现了；弗普兰克脱下外衣，解开领口，卷起袖子。

===Original===
Beside him, bending over a case, the old man mumbled. He was a surgeon. The hour was not to his liking. He believed in duels, they were a source of revenue to him, but he believed in fighting on an empty stomach and, in the afternoon, who had that?

He looked up at Verplank. “Monsieur, you are young, you are brave, I doubt not you are also adroit. But had I been you, when your seconds asked, as I may suppose they asked: ‘Which shall it be, the pistol at twenty paces or the sword?’ I would have said to them: ‘Give me the sword at twenty paces.’ Yes; that is what I would have said.”

Verplank ignored him utterly. In the center of the garden the seconds grouped together, were concluding details. Beyond, near the house, Barouffski stood. He also was now bare-armed. Near him, emptying a case, was the young man with the serious face. Beside him, placed upright against a wall, were two long green bags. From the street came the usual rumble, the noise of motors, the cries of hawkers, the snorting of stallions, the clatter of hoofs.

Silverstairs, abandoning the others went over to Verplank.
===Chinese===
在他身旁，一位老人俯在箱子上咕哝。他是外科医生。这个时辰不合他的意。他信决斗这一套——那是他的一宗进项——但他信的是空着肚子打架，可到了下午，谁还空着肚子？

他抬头望望弗普兰克。「先生，您年轻，您勇敢，我毫不怀疑您也灵巧。可我若是您，当您的副手问起——我料想他们总问过：『二十步手枪，还是剑？』我就会对他们说：『给我二十步开外的剑。』对；我就会这么说。」

弗普兰克全然没理会他。花园中央，双方副手聚在一起，正在敲定细节。稍远处，靠近宅子，站着巴罗夫斯基。他这时也光了臂膀。他身边，那个神色严肃的年轻人正在开箱。他脚边，两只绿色长袋直立靠着墙。街上传来惯常的隆隆声：汽车的噪声，小贩的叫卖，种马的嘶鼾，马蹄的嘚嘚。

西尔弗斯泰尔斯撇下旁人，走到弗普兰克跟前。

===Original===
“De Fresnoy has been chosen director.”

Verplank, from his trousers pocket, had taken a pair of gloves. The palm of one of them, previously moistened, had been dusted with rosin. Now, as he put it on, he looked across at Barouffski who was looking at him. The man’s bare arms were hairy and the sight of them was repugnant to Verplank. At once all the jealousy, all the hate of the male, mounted like wine to his head. He coloured, his hand shook. Then, resolutely, he reacted. In a moment he had again control of himself and it was idly, with an air of indifference, as he finished with his glove, that, in reference to the dinner that evening, he said:

“Are you to have many people tonight?”

Silverstairs, delighted that Verplank, showing up in such form, should be so sure of the result, laughed. “No, it is to be small and early. Afterward we go on to a play. The missis has a box for something, the Gymnase I think.”
===Chinese===
「德·弗雷努瓦被选为裁判。」

弗普兰克从裤袋里掏出一副手套。其中一只的掌心预先抹过水汽，又扑上了松香粉。他一面戴手套，一面望向对面的巴罗夫斯基，对方也正望着他。那人裸露的手臂上毛茸茸的，那样子让弗普兰克心生憎恶。顿时，男性的全部妒意、全部恨意，像酒一样冲上头顶。他涨红了脸，手发起抖来。随即，他断然克制住自己。不多时他又稳住了，于是一边漫不经心地、带着漠然的神情戴好手套，一边就当晚的晚宴问道：

「今晚你们客人多吗？」

西尔弗斯泰尔斯见弗普兰克状态这么好、对结果这么有把握，乐了。「不多，小宴，早散。饭后我们去看戏。内人订了个包厢，看什么来着，我想是吉姆纳斯剧院（the Gymnase）。」

===Original===
Verplank bent over and turned up the ends of his trousers. A moment before he had been considering methods of attack, in particular a direct riposte after a certain parade and it was springingly, as though delivering it, that he straightened.

But now de Fresnoy approached. Silverstairs moved to one side where he was joined by Tyszkiewicz, a thin, tall man with a prominent nose and an air vaguely pedagogic, and by Palencia who, with great black eyebrows that met and a full black beard, looked like Fra Diavolo disguised as a clubman.

From one of the long green bags de Fresnoy had taken a pair of foils. These he offered, hilt foremost, to Verplank who grasped one and then to gauge its temper, or his own, lashed the air with it. The movement revealed a suppleness of arm, a muscular ease, the swelling biceps which training alone provides.

Save Barouffski, no one noticed. For a moment his eyes shifted absently. It was as though he too had meditated a coup and now was meditating another. Meanwhile he also had received a foil.
===Chinese===
弗普兰克俯下身，翻起裤脚。方才他还在琢磨进攻的路数，尤其是一记格挡之后的直刺反还；他弹身站直，仿佛正把这一刺递出去。

可这时德·弗雷努瓦走过来了。西尔弗斯泰尔斯让到一旁，蒂什凯维奇——一个高瘦个子、鹰钩鼻、隐约带几分学究气的人——和帕伦西亚也聚了过来；后者两道浓黑的眉毛连成一线，蓄着一部黑胡子，活像扮成俱乐部绅士的弗拉·迪亚沃洛（Fra Diavolo，传说中的义大利大盗）。

德·弗雷努瓦从一只绿长袋里取出一对花剑，剑柄朝前，递给弗普兰克。弗普兰克握住一支，为试剑性——或试自己的腕性——朝空气抽了一鞭。这动作显出臂膀的柔韧、肌肉的松快，那二头肌的隆起，唯有锻炼才能养成。

除了巴罗夫斯基，谁也没有留意。他的目光一时出神地移了移，仿佛他也盘算过一记妙招，此刻又在盘算另一记。与此同时，他也接过了一支花剑。

===Original===
“Messieurs!” de Fresnoy called. He spoke in a loud, clear voice. He had moved back and stood at an angle to Barouffski and Verplank. Opposite, at an equal angle were the seconds and surgeons. All now were so stationed that they formed a sort of cross.

“Messieurs, I do not need to remind you of the common loyalty to be observed. What I have to say is that the encounter will proceed in engagements of three minutes, followed each by three minutes of repose, until one of you is incapacitated.”

De Fresnoy looked from Barouffski to Verplank. At once in his loud, clear voice he called:

“On guard!”
===Chinese===
「诸位先生！」德·弗雷努瓦喊道。他嗓音洪亮清脆。他已退后，站在与巴罗夫斯基和弗普兰克成一角的位置。对面，成同样角度站着副手们和外科医生们。此刻各人站定的位置，恰好连成一个十字。

「诸位先生，双方应守的共同信义，无须我提醒。我要说的是：交锋以三分钟为一合，每合之后休息三分钟，直到两位中有一人不能再战为止。」

德·弗雷努瓦的目光从巴罗夫斯基移到弗普兰克身上，随即以他洪亮清脆的嗓音喊道：

「预备！」

===Original===
The two men fell into position. De Fresnoy moved forward, took in either hand the foils at the points, drew them together until they met, left them so and moved back.

“*Allez*, messieurs!”

At the word “*Allez*,” or, in English, “go,” and without waiting for the term “Messieurs” that followed, instantly Barouffski lunged. His foil pierced Verplank in the cheek and touched the upper jaw.

Verplank had a vision of a footman peering from a window, a taste of something hot and acrid in his mouth, a sense of pain, the sensation of vulperine fury.
===Chinese===
两人摆开架势。德·弗雷努瓦上前，两手各执两剑的剑尖，将它们拉到一处相触，便那样放着，退了回来。

「*Allez*（法语：开始），诸位先生！」

只听那声「*Allez*」——换作英文便是「go（开始）」——不等后面的「诸位先生」出口，巴罗夫斯基立刻突刺。剑尖刺中弗普兰克的面颊，抵到了上颚。

弗普兰克眼前闪过一个从窗口窥视的男仆，嘴里泛起一股火辣辣的苦味，一阵痛感，一阵狐狸似的狂怒。

===Original===
De Fresnoy’s face had grown red as his neckcloth. He brandished his stick.

“Monsieur!” he cried at Barouffski. “Your conduct is odious. You shall answer to me for it.”

Barouffski bowed. “For the expression which it has pleased you to employ, you shall answer to me.”

“Permit me! Permit me!” Tyszkiewicz interjected. “To what do you object?”
===Chinese===
德·弗雷努瓦的脸涨得同他的领巾一样红。他挥起手杖。

「先生！」他冲巴罗夫斯基喝道。「您的行径卑劣之至。您要为此向我交代。」

巴罗夫斯基一躬身。「您方才赏脸用的那个字眼，您才要向我交代。」

「对不起！对不起！」蒂什凯维奇插了进来。「您指的是哪一点？」

===Original===
Angrily de Fresnoy turned at him. “Your principal drew before the order. He﻿—”

“Permit me! Permit me!” Tyszkiewicz interrupted. “The word ‘*Allez*’ is an order. The moment it is uttered hostilities begin. The term ‘Messieurs’ is but a polite accessory, a term which may or may not be employed.”

Insolently de Fresnoy considered him. “I have no lessons to receive from you.”

“Permit me! Permit me﻿—”
===Chinese===
德·弗雷努瓦恼怒地转向他。「您的委托人抢在号令之前出手。他——」

「对不起！对不起！」蒂什凯维奇打断他。「『*Allez*』这个词就是号令。它一出口，交手即告开始。『诸位先生』不过是句客气的缀饰，用不用皆可。」

德·弗雷努瓦倨傲地打量他。「我可没有要领您的教。」

「对不起！对不起——」

===Original===
But de Fresnoy had turned on his heel. Before him Verplank stood, Silverstairs on one side, the old surgeon on the other. The young surgeon had joined them. Beyond, Barouffski was examining the point of his foil.

From Verplank’s mouth and face blood was running. The wound had not improved his appearance. The old surgeon, on tiptoes, was staunching it.

“What I like,” he confided, speaking the while very unctuously as though what he was saying would be a comfort to Verplank; “what I like is to attend to gentlemen whose wives have deceived them. Outraged husbands, monsieur, that is my specialty!”

Verplank brushed him aside, shook his foil, and called at de Fresnoy.

“Are the three minutes up?”
===Chinese===
可德·弗雷努瓦已转过脚跟。他面前站着弗普兰克，西尔弗斯泰尔斯在一边，老外科医生在另一边。年轻的外科医生也加入了他们。稍远处，巴罗夫斯基正在验看剑尖。

弗普兰克的嘴上、脸上淌着血。这伤口可没有让他的模样好看半分。老外科医生踮着脚，在为他止血。

「我喜欢伺候的，」他推心置腹地说，口气油滑得很，仿佛他说的这番话对弗普兰克是个安慰，「我喜欢伺候的，就是老婆偷了人的绅士们。戴绿帽的丈夫，先生，那是我的专长！」

弗普兰克把他一把推开，抖了抖剑，朝德·弗雷努瓦喊道：

「三分钟到了没有？」

===Original===
“Monsieur!” the old surgeon protested.

The young surgeon intervened.

“But, monsieur﻿—”

De Fresnoy motioned at them.

“Is he in a condition to continue?”
===Chinese===
「先生！」老外科医生抗议。

年轻的外科医生出来打圆场。

「可是，先生——」

德·弗雷努瓦朝他们摆了摆手。

「他还续得了吗？」

===Original===
“Why not?” Verplank scornfully replied.

He raised his left hand, and, with a gesture of excuse, turned and spat. He looked up. His mouth was on fire, his jaw burned, the wound in his cheek was a flame. Yet these things but added to the intensity of his eyes. They blazed. There was blood on his face, on his chin, on his shirt, on his feet. He was hideous. But he was a man, and a mad one.

“He ought to be horsewhipped,” muttered Silverstairs, glaring as he spoke at Barouffski, who was talking to his seconds.

“On guard, then!” called de Fresnoy.

“Permit me, permit me,” cried Tyszkiewicz. “The point of my principal’s sword is broken.”
===Chinese===
「怎么续不得？」弗普兰克轻蔑地答道。

他抬起左手，做了个告罪的手势，转过身吐掉一口血。他抬头挺立。他的嘴烧着，他的颚灼着，颊上的伤口是一团火。可这些只让他眼里的光更烈。那双眼灼灼燃烧。他脸上有血，下巴上有血，衬衫上、脚上都有。他样子骇人。但他是个男人，而且是个疯子。

「真该拿马鞭抽他一顿，」西尔弗斯泰尔斯低声嘀咕，眼睛瞪着正在同副手们说话的巴罗夫斯基。

「那么，预备！」德·弗雷努瓦喊道。

「对不起，对不起，」蒂什凯维奇叫道。「我委托人的剑尖断了。」

===Original===
“Give him another then,” de Fresnoy roughly threw out. Insolently he added: “And teach him how to use it.” In a moment, when from the other bag, a foil had been got and measured, “On guard,” he repeated.

Again he united the foils. Again he gave the command.

For a moment the weapons clashed.

Suddenly and excitedly Palencia cried: “My principal is touched.”
===Chinese===
「那就再给他一支，」德·弗雷努瓦粗声粗气地抛下一句，又倨傲地补一句：「再教教他怎么使。」不一会儿，从另一只袋子里取出一支花剑、量过之后，「预备，」他又喊。

他再把两剑并到一处，再发出号令。

兵刃相交了一阵。

突然，帕伦西亚激动地喊道：「我的委托人中剑了。」

===Original===
“Halt,” de Fresnoy, intervening with raised stick, commanded.

Verplank moved back. “Damn him,” he muttered, “I haven’t done with him yet.”

About Barouffski now, Palencia and the young man with the serious face had come. The latter was examining Barouffski’s right arm. On it a thin red line was visible. Very gravely the young man looked up.

“My client is disabled. Profound incision in the region of the *flexor digitorum sublimis* accompanied by a notable effusion of blood.”
===Chinese===
「停，」德·弗雷努瓦举着手杖插入两人中间，喝令。

弗普兰克退后一步。「该死的，」他咕哝道，「我还没跟他完呢。」

这时帕伦西亚和那位神色严肃的年轻人围到巴罗夫斯基身边。后者正在查验巴罗夫斯基的右臂。臂上现出一道细细的红线。年轻人极为凝重地抬起头。

「我的当事人不能再战。*Flexor digitorum sublimis*（指浅屈肌）区域深度切创，伴有显著溢血。」

===Original===
The old surgeon chuckled. Confidentially as before he addressed Verplank. “I know that term. It means a scratch. Those ladies there, it must amuse even them.”

As he spoke he indicated a window at which Violet and Leilah had appeared, but from which now Leilah was retreating.

Verplank did not hear, did not see. The young surgeon, resuming, had announced himself as opposed to a continuation of the encounter. It was this that preoccupied Verplank.

Loudly and angrily he cried: “Let’s have pistols then. That man can use his left hand and I’ll do the same.”
===Chinese===
老外科医生呵呵一笑。他又像方才那样，推心置腹地对弗普兰克说：「这个词我认得。意思是擦破点皮。那边那几位太太，看了保管也乐。」

说着他朝一扇窗户一指：维奥莱特和蕾拉方才出现在那里，只是蕾拉此刻正从窗边退走。

弗普兰克没有听见，也没有看见。年轻的外科医生接着宣布，他反对继续交手。这才是在弗普兰克心头盘踞的事。

他又响又怒地喊道：「那就用手枪。那人可以用左手，我也照样。」

===Original===
“*Cristi! La jolie dame!*” the old surgeon muttered to himself.

In the doorway Leilah had come. Hurriedly she moved to Verplank. As she did so Barouffski tried to prevent her.

“*Cara mia*, I must beg of you﻿—”

He had got in her way but she eluded him, while the other men looked curiously at this woman who now agitatedly was addressing Verplank.
===Chinese===
「*Cristi! La jolie dame!*（法语：天哪，好一位俏丽的夫人！）」老外科医生自言自语地咕哝。

门口出现了蕾拉的身影。她急急奔向弗普兰克。她一来，巴罗夫斯基便想拦住她。

「*Cara mia*，我求求你——」

他横在她面前，她却绕开了他；旁的男人们好奇地望着这个女人，此刻她正激动地对弗普兰克说话。

===Original===
“Don’t fight any more, don’t!”

Roughly Verplank answered: “I haven’t begun.”

“Sir,” cried Barouffski. “I can permit no conversation with this lady.”

Verplank ignoring Barouffski as utterly as he had ignored the surgeon, looked at Leilah.

“That story of yours is﻿—”
===Chinese===
「别再打下去了，别打了！」

弗普兰克粗暴地答道：「我还没开始呢。」

「先生，」巴罗夫斯基喝道，「我不能容许任何人与这位夫人交谈。」

弗普兰克把巴罗夫斯基无视得同无视那外科医生一样彻底，只望着蕾拉。

「你说的那件事——」

===Original===
But whatever he may have intended to say, Barouffski interrupted. He was shouting at Verplank, calling, too, at Leilah whom he had got by the arm and whom he would have drawn away, but this Verplank prevented. Shifting his foil to his left hand, with his right he seized Barouffski and with a twist which separated him from Leilah, shoved him aside.

“To your shambles!” he called at him.

But already the others were intervening. Tyszkiewicz with his eternal “Permit me,” got between the two men. Palencia held Barouffski by the shoulder. Silverstairs drew Verplank away, while de Fresnoy, viewing the situation as hopeless, declared the duel at an end.

The actions of all were practically so simultaneous that they were as one to Leilah who, bewildered by the confusion which she herself had caused, horrified by Verplank’s appearance and tortured by the riddle of his interrupted words, now, over the heads of the others, again called to him:
===Chinese===
可无论他想说什么，巴罗夫斯基都打断了。他冲弗普兰克吼叫，也朝蕾拉叫嚷；他已抓住她的胳膊想把她拽走，弗普兰克却不许。他把剑换到左手，右手抓住巴罗夫斯基，一拧，把他从蕾拉身边撕开，掼到一边。

「回你的屠宰场去！」他冲他喊。

可旁人已经赶来劝解。蒂什凯维奇带着他那句永恒的「对不起」挤到两人中间。帕伦西亚抓住巴罗夫斯基的肩。西尔弗斯泰尔斯把弗普兰克拉开；德·弗雷努瓦眼见局面无可挽回，宣布决斗结束。

众人的举动几乎同时在眨眼间完成，在蕾拉眼里恍如一个整体；她被自己惹起的这场混乱弄得晕头转向，被弗普兰克的样子吓得魂飞魄散，又被他那半截被打断的话苦苦折磨，此刻越过众人的头顶，又朝他喊：

===Original===
“You say that the story is﻿—”

“At five!” Verplank threw back.

Barouffski, bursting with rage and impotence, shouted:

“I say this conversation must cease.”

The old surgeon, nudging his colleague, laughed:

“There is my specialty!”
===Chinese===
「你说那件事——」

「五点！」弗普兰克抛回一句。

巴罗夫斯基怒火中烧却又无可发作，吼道：

「我说，这场谈话必须停止。」

老外科医生用胳膊肘碰碰同事，笑道：

「瞧，我的专长来了！」

===Original===
Both surgeons then were occupied with their bags. De Fresnoy, overhearing the remark, could not but smile. To conceal it he turned to the gate where he was joined by Silverstairs and where at once, Palencia and Tyszkiewicz followed, leaving in the center of the garden Leilah and her two husbands, one of whom with a shrug which for an American was perhaps rather French, went to the bench where his coat lay.

In this instance again the actions of all so closely coincided that barely an instant intervened before Leilah was throwing after Verplank the two syllables he had thrown at her.

“At five!”

Later she was unconscious of having done so. But Barouffski heard and presently, when the others had gone and in this garden those two were alone, with angry suspicion he confronted her.
===Chinese===
两位外科医生随即都忙起各自的提包。德·弗雷努瓦无意间听见这话，也不由得一笑。为掩住笑，他转身朝园门走去，西尔弗斯泰尔斯跟了上去；帕伦西亚和蒂什凯维奇也随即跟上，把蕾拉和她的两位丈夫留在花园中央；其中一位耸了耸肩——这姿势对美国人而言或许相当法国——径自走向放着他外衣的长凳。

这一次，众人的举动又是衔接得那么紧，前后不过一瞬，蕾拉已朝着弗普兰克的背影，把他抛给她的那两个字又抛了回去。

「五点！」

事后她对这一节毫无记忆。可巴罗夫斯基听见了；不多时，待旁人散尽，花园里只剩他们两个，他满腹狐疑地逼视她。

===Original===
“Five! What is that? What does it mean?”

Leilah had turned to go. A bit unsteadily she moved on, reached the entrance, leaned there for support.

But Barouffski was at her heels.

“Five,” he repeated. “He said it, you said it, what does it mean?”
===Chinese===
「五点！那是什么？什么意思？」

蕾拉已转身要走。她脚步微晃地往前挪，走到大门口，倚着门框支撑身体。

可巴罗夫斯基紧随其后。

「五点，」他又说。「他说了，你也说了，什么意思？」

===Original===
“It means that God willing some day I may have peace.”

She had half-turned. She turned again. In a moment she had gone.

Menacingly Barouffski’s eyes followed her. “That’s what five means, does it?”

Then he too turned. Nearby, on the marble chair, were his coat and waistcoat. Slowly, thoughtfully, he put them on. As he did so he noticed the dogs. It may have seemed to him then that they were his only friends. Longly he looked their way.
===Chinese===
「意思是：若蒙上帝垂怜，有朝一日我或许能得安宁。」

她半转过身，又转了回去。转眼间她已走了。

巴罗夫斯基的目光威胁着送她远去。「原来五点是这个意思，是么？」

随后他也转过身。近旁的大理石椅上放着他的外衣和马甲。他慢慢地、若有所思地穿上。穿的时候他看见了那几条狗。此刻在他眼里，它们多半是他仅剩的朋友了。他久久地朝它们望去。

===Original===
Suddenly, as though illumination had come, he touched a bell and looked up at Leilah’s window.

After a brief delay, Emmanuel appeared.

“Shut the gate,” Barouffski ordered.

“Perfectly, monsieur le comte,” the footman very deferentially replied and started to do as bidden.
===Chinese===
突然，仿佛灵光乍现，他按了按铃，抬头望向蕾拉的窗口。

稍过片刻，埃马纽埃尔来了。

「关上园门，」巴罗夫斯基吩咐。

「遵命，伯爵先生，」男仆必恭必敬地应道，转身要去照办。

===Original===
Barouffski checked him. Indicating the lower window, he added:

“At five or thereabouts be in there. I will tell you then what to do. You hear me?”

“Perfectly, monsieur le comte.”

Again Barouffski glanced at the upper window. As he glanced he smiled.
===Chinese===
巴罗夫斯基拦住他，指了指楼下的窗，又添一句：

「五点前后，你到那屋里候着。到时我告诉你做什么。听见没有？」

「遵命，伯爵先生。」

巴罗夫斯基又瞟了一眼楼上的窗。瞟的时候他笑了。

===Original===
“*Cara mia*, five may mean more things than you say, more even than you think.”

He was still smiling but it was not a pleasant smile to see.

Beneath his breath, Emmanuel, who was looking at him, muttered:

“*Quelle gueule de maquereau!*”
===Chinese===
「*Cara mia*，五点的意思，可以比你说出来的多，甚至比你想得到的还多。」

他还在笑着，只是那笑容看上去并不叫人愉快。

埃马纽埃尔望着他，在呼吸底下咕哝了一句：

「*Quelle gueule de maquereau!*（法语：好一张龟公的嘴脸！）」

## X / 十

===Original===
“Gracious!” Violet exclaimed. She had been smoking and now in putting a cigarette in a *cendrier* she had succeeded in overturning it. Undismayed she looked at a clock. “Gracious!” she repeated. “Since that stupid duel, I have sat here an hour.”

Leisurely the lady arose. She was a glowing object in this room which, filled with costly futilities and furnished in canary and black, otherwise was Empire and brilliant. The main entrance, hung with heavy portières of yellow damask had, opposite it, across the room, a tapestry panel which masked a spiral stairway that led below. To one side, at an elaborate table, which now the overturned *cendrier* had strewn with ashes, Leilah was seated. Behind her, through an open window, shone the eager sun. Before her, rising from a sofa was her friend.

Leilah wished that she would go, wished too that she would stay, wished rather﻿—as at times we all wish of those who are near us﻿—that she were different, less mondaine perhaps, more simple. To Violet, the spectacle in the garden had been tedious. To Leilah it was horrible. Moreover the atmosphere of blood and hate, the enigma of Verplank’s words, the menaces of Barouffski’s eyes, these things frightened her, inducing a dread which seemed to brood not in the mind but in the body. She could have put a hand to her girdle and have said: “It is here.” In addition she felt﻿—as in every spiritual crisis we all do﻿—alone. Of this she could not tell Violet. She felt that she lacked the power to express it and that Violet lacked the ability to understand. Pain has accents which only its graduates know. Violet, in all her brilliant life, had never shed a poignant tear.

“What do you propose to do now?” the lady was asking.
===Chinese===
「天哪！」维奥莱特失声道。她方才一直在抽烟，这会儿往 *cendrier*（法语：烟灰缸）里搁一支香烟时，竟把缸给掀翻了。她毫不慌乱，看了看钟。「天哪！」她又说了一遍。「自打那场愚蠢的决斗，我在这儿坐了一个钟头了。」

这位女士慢悠悠地站起身。在这间屋子里，她是一团灼灼的亮色；屋里满是昂贵的无用之物，家具以鹅黄与墨黑为调，余下的则是帝政式（Empire）的气派与辉煌。正门悬着黄色锦缎的厚重门帷，正对面、隔着屋子，是一幅挂毯壁板，遮着一道通往楼下的螺旋楼梯。一侧的考究桌旁——桌上这时正散着被掀翻的烟灰缸泼出的灰——坐着蕾拉。她身后，一扇敞开的窗外，闪耀着热切的太阳。她面前，从沙发里立起来的，是她的朋友。

蕾拉巴不得她走，又巴不得她留，更巴不得——正如我们有时对亲近之人所巴望的那样——她换个样子：少些交际场气，多些简单。花园里那一幕，在维奥莱特看来乏味得很，在蕾拉却是恐怖。何况血与恨的空气，弗普兰克话里的谜，巴罗夫斯基眼里的威胁，这些都吓着她，惹起一种似乎不栖在脑中、而栖在身体里的惧怕。她简直可以伸手按住腰带，说一声：「它就在这儿。」此外她还感到——每逢精神的危机，我们人人都如此——孤独。这一点她无法告诉维奥莱特。她觉得自己没有力量表达，维奥莱特也没有能力领会。痛苦自有只发给毕业生的口令。维奥莱特在她花团锦簇的一生里，从没有掉过一滴锥心的眼泪。

「如今你打算怎么办？」这位女士正问着。

===Original===
Cheerlessly Leilah replied: “My duty.”

Here was something which Violet did understand. Brightly she nodded.

“Yes, and I may tell you that it is your duty to preserve your looks and avoid a scandal. I did not at all like your fantasia in the garden. A gentlewoman never does anything important and that was an important thing. In no time it will be all over the place. You can believe that, can’t you?”

“I don’t know what to believe.”

Violet laughed. “I always believe what I like. That I find so satisfactory. Apropos. What was that story about which Verplank was shouting? Mercy! I could have heard him a mile away.”
===Chinese===
蕾拉没精打采地答：「尽我的本分。」

这倒是维奥莱特懂得的。她欢快地点头。

「正是。而且我可以告诉你，保住你的容貌、避开一场丑闻，就是你的本分。你在花园里那出即兴戏我一点也不喜欢。一个大家闺秀从不做要紧的事，而那偏偏是件要紧的事。转眼之间它就会传得满城风雨。这个你总信得过吧？」

「我都不知道该信什么了。」

维奥莱特笑了。「我一向只信我乐意信的。我发觉这法子再称心不过。对了，弗普兰克嚷嚷的那个『那件事』到底是什么？天哪！隔一英里地我都听得见他。」

===Original===
In weary protest Leilah shook her head. “You know I can’t tell you.”

“The same old thing, was it? But how antiquated you are! Really it is piteous. There are no secrets anymore. All that sort of thing went out with hoopskirts. Private life which used to be a sealed book has become an open newspaper. It is plain for instance, plain to everybody, plain as a pikestaff that you are still in love with Verplank. What you left him for the Lord in his infinite wisdom and mercy only knows. That is a secret certainly but only because you choose to make it one. It is no secret to me though that you are dying to go back to him. But don’t you know you can’t? Don’t you know it? Don’t you know that you can’t budge an inch until you have shipped Barouffski? Now how are you going to do that? Tell me.”

Leilah made a pass with a hand. It was as though, in some rite known but to her, she were consulting the lap of the invisible gods and, in it, the equally invisible future.

“Then I will tell you. You have got to buy him off. Listen to this. I will pack Silverstairs straight to the Embassy. There he will get all the law and most of the prophets. Meanwhile promise that you’ll keep your head.”

“I will try,” Leilah, mechanically, her thoughts afar, replied.
===Chinese===
蕾拉疲惫地摇着头抗议。「你知道我不能告诉你。」

「还是那老一套，是吧？你可真是老派得可以！说实在的，可怜见的。如今没有什么秘密了。那一套全跟着钢箍裙一起过时了。私生活从前是一部上了封的书，如今成了一张摊开的报纸。比方说吧，明摆着的，人人眼里明摆着，你分明还爱着弗普兰克。至于你为什么离开他，只有那以无穷智慧与慈悲行事的上帝知道了。那当然是个秘密，可不过因为你自己非要它成为秘密。不过你恨不得回到他身边去，这对我可不是秘密。可你难道不知道办不到吗？你不知道吗？你难道不知道，不先把巴罗夫斯基打发掉，你就寸步难动？那么你打算怎么打发？告诉我。」

蕾拉一只手虚划了一道。仿佛在一场只她一人知晓的仪式里，向无形诸神的膝头求问，并在其中求问同样无形的将来。

「那就我来说给你听。你得把他买出来。听好。我这就把西尔弗斯泰尔斯打发到大使馆去。他会在那儿捞到全部的律法、大半的先知。这期间你得答应我，把稳你的头脑。」

「我尽量，」蕾拉答道，机械地，心思在很远的地方。

===Original===
“There!” Violet exclaimed. “That’s right. When there is a divorce in the air it’s so much better to try than to be tried.”

At the inane jest she laughed, embraced her friend. In a moment she had gone, distributing as she went a faint, sweet smell of orris.

Leilah who had risen moved to the window and looked out at the gate through which Verplank would come. It was as she had said: She did not know what to believe and mutely for a moment she prayed for guidance.

“O, Lords of Karma, Watchers of the Seven Spheres, grant me so to live that, hereafter, I may say, I have harmed no heart, I have made no one weep. Out of your infinite bounty grant that somewhere, sometime, there may be peace to Gulian’s soul and mine.”

The prayer concluded she felt securer. Momentarily the cancer of anxiety had ceased to gnaw, the fascination of fear had departed. In the respite she turned to the clock. It was nearly five and she rang for one of her women.
===Chinese===
「这就对了！」维奥莱特嚷道。「乖孩子。空气里一有了离婚的味道，试人总比受试强得多。」

听了这句无聊的俏皮话，她笑起来，抱了抱她的朋友，转眼间人已走了，一路洒下一缕淡淡的鸢尾甜香。

蕾拉站起身，走到窗前，望向那扇园门——弗普兰克要从那里进来。正如她方才所说：她不知道该信什么；她默然片刻，祈求指引。

「哦，业之诸尊，七域的守望者们，赐我如此度日：使我来日能说，我不曾伤过一颗心，我不曾令一人哭泣。以你们无尽的恩慈，赐下安宁——在某处，在某时，给格里安的灵魂，也给我的。」

祷告完毕，她安稳了些。焦虑的毒瘤一时停止了啃噬，恐惧的魅惑也退去了。趁这喘息，她转向时钟。快五点了，她摇铃唤一个女仆。

===Original===
“Parker,” she began, when the servant appeared.

“Yes, my lady.”

“Presently, in a few minutes, a gentleman will come by the gate. Be there and bring him here. Bring him through the dining room and up the back way. If possible, I prefer that no one should see him.”

“Yes, my lady. Thank your ladyship.”

At once, with that air which those acquire who attended to delicate matters, the woman drew aside the tapestry that masked the stair, which then discreetly, almost atiptoe, she descended.
===Chinese===
「帕克（Parker），」仆人来了，她开口道。

「是，夫人。」

「一会儿，几分钟内，有位先生会从花园门来。你在那儿候着，把他带到这里来。带他穿过饭厅，走后面的楼梯。可能的话，我不愿让任何人看见他。」

「是，夫人。谢夫人。」

那女仆当即显出一副办惯细密事务的人才有的神气，拉开遮楼梯的挂毯，几乎是踮着脚尖、蹑手蹑脚地下去了。

===Original===
As the tapestry fell again, instantly there returned to Leilah the sense of evil and impending ill. The brilliant room seemed full of terrors. In each bright corner a danger lurked. So strong was the impression that she felt it must be she was being warned, that she was being visited by those obscure phenomena which occultists call impacts from the astral, and that these were urging her to go, to meet Verplank without, in the garden, in the street, anywhere except in this fastidiousness.

Coerced by the impression, she entered an adjoining room, got there a fichu which she put on her head, a light wrap which she drew about her. Excited as she was, unaided as well, it took several minutes before she could find these accouterments. When, at last equipped, she reentered the sitting room, she started.

Before her, his hat on, one side of his face medallioned with courtplaster, stood Verplank.

At sight of Leilah, he removed his hat which he tossed on the sofa and said at once and simply enough:
===Chinese===
挂毯重新落下的一瞬，邪恶与祸事将临之感立刻回到蕾拉身上。这间辉煌的屋子似乎满是恐怖。每个明亮的角落里都潜伏着一个危险。这印象强到这般地步，她觉得必是有人正在警醒她，她正被那些幽微的现象造访——通灵者所谓的来自星光界（the astral）的冲击——而这些正催她出去，到外面去会弗普兰克，在花园里，在街上，哪里都好，唯独别在这座精致讲究的屋子里。

被这印象驱使着，她走进隔壁房间，取来一条三角披巾（fichu）罩在头上，又拉过一件薄外套裹在身上。她心绪激荡，又无人帮手，好几分钟才找齐这些行头。待终于穿戴停当、回到客厅时，她吓了一跳。

她面前站着弗普兰克，帽子戴在头上，半边脸贴着膏药，像嵌了一枚圆徽章。

一看见蕾拉，他脱下帽子往沙发上一丢，开门见山、再直白不过地说：

===Original===
“That story of yours is false as Judas.”

“Gulian!” At the moment it was all she found, but then fancy a blind man dazzled.

Verplank nodded. “Yes. The letters you received at Coronado﻿—there were three of them, were there not? three written on gray paper each signed Effingham Verplank?﻿—well, my father wrote them, that’s true enough, but he wrote them to your aunt, your mother’s sister, Hilda Hemingway. Did you never hear that the governor had an affair with her? Did you?”

Leilah’s face spoke for her. From the bewilderment there, it was obvious that of it all she was ignorant.

With an uplift of the chin Verplank considered her.
===Chinese===
「你那件事，假得像犹大。」

「格里安！」此刻她只找得出这一个词——可你也体谅体谅骤然被强光晃了眼的人吧。

弗普兰克点头。「是假。你在科罗纳多收到的那几封信——一共三封，是不是？三封灰纸的信，每封的签名都是埃芬厄姆·弗普兰克？——嗯，信是我父亲写的，这千真万确；可他是写给你姨母的，你母亲的姐姐，希尔达·海明威（Hilda Hemingway）。老爷子同她有过一档子事，这个你从没听说过吗？有没有？」

蕾拉的脸替她答了。从那张脸上的迷惘看得出，这一切她全然不知。

弗普兰克扬着下巴打量她。

===Original===
“That’s odd, girls generally only hear what they ought not to. However, Hemingway became suspicious﻿—for very good reasons, no doubt, or, if you prefer, for very bad ones﻿—the result being that his wife turned the letters over for safekeeping to your mother. When she died your father found them. He did not stop there, he showed them to my mother. My mother knew the facts, but she said your father was so convinced of your mother’s infidelity that it seemed a pity to disabuse him. Those were her very words to me today.”

“Gulian!” Still Leilah found but that. Visibly the light was there. As yet she could not credit it.

Again, but now appreciatively, Verplank nodded. “Yes. I know. It does seem queer. But then my mother is not the ordinary woman. She thought the governor so created to conquer that it no more occurred to her to sit in judgment on his victims than it occurred to her to sit on him. In the true spirit of Christian charity she overlooked it all.”

Verplank paused, opened and closed a hand. “It was not matrimony perhaps, but it was magnificent,” he obliging resumed, forgetting wholly that it was not in him to do likewise.

“Come,” he added. From the start, Leilah’s apparel, the fichu and wrap, had made him fancy that she was as ready to go as he was to take her and it all seemed very simple. “Come. Let’s be off. I have a cab for you.”
===Chinese===
「那就怪了，姑娘家素来只听得进那些不该听的。总之，海明威起了疑心——无疑缘由极正当，或者，随你怎么说，缘由极不正当——结果是他的太太把那些信转交给你母亲保管。你母亲去世后，你父亲发现了它们。他还不止于此，还拿给我母亲看了。我母亲知道底细，可她说，你父亲对你母亲的不忠信得那么死，当面戳破他未免可惜。这是她今天亲口对我说的原话。」

「格里安！」蕾拉仍旧只找得出这个词。光分明已经在那里了。只是一时她还不能信。

弗普兰克又点了点头，这一回带着嘉许。「是。我知道。是够怪的。可我母亲不是寻常女人。在她看来，老爷子生来就是为了征服的，她做梦也不会想去评判他的牺牲品，正如她做梦也不会想去评判他本人。本着基督徒慈爱的真精神，她把这一切都宽宥了。」

弗普兰克顿了顿，一只手张开又合拢。「那也许算不得婚姻，可是够壮观的，」他和气地接着说，全然忘了自己并无本事照此办理。

「来吧，」他又说。打从一开始，蕾拉的装束——披巾与外套——就让他以为，她同他想接她走一样随时可以动身，而这一切看上去又那么简单。「来吧。咱们走。我给你雇了车。」

===Original===
But at the suggestion which was a command she undid the lace, loosened the cloak.

“Gulian, I cannot.”

“Cannot!” he angrily repeated. “Why can’t you? Have you not heard what I said. You are not my sister, you are my wife. Come.”

“Gulian! You do not know what you ask!”

“I know perfectly well. If you hesitate it is because you do not believe me. But would I urge you if that malignity were true? Would I?”
===Chinese===
可对这个实为命令的建议，她解开了蕾丝，松开了外套。

「格里安，我不能。」

「不能！」他愤然重复。「为什么不能？我说的你没听见吗？你不是我妹妹，你是我的妻子。来。」

「格里安！你不知道你在要求什么！」

「我知道得一清二楚。你要是迟疑，那是因为你不信我。可那件事若是真的，那种恶毒若是真的，我还会这样催你吗？会吗？」

===Original===
“Gulian, no, you would not.”

“There! You see! You have to believe me.”

“It is not that.”

“It is the divorce then! But you are no more married to that dismal cad than I am to one of your maids. Except in Nevada the decree has no effect whatever. But without bothering to have it set aside, come with me and let this saurian get another.”

“Gulian, yes, but for the moment surely you can see that this is impossible.”
===Chinese===
「格里安，不会，你不会的。」

「这就对了！你瞧！你不得不信我。」

「不是那个缘故。」

「那就是为离婚！可你同那具阴沉的活尸的婚姻，不见得比我同你的哪个女仆更实在。那份判决出了内华达便毫无效力。不过也不必费事去撤它，跟我走，让这条蜥蜴另娶一个去。」

「格里安，是，可眼下你总看得出，这不可能。」

===Original===
“Impossible! There is nothing impossible. Why do you say so? Why do you make so many objections? You should not make any. You hear a cock-and-bull story, take it for gospel, run away, get a divorce, marry a damned scoundrel and, when you find the story is a brutal lie, stick like a leech to him.”

“Gulian, if you but knew. My position is horrible﻿—”

She wanted to tell him that she was in a prison replete with tortures, one from which she was as eager to go as he was to take her, yet one from which she could not escape through the open door of sinning.

He gave her no time. Instantly he interrupted her.

“I know your position, it is all of your own making, too. By God, you can make up your mind to one thing. You’ll come, if I have to take you.”
===Chinese===
「不可能！天下没有不可能的事。你为什么这样说？为什么横生这么多枝节？你本不该有半句。你听了一个荒唐故事，奉为福音，一跑了之，离了婚，嫁了一个恶棍，等到发现那故事是个残忍的谎言，你倒像水蛭一样叮住他不放。」

「格里安，你要是知道就好了。我的处境可怕——」

她想告诉他：她身陷一座布满酷刑的监狱，一座她同他想带她走一样急切想逃的监狱；只是这座监狱，她无法从那扇名叫罪愆的敞门里逃出去。

他不容她分说，当即打断。

「你的处境我知道，那也是你自找的。老天作证，有一件事你大可拿定主意：你会走的——要是我非得把你带走不可。」

===Original===
As he spoke he looked so brutal that she shrank.

“Gulian, you will kill me. I thought so before. I know it now.”

“It is only what you deserve.”

“Gulian! And you said you loved me!”

“Yes, but you make me doubt it.”
===Chinese===
他说这话时神情那样狰狞，她缩了一下。

「格里安，你会杀死我的。从前我这样想，如今我知道了。」

「那不过是你应得的。」

「格里安！你说过你爱我的！」

「是说过，可你叫我对那话起了疑。」

===Original===
The wrap which previously she had loosened she let fall on a chair beside her and put the fichu on the table.

“Gulian, you must give me time.”

The words were simple, plaintively uttered, but her action with the cloak and lace, gave them an emphasis which added to his irritation.
