“Nonsense,” he retorted. “You have had time enough. Now you must act.” Roughly he considered her. “Anyone else might think you cared for that﻿—”

“Gulian, in all the world you know I love but you.”
===Chinese===
她把先前松开的外套褪在身侧的椅子上，又把披巾搁到桌上。

「格里安，你必须给我时间。」

话很简单，说得哀婉；可她处理外套与蕾丝的动作，给它们添了一层强调，反而更添他的恼怒。

「胡说，」他回敬。「你的时间够多了。现在你必须行动。」他粗暴地打量她。「换了旁人，怕要以为你舍不得那个——」

「格里安，全世界你知道，我爱的只有你。」

===Original===
Verplank raised the cloak, reached for the fichu.

“Put these on, then, and come.”

But Leilah, with a gesture that was less of resistance than of appeal, motioned them from her. The gesture infuriated him. He threw the cloak about her.

“By God! You shall put them on. What’s more, you will come whether or not you want to.”

As he spoke he seized her, lifted her.
===Chinese===
弗普兰克拾起外套，又去拿披巾。

「那就穿上，走。」

可蕾拉一摆手，与其说是抗拒，不如说是恳求，把它们从身边推开。这手势激怒了他。他把外套朝她身上一裹。

「老天作证！你一定得穿上。不但如此，不管你愿不愿意，你都得走。」

说着他抓住她，把她抱了起来。

===Original===
To Leilah it seemed as though she were about to be carried off violently, like a prey. Unresistingly she raised her face to his.

“Gulian, kill me. It will be better; it will end it all.”

Something, the words, the tone in which they were uttered, the helplessness of them and of her; but, more than anything else perhaps, the fact that as he held her he felt her tremble, stayed him. He put her down. His arms fell from her.

Catching again at the chair she steadied herself, and added:

“But if I am to live and love you, be patient. Gulian, if you would stop to think, to realise, you would be patient, you﻿—”
===Chinese===
蕾拉只觉得自己就要像一件猎物似的被人强行抢走。她不作抵抗地仰起脸，朝着他的脸。

「格里安，杀了我吧。那样倒好；一切就都了了。」

是什么止住了他——是那些话，是话里的声调，是话与人的无助；而比一切都更甚的，或许是这一桩：他抱着她时，觉出她在发抖。他把她放了下来。他的手臂从她身上垂落。

她又抓住那把椅子稳住自己，添了一句：

「可若要我活着爱你，就请你忍耐。格里安，你只要肯停下来想一想、悟一悟，你就会忍耐的，你——」

===Original===
He started from her. “You don’t mean﻿—”

At the question and its insinuation, hotly she flushed. Verplank saw but the flush. The day previous she had told him that she had taken Barouffski to serve as a barricade between them. Since then he had cajoled his imagination with the idea that the creature stood to her as husbands do on the stage, show entities who, the role performed, cease otherwise to be husbands. Now the idea seemed to him hideously naive. The flush refuted it. It did more. It revealed not only other relations but the result of them. Instantly he divined that it was for this that she refused to go. At once within him waked the primitive, the aboriginal self that lurks always and, save in the high crises of the emotions, sleeps always within us all. He was in that condition in which men slay with bare hands and afterward consider them marvelingly, wondering at whose command they could have worked. Perspiration came to his forehead, started about his nose and mouth. With the fichu which he held he wiped them, but on the table from which he had taken it was a layer of dust and ashes, the refuse of the *cendrier* which Violet had overturned. It streaked his face, griming him with a mask comic and sinister.

With that mask, he called at her.

“Then may you be forever damned.”

The malediction passed from him, reached her, shook her. She held to the chair for support. Then indignantly she protested.
===Chinese===
他猛地退开。「你不会是说——」

这问题连同它的言外之意，令她腾地红了脸。弗普兰克却只看见那红晕。前一天她还告诉过他，她嫁给巴罗夫斯基，是要他做横在她与弗普兰克之间的一道壁垒。自那以后，他的想象力一直被这样一团诱哄养着：那个东西之于她，正如舞台上的丈夫之于女演员——些摆样子的存在，戏份一完，便不再是丈夫。此刻这念头在他看来天真得可憎。这红晕驳倒了它。还不止驳倒。它披露的不止是别样的关系，还有那关系的结果。他立刻参透：她拒不肯走，正为此故。霎时间，他体内醒来了那个原始的、太古的自我——它永远潜伏着，除了在情感的滔天危机里，永远沉睡在我们众人之中。他正处在男人们会赤手杀人的境地，事后还会惊奇地看着自己那双手，纳闷它们究竟奉了谁的号令。汗珠渗上他的额头，鼻子和嘴边也冒了出来。他拿手里的披巾去擦；可桌上——他拿披巾的那张桌上——积着一层灰与烟灰，是维奥莱特掀翻烟灰缸剩下的残渣。灰痕抹了他一脸，给他糊上一副又滑稽又狰狞的面具。

他就戴着那副面具，冲她喊：

「那就愿你永世受诅咒。」

这诅咒从他身上发出去，抵达她，撼动她。她扶住椅子才没有倒。随即她愤然抗议：

===Original===
“Gulian!”

He did not hear. An idea had come to him, one that had visited him in Melbourne, again in New York, to desist from further effort, to leave her where she was, behind the barriers she had raised. At the moment he believed he desired her no longer, loved her no more, had never loved her at all. Occupied with the idea he looked at this woman who had ruined her life, ruined his own.

She had been saying something, what he did not know nor, self-centered in his anger, did he care.

In his pocket was a revolver. He felt of it and infuriatedly cried:

“You ought to be shot.”
===Chinese===
「格里安！」

他没有听见。他生出了一个念头——在墨尔本访问过他、在纽约又来过一次的那个念头：就此撒手，由她留在原地，留在她筑起的壁垒后面。此刻他相信自己不再要她，不再爱她，从来不曾爱过。他耽着这念头，打量这个毁了自己一生、也毁了他的一生的女人。

她方才一直在说着什么，他不知道，也不在乎——他的愤怒只以自己为轴心。

他口袋里有一把左轮手枪。他隔着衣料按了按它，狂怒地喊：

「你该吃枪子儿。」

===Original===
“Gulian!”

“You are on a par with the beast you took up with.”

“I took his name, Gulian, his name alone.”

It was her turn to be angry. The flush had gone, she was pale again and she had abandoned the chair’s support. She stood upright, confronting him with that purity which was hers.

“I have no more been his wife than I was yours.”
===Chinese===
「格里安！」

「你同你搭上的那头畜生正是一路货。」

「我拿去的只是他的姓氏，格里安，单单他的姓氏。」

这回轮到她动怒了。红晕退了，她又白了脸，也不再倚着椅子。她笔直站着，以她本有的那份纯洁迎着他。

「我做他的妻子，从不比做你的妻子更真。」

===Original===
“What!”

This time he heard. But her words, conflicting with his thoughts, rolled over together. In this mental confusion he stared.

“What!”

“It is as I tell you.”

“You swear it?”
===Chinese===
「什么！」

这一回他听见了。可她的话同他的念头顶撞在一起，滚作一团。他在精神的混乱中瞪着。

「什么！」

「我说的都是实情。」

「你敢起誓？」

===Original===
“Do I need to?”

Still he stared. Truth which acts on us and in us like a chemical precipitate was disclosing to him her whiteness and its own.

“Do I?” she repeated.

“No, by God, you don’t. I believe you. I can’t help myself. It is in your eyes.”

He paused and awkwardly added:
===Chinese===
「还用起誓吗？」

他还在瞪。真相当头一击，像化学沉淀一样在我们身上、在我们里面起作用，正把他向她的清白、连同它自己的清白，一一披露出来。

「还用吗？」她重复。

「不用，老天作证，不用。我信你。我由不得自己不信。它就在你眼睛里。」

他顿了顿，又笨拙地补上一句：

===Original===
“Forgive me.”

Faintly and sadly she smiled.

“Will you?” he asked.

“Kiss me.”

In the unique syllables of the words, which in a woman’s mouth are so fluid, there was a forgiveness so entire and a love so great that in passionate contrition he drew her to him. Longly their lips met. She closed her eyes, opened them, disengaged herself, moved back a step and looked at him. For the first time she noticed the grime on his face. It did not astonish. It seemed natural after what they had both been through and it occurred to her that her own appearance might be equally bizarre.
===Chinese===
「饶恕我。」

她微弱而凄然地笑了。

「你肯吗？」他问。

「吻我。」

这两个字，从一个女人口中说出来是那样柔软，其中蕴含的宽恕那样完整、爱那样深，他以炽烈的悔恨把她搂进怀里。他们的唇久久相贴。她闭上眼，又睁开，挣脱出来，退后一步，望着他。她这才第一次注意到他脸上的污痕。她并不惊讶。经了两人共同经过的这一切，这倒显得自然；她还想到，她自己的模样说不定同样古怪。

===Original===
Briefly then, in this lull in the storm, she told him what Violet had suggested﻿—the buying and divorce of Barouffski.

“That will take time,” he objected. “The shortest way ’round is the quickest way out. If you had not interfered in the garden﻿—”

A gesture completed the sentence.

“No matter,” he grimly added. “I haven’t done with him yet.”

In speaking he had crossed the room, now he recrossed it.
===Chinese===
随后，趁风暴暂歇，她把维奥莱特的主意对他讲了——收买巴罗夫斯基，再行离婚。

「那要费时间，」他反对。「绕最短的路，才是最快的出路。方才你要是在花园里不加阻拦——」

一个手势把这句话说完了。

「不打紧，」他狠狠地又加一句。「我跟他还没有完。」

说这话时他已穿过屋子，这时又折了回来。

===Original===
Imploringly Leilah approached him.

“Gulian, not that, not that! Don’t fight with him again. Don’t, I beseech you. It is not alone that anything of the kind is so horrible but he is one of the trickiest swordsmen here. Think what that means! Think what would become of me if﻿—if﻿—”

From the pocket of his coat Verplank had taken the revolver. He looked at it, looked at her, replaced it.

“I am a trifle interested in the matter myself. Besides, there are other weapons than the foil. If I can shoot pigeons﻿—and I believe I can﻿—I ought to be able to land a buzzard.”

At sight of the revolver Leilah had winced. Now she cried:
===Chinese===
蕾拉哀恳地迎上去。

「格里安，别那样，别那样！别再同他动武了。别了，我求你。倒不只是这类事有多可怕，还因为他是此地最刁滑的剑手之一。想想这意味着什么！想想我该怎么办，要是——要是——」

弗普兰克已从外套口袋里掏出那把左轮枪。他看了看枪，又看了看她，把枪放了回去。

「这事我自己也有点兴味。再说，武器也不止花剑一种。我要是打得中鸽子——我相信打得中——就该打得中一只秃鹫。」

一见左轮枪蕾拉就打了个哆嗦。此刻她喊：

===Original===
“Give it to me!”

Verplank, amused at her simplicity, smiled.

“That isn’t a dueling pistol.”

“But you never carried one before.”

“In the States I did not need to. Here, in Paris, particularly at night, the streets are seldom sure. I have this thing for protection.”
===Chinese===
「把它给我！」

弗普兰克被她的天真逗笑了。

「那不是决斗用的枪。」

「可你从前从不带枪。」

「在美国用不着。这里，在巴黎，尤其是夜里，街上难得保险。我带这家伙是为了防身。」

===Original===
“Promise me then﻿—”

Verplank looked her over.

“Don’t be a fool.”

But as he looked, suddenly she started and he saw that she was trembling.

“What the deuce is the matter?”
===Chinese===
「那你答应我——」

弗普兰克把她上下打量。

「别犯傻。」

可就在他打量的时候，她忽然一惊，他看出她在发抖。

「究竟怎么了？」

===Original===
Trembling still, peeringly now she had turned to the portières.

“What is it?” he repeated.

“I am so frightened.”

“Frightened? What at?”

Uncertainly, her head drawn back as a deer’s is when surprised, she glanced about her. Slowly then her eyes returned to his.
===Chinese===
她还在抖，眯着眼，这时已转向门帷。

「是什么？」他又问。

「我好怕。」

「怕？怕什么？」

她游移不定，脑袋像受惊的母鹿那样往后缩着，四下里张望。随后她的眼睛缓缓回到他身上。

===Original===
“I am so frightened!”

“Yes, but at what?”

She motioned at the room. “Before you came there seemed to be something here, something around me and just now﻿—”

“Well?”

“I heard something.”
===Chinese===
「我好怕呀！」

「嗯，可怕什么？」

她朝屋子摆了摆手。「你来之前，这里仿佛有什么东西，有什么东西围着我，而方才——」

「嗯？」

「我听见了什么。」

===Original===
“Your maid probably.”

With an intake of the breath she raised a finger and for a moment both were silent.

“There is no one,” he presently told her. “And what if there were!”

At the idea, he laughed.

The laugh, succeeding the silence, while intended to reassure her, did not wholly succeed. She turned to him anew and in a low voice, said:
===Chinese===
「多半是你的女仆。」

她倒吸一口气，竖起一根手指，两人一时都沉默了。

「没有人，」他随即告诉她。「再说，就算有又怎样！」

想到这个，他笑了。

这笑声接在沉默之后，本想让她安心，却未尽如愿。她又转向他，低声说：

===Original===
“You must go. Tomorrow come to Violet’s.”

“I dine there tonight.”

“Yes, I know. Tell her that tomorrow, say at three, we will both be with her. Then she can tell us﻿—”

But Verplank had drawn her to him. Again her eyes closed.

“Go,” she said at last.
===Chinese===
「你必须走了。明天到维奥莱特家来。」

「今晚我在她家吃饭。」

「嗯，我知道。告诉她，明天，就说三点吧，我们两个都到她那儿。到时她就可以告诉我们——」

可弗普兰克已把她拉进怀里。她的眼睛又闭上了。

「走吧，」她终于说。

===Original===
On the sofa was his hat. He reached for it. While he did so, she moved to the tapestry, raised it, disclosing the stair up which he had come.

“Tomorrow, then,” he said, as he entered there.

She nodded at him. “At three!”

Dropping the tapestry she turned, but very quickly, for again she heard or thought she heard a noise.

Across the room the portières were parting. Through them Barouffski appeared.
===Chinese===
沙发上放着他的帽子。他伸手去拿。趁这当口，她走到挂毯前，掀起它，露出他方才上来的那道楼梯。

「那么，明天见，」他边往下走边说。

她朝他点头。「三点！」

她放下挂毯转过身——转得飞快，因为她又听见了、或者自以为听见了什么响动。

屋子那头，门帷正被分开。巴罗夫斯基从帷后出现了。

===Original===
“I might have known it,” she told herself, and realising that he had been listening, she realised also that the opportunity was as good as another for making an offer which she had in mind.

These ideas, instantaneous at sight of him, were for the moment stayed. On turning she had seen but the man. Now hastening toward her was a creature with an expression so venomous that instinctively, in search of help, with the idea of calling to Verplank, she turned to the tapestry again.

Quicker than she, he caught and tossed her spinning on the sofa. Then, running to the open window, he shouted from it:

“Emmanuel! The dogs!”
===Chinese===
「我早该如何道了，」她对自己说；一意识到他方才一直在偷听，她也随即意识到：这倒是个再好不过的机会，正好提她心里盘算的那桩交易。

这些念头在他现身的一刹那间闪过，一时却被定住。她转身时看见的只是那个男人。此刻朝她扑来的却是一个生物，脸上的表情毒恶到这般地步，她本能地想求援，起了唤弗普兰克的念头，重又转向挂毯。

他比她更快，一把擒住她，把她掼得直转，摔在沙发上。随即他奔到敞开的窗前，冲窗外大喊：

「埃马纽埃尔！放狗！」

===Original===
Leilah, falling backward on the lounge, was too stunned to hear. But she steadied herself, recovered, got to her feet and making again for the stair, called at Barouffski:

“Free me from you and you shall have half of what I have.”

“Half!” he repeated. At once he was upon her. “All,” he cried. “I want it all, all of yours and all of his.”

As he spoke he struck her, shoved her aside, raised the tapestry and vanished. For a second she heard him hastening down, while at once from without came the barking of dogs, the jar of oaths, the sound of cries.
===Chinese===
蕾拉仰面倒在长榻上，惊得什么也没听见。但她撑住自己，缓过神，站起身，重又扑向楼梯，冲巴罗夫斯基喊：

「放我自由，我的东西分你一半。」

「一半！」他重复。随即他扑到她身上。「全部，」他喊。「我要全部——你的全部，加上他的全部。」

说着他打了她一记，把她搡到一边，掀起挂毯，一闪而没。有一秒钟，她听见他急奔下楼的声响；而墙外顷刻传来狗吠、咒骂的聒噪，还有声声喊叫。

===Original===
What it meant she did not know. Her head was whirling. The fall, the blow, the indignity of both clouded and confused her. From without the uproar mounted and suddenly, the uproar prompting, into the turmoil that was her mind, a gleam of understanding shot. At the apperception of it she shrieked, ran to the window where she shrieked again. The loosened dogs had sprung at Verplank, who, overwhelmed had fallen.

Again she shrieked. Answering the shriek, mingling with it, were snarls, the gnashing of fangs, the din of great hounds ferociously struggling for blood, tearing vehemently at flesh, at a flesh, though, that rebelled.

Verplank rose up between them. With a kick he sent one of them sprawling. But, in the recoil, torn at by the other hound, he stumbled. The dog was at his throat. In protection he held his left arm against it. With his right hand he got at the revolver in his pocket, and, through the pocket, fired twice into the brute. Gnashing still, it rolled away.

But now, from the other side, the second hound was on him. He saw its eyes, felt its breath, felt its fangs. Again he fired. As he did so, his hand relaxed. He heard a woman shrieking, the sound of hurrying feet. The wall before him mounted. His senses scattered into night.
===Chinese===
这意味着什么，她不知道。她的头在打转。那摔、那打、两者加于她的屈辱，把她搅得又昏又乱。墙外的喧嚣越来越高，突然，喧嚣一催，一线了悟射进她心里那团乱麻。她一彻悟便尖叫起来，奔到窗口，在那里又尖叫一声。放开的两条狗扑向了弗普兰克，他寡不敌众，倒下了。

她再一声尖叫。应和着尖叫、与尖叫混作一处的，是低吼，是獠牙的磨锉，是巨犬们为血疯狂争食的喧嚣，狂暴地撕扯着肉——只是那肉，不甘就范。

弗普兰克从犬群中间撑起身。他一脚把其中一条踹了个跟头。可反冲之下，另一条猛犬撕咬着他，他踉跄了。狗扑向他的咽喉。他把左臂横过去抵挡。右手掏到口袋里的左轮枪，隔着口袋朝那畜生连开两枪。它还咬着牙，滚到一边去了。

可这时，另一条犬又从另一侧扑上他。他看见它的眼睛，觉出它的鼻息，觉出它的獠牙。他又开了一枪。枪响之际，他的手松了。他听见一个女人在尖叫，听见杂沓奔跑的脚步。眼前的那面墙升了起来。他的知觉散入黑夜。

===Original===
Suddenly the garden was filled with people. Through the gate, two *sergents de ville* had come. These, forms furtive and uncertain followed. From the house, led by Barouffski, the footmen ran. Above, from the window, still there issued a woman’s shrieks.

Barouffski stopped, and turned. He looked up at the window. He smiled. With one hand he tapped his breast, with the other he pointed at Verplank. Then, in French, reassuringly, he called:

“My dear! See! You may be tranquil. I, I am unharmed. It is the robber.”

At the ignominy of that flouting jeer, Leilah, impelled by the impulse to do something, though it were but to beat her head against a wall, rushed from the window, and, strangling with spasms, fled out of the room and down the stair, where horror so suffocatingly enveloped her that in it her brain tipped, and she fell.
===Chinese===
花园里霎时挤满了人。两名 *sergents de ville*（法语：巡警）从园门进来，身后跟着几条鬼祟游移的影子。宅子里，巴罗夫斯基领着男仆们奔出来。楼上，窗口仍传出女人的尖叫。

巴罗夫斯基停下脚步，转过身。他抬头望向窗口。他微笑了。一只手拍拍自己的胸口，另一只手指向弗普兰克。随后，用法语，宽慰地喊道：

「亲爱的！瞧！你可以放心。我，我好端端的。那是强盗。」

那当众轻慢的奚落太辱人，蕾拉被一股冲动驱使着——哪怕只是拿头去撞墙，也总得做点什么——从窗口冲了出去，噎堵得直抽搐，逃出房间，奔下楼梯；恐怖在那里把她裹得严严实实、令她窒息，她的脑子在恐怖里一个倾侧，她倒了下去。

## XI / 十一

===Original===
In the golden half light of the Opéra, a chorus, soprano voices on one side of the stage alternating with contralto on the other, vaporised the subtle sensuality of the scene.
Violet Silverstairs, turning to her husband, who was seated behind her, remarked:
“How much better the Italian school is than the French.”
Silverstairs, ignorant of either, and indifferent to both, promenaded his glass about the house.

===Chinese===
在歌剧院金色的半明半暗中，一支合唱队——舞台一侧的女高音与另一侧的女低音交替应答——把场面上微妙官能的气息化成了雾霭。
维奥莱特·西尔弗斯泰尔斯转向坐在她身后的丈夫，说道：
「意大利乐派比法国乐派高明多少啊。」
西尔弗斯泰尔斯对两者都一无所知，也对两者都无动于衷，只顾举着望远镜扫视全场。

===Original===
“I wonder why Tempest doesn’t show up? There is Marie de Fresnoy! I saw de Fresnoy today for the first time since his duel with Barouffski. What a ridiculous affair that was! I suppose one of these days he will have another with d’Arcy.”
Violet turned to him again.
“Because of Marie? How absurd you are! D’Arcy doesn’t interest her. No man could unless he drove at her with a four-in-hand, and d’Arcy has nothing.”
Silverstairs, still promenading his glass, exclaimed:

===Chinese===
「不知道坦皮斯特为什么还不露面？玛丽·德·弗雷努瓦在那儿！我今天见到了德·弗雷努瓦，那是他跟巴罗夫斯基决斗以来我头一次见到他。多可笑的一场事！我想过些日子他还会跟达尔西来一场。」
维奥莱特又转向他。
「为了玛丽？你多荒唐！达尔西引不起她的兴趣。没有一个男人能，除非他赶着四驾马车向她冲过去，而达尔西一无所有。」
西尔弗斯泰尔斯还在扫着他的望远镜，叫道：

===Original===
“There he is now!”
“Who? D’Arcy?”
“Yes, with the Helley-Quetgens, in that box between the columns. Isn’t that your friend Leilah whom he is talking to? By Jove, it is, and Barouffski is there, also.”
Violet, who had also been promenading her glass, put it down.

===Chinese===
「他这不是来了吗！」
「谁？达尔西？」
「对，跟埃利-凯特根一家在一起，在柱子中间那个包厢里。跟他说话的不正是你的朋友蕾拉吗？天哪，正是她，巴罗夫斯基也在那儿。」
维奥莱特本来也在扫视，这时放下了望远镜。

===Original===
“Well, he ought to be. I do think she has acted scandalously. What is said at the club?”
“About Verplank? It is forgotten already. Barouffski, you know, claimed that it was a mistake, and as it appears that Verplank agreed with him, as from neither the one nor the other any charge was forthcoming, the police could do nothing but get Verplank back to the Ritz.”
Impatiently Violet unfurled her fan.
“Yes, where she has been every day; every day, that is, when she has not been with d’Arcy.”

===Chinese===
「嗯，他理当在。我确实认为她行事够出格的。俱乐部里怎么说？」
「关于弗普兰克？早被忘掉了。你知道，巴罗夫斯基声称那是个误会，而既然弗普兰克看来也同意他，双方又都拿不出任何指控，警察无计可施，只能把弗普兰克送回里兹。」
维奥莱特不耐烦地抖开扇子。
「是啊，她天天都在那儿；天天，就是说，不在达尔西身边的那些天。」

===Original===
The statement was inexact. Leilah had indeed been at the Ritz but d’Arcy she had seen but once, momentarily, by accident﻿—if there be such a thing, in any event through one of those seeming hazards which, however fortuitous at first, afterward appear to have been designed. It was a little, though, before Leilah took that view of things. Meanwhile, when, on recovering from her swoon, she learned that Verplank had also recovered she realised with thanksgiving that Destiny which has its tyrannies has its mercies as well. So soon then as she could get from the bed into which the horrors of the midspring nightmare had thrown her, she went to the Ritz where she found Verplank amply attended, abundantly bandaged, severely but not dangerously hurt.
“One of the brutes nearly chewed my arm off,” he told her. “If the other omitted to eat me entirely, it was not because he did not try. I did for them, though,” he added, and smiled as he said it. After the manner of man, he took comfort in the feat.
“But not for the worst brute,” Leilah answered wishing in spite of herself, wishing instinctively and even ungrammatically that some good fate might.
From beneath a bandage, Verplank laughed:

===Chinese===
这话并不确切。蕾拉确实去过里兹，但达尔西她只见过一次，转瞬即逝，出于偶然——如果世上有偶然这种东西的话，总之是经由那种乍看似出于运气、事后却显得早有安排的表面巧合。不过，蕾拉过了一阵才这样看待此事。当时，她从晕厥中苏醒过来，得知弗普兰克也已脱险，便满怀感恩地意识到：命运有它的暴虐，也有它的慈悲。于是，一等到能从仲春噩梦把她摔进去的那张床上爬起来，她就去了里兹，发现弗普兰克得到周全的照料，缠满了绷带，伤得重，但无性命之虞。
「有一只畜生差点把我的胳膊咬下来，」他告诉她。「另一只没把我整个吃掉，不是因为它没试过。不过我收拾了它们俩。」他补了一句，说的时候还笑了笑。照男人的方式，他在这番功绩里找到了安慰。
「可最凶的那只畜生你没收拾，」蕾拉答道，不由自主地、本能地、甚至不合文法地盼着某个好命运能替他收拾。
弗普兰克从绷带底下笑了。

===Original===
“Bah! I’ll do for him, too.”
But Leilah did not hear. She was speaking to the surgeon, whom﻿—with a bravery which in itself was a little defiant, and which in any event might have been more discreet﻿—thereafter, daily and openly, she supplied with that which every surgeon wants, a nurse obedient, attentive, skilful, alert, and who, in addition ministers for love.
Presently, Verplank was able to be up. The surgeon said that in a day he would be able to be out. Verplank, who knew as much without being told, asked Leilah to go with him on the morrow.
Leilah refused. Verplank, for an invalid, became then surprisingly demoniac. The demonism of him affected her less than a conception, feminine perhaps but erroneous, of her own selfishness. If she went, she knew beforehand that irremissibly she would be dishonoured. But she knew also that any sense of dishonour must, if it is to ashame, come not from without but from within. If she went, her conscience, she thought, would acquit her. She thought that she would not feel dishonoured, though she knew that she would be disgraced. To refuse on that account seemed to her selfish. As a result finally she consented. Yet in consenting she made one stipulation. Characteristic in itself, it was that there must be nothing clandestine, that he must come for her in the Rue de la Pompe, and that from there, her boxes put on whatever vehicle he brought, they would leave for darkness by daylight.

===Chinese===
「呸！我连他也一样收拾。」
但蕾拉没有听见。她在跟外科医生说话——此后，她天天、公开地，以一种本身就带着几分挑战意味、且无论如何本可更含蓄些的勇气，向外科医生提供了每个外科医生都想要的东西：一个顺从、周到、灵巧、机警的护士，而且还是出于爱而看护的护士。
不久，弗普兰克能下床了。医生说再过一天他就能出门。弗普兰克不用人告诉也知道得一样清楚，便邀蕾拉明天陪他一起出去。
蕾拉拒绝了。弗普兰克作为一个病人，这时变得出奇地魔性大发。他的魔性对她的影响，倒不如她对自己自私的一种观念——也许是女性的观念，却是错的——对她的影响大。她若去，她预先就知道，将不可挽回地蒙羞。但她也知道，耻辱之感若要灼人，必不来自外界，而来自内心。她若去，她想，她的良心会开释她。她想她不会感到羞辱，尽管她知道自己将声名扫地。为此而拒绝，在她看来就是自私。于是她最终答应了。但在答应的同时她提了一个条件。这条件本身很有她的特色：一切必须光明正大，不得有任何偷偷摸摸；他必须到蓬佩街来接她，从那里，把她的行李箱装上他带来的任何车辆，然后他们在白昼里动身，驶向黑暗。

===Original===
The plan pleased Verplank. He agreed at once. He told her that he would come the next day.
When he had, she added: “Tonight I go to the Opéra; the Helley-Quetgens have asked me. It is my last look at this world.”
Then, shortly, the arrangements for the evasion completed, she left the hotel.
Without, her motor waited. She told the groom to have it follow her. The air tempted, though the sky was dirty. She thought of the California glare, the eager glitter of New York. She wondered would they go back there. Perhaps, she told herself, we shall at last see Bora-Bora.

===Chinese===
这个计划让弗普兰克称心。他立刻同意了。他告诉她第二天就来。
等他来了，她补充道：「今晚我去歌剧院；埃利-凯特根一家邀请了我。这是我看这个世界的最后一眼。」
随后不久，出逃的各项安排都已办妥，她离开了旅馆。
门外，她的汽车等着。她让司机开着车跟在她后面。天气诱人，虽然天色阴沉沉的。她想起加利福尼亚的烈日，想起纽约热切的闪烁。她不知道他们会不会回到那里去。也许，她对自己说，我们终究会见到博拉博拉岛（Bora-Bora）。

===Original===
Her walk took her through the arcades of the Rue de Rivoli to the fountains of the Place de la Concorde. From there she was about to enter the Champs Élysées when she became conscious of being accosted.
“*Chère madame*,” someone was saying, “I precipitate myself to renew the expression of my homage.”
D’Arcy, hat in hand, was before her. At once, with a view to what the French agreeably describe as the placing of landmarks﻿—*pour poser des jalons*﻿—he asked to be permitted to accompany her.
Leilah smiled.

===Chinese===
她一路步行，穿过里沃利街（Rue de Rivoli）的拱廊，走到协和广场（Place de la Concorde）的喷泉。她正要从那里走进香榭丽舍大街（Champs Élysées），忽然察觉有人上前搭话。
「*Chère madame*（亲爱的夫人），」有人在说，「我急切投身向前，只为重申我致敬之意。」
达尔西手拿帽子，站在她面前。随即，本着法国人美其名曰「设置地标」——*pour poser des jalons*——的用意，他请求获准陪她同行。
蕾拉微微一笑。

===Original===
“Not for the world!”
She motioned at the motor. Then, with that graciousness which is natural to the mondaine, with perhaps the desire also to attenuate whatever there were of brusqueness in her reply, she added, as she got in the car:
“I shall be at the Opéra with the Helley-Quetgens tonight. Could you not look in?”
D’Arcy, habituated to the abruptest victories, accustomed to inflame, with but a glance, by the mere exhibition of his Olympian good looks, and, therefore, indifferent when not bored by the celerity of his successes, but piqued by the tranquil air with which this woman had always regarded him, thanked her, assured her that he would not fail to be there, and replaced his hat.

===Chinese===
「万万不可！」
她朝汽车示意了一下。随后，带着交际场中人与生俱来的那种雍容，或许也带着冲淡她回答中生硬之处的愿望，她一边上车一边补了一句：
「今晚我在歌剧院，跟埃利-凯特根一家。你不来看看吗？」
达尔西惯于最迅捷的胜利，惯于仅凭展示他奥林波斯式的俊美、一个眼色便燃起火焰，因此对成功的神速不是无动于衷就是厌倦，却被这个女人一贯用以看待他的那种平静神气所刺痛。他谢过她，向她保证一定到场，然后戴回帽子。

===Original===
Immediately he raised it again, straight from the head, high in the air. Looking with brilliant eyes from a brilliant brougham, Violet Silverstairs was dashing by.
Coincidentally, unobserved but observant, Barouffski was also passing that way.
Leilah’s motor flew off and she sank back, wondering at herself, wondering rather what influence, malign and unhallowed, could possibly have prompted her to ask this man, whom she disliked as﻿—in spite of a theory to the contrary﻿—honest women do dislike a man of his type. But though, at the time, she could not understand what impelled her, later it seemed to her that it must have been fate.
Barouffski had a different interpretation. At the Joyeuses he had seen Leilah and d’Arcy together. Now, here they were again. The circumstance, of which the fortuitousness was unknown to him, irritated him for that very cause. But he could imagine and did. At once it was clear to him that the brute was after the blue eyes of her bankbook. The deduction, however erroneous, was easy. He was viewing the matter, not, as he fancied, from d’Arcy’s standpoint, but from his own. In spite of which, or rather precisely on that account, he told himself that d’Arcy was a damned scoundrel. The humour of this quite escaped him. But that perhaps was in the order of things.

===Chinese===
他立刻又把帽子摘了下来，笔直地脱开头顶，高高举在空中。一辆漂亮的布劳厄姆马车（brougham）飞驰而过，维奥莱特·西尔弗斯泰尔斯正从车里用明亮的眼睛张望。
碰巧，巴罗夫斯基也在那时经过，无人注意，却注意着一切。
蕾拉的汽车飞驰而去，她向后靠坐，为自己纳罕，更确切地说，是为究竟是什么恶毒而不洁的影响，竟能促使她邀请这个男人而纳罕——她厌恶他，正如——尽管有一种相反的理论——正经女人厌恶他这一类型的男人那样。虽然当时她不明白是什么在驱使她，事后她却觉得，那想必就是命运。
巴罗夫斯基另有一番解释。在茹瓦耶兹家他见过蕾拉和达尔西在一起。现在，他们又在这里。这一情况——他并不知道其偶然性——恰恰因其偶然而激怒了他。但他能够想象，也确实想象了。他立刻明白了：那头畜生盯上的是她那本银行存折的蓝眼睛。这推断虽然错误，却来得容易。他看待这件事，并非如他自己所想，是从达尔西的立场，而是从他自己的立场。尽管如此，或者毋宁说正因如此，他告诉自己：达尔西是个该死的恶棍。这其中的幽默他全然没有领会。但那或许也是事物之常理。

===Original===
Since the night at the Joyeuses, he had been measuring himself solely against Verplank. Twice he had failed with him, but he knew that soon they would be at each other again and for the next bout he had in view a coup which, he felt, would do for him definitely. Meanwhile, if in regard to Leilah he had been led into certain vivacities, he felt that with time, which is the great emollient, her memory of these vivacities would pass. Even otherwise, the law was with him. He proposed to see to it that she was also﻿—she and with her her purse. The one menace to both had been Verplank. Here, now, apparently, was another. Here was d’Arcy with his pseudo-Pheidian air, that famous yet false appearance of a young and dissolute Olympian which made imbeciles turn and stare. Ragingly Barouffski reflected that canaille though d’Arcy were, he carried a great many guns, almost as many as Verplank, who, worse luck, had, in addition, the signal advantage of being Leilah’s first love﻿—that love to which it is said one always returns.
But even as he sounded the stupidity of that aphorism, vaguely, for a dim second, he intercepted a gleam refracted from truth. The danger with which he had to contend, Verplank did not personify or d’Arcy either, it was himself. When the golden six was tossed him, had he but then known how to secure the box, there would now be no danger at all. But truth, when it does not console, confounds. Barouffski put it from him. It was too exasperating. “Bah!” he told himself, “if her attitude does not change, a sojourn in the solitudes of Lithuania may alter it.” Angrily he nodded. Things more surprising have occurred there.
On this day it was Leilah who surprised him.
Since he had called to her from the garden, she had encountered him only in the hazards of entrances and halls. On such occasions she had passed with an air of being unaware that there were anything save chairs and tables about.

===Chinese===
自茹瓦耶兹家那一夜以来，他只拿弗普兰克来掂量自己。跟弗普兰克他已两次失手，但他知道他们很快又会交上手，而为了下一回合，他心中盘算着一记他自觉定能彻底了结对手的重击。至于蕾拉，倘若他此前对她有过某些暴烈之举，他觉得，随着时间——那伟大的缓和剂——这些暴烈之举在她记忆里终会过去。即便不然，法律也站在他一边。他打算设法让她也站过来——她，连同她的钱袋。对这两者的唯一威胁一直是弗普兰克。现在，看样子，又来了一个。这就是达尔西，带着他那副伪菲狄亚斯（Pheidian）气派——那副年轻而放荡的奥林波斯神的著名而虚假的相貌，蠢人们见了都要回头瞪视。巴罗夫斯基狂怒地想：达尔西虽是下流胚，武装却很齐备，几乎跟弗普兰克一样齐备；而弗普兰克，更倒霉的是，还占着一桩显著的优势——他是蕾拉的初恋——据说人总会回到初恋身边的那种爱。
然而，就在他掂量这句格言的愚蠢时，恍惚间有一秒钟，他截获了一缕从真理折射来的微光。他必须与之抗争的危险，弗普兰克不能化身它，达尔西也不能，那是他自己。当初金色的六点掷到他手里时，他若知道如何守住那只匣子，如今就根本没有危险。但真理不能安慰人时，便只会搅乱人。巴罗夫斯基把它推开了。它太让人恼火了。「呸！」他对自己说，「要是她的态度不变，到立陶宛（Lithuania）的荒僻之地住上一阵也许能变。」他愤怒地点点头。在那边，更出人意料的事都发生过。
这一天，倒是蕾拉让他吃了一惊。
自从他在花园里向她呼喊那回起，她只在进门和过厅的偶然相遇中碰到过他。每逢这种场合，她走过去时总带着一副只当屋里除了桌椅别无他物的神气。

===Original===
In part, it was this attitude which he thought certain solitudes might change. Oddly enough, Leilah herself wished it altered. But to want to do one thing and to do something else, happens to all of us, even to the best. She despised Barouffski and yet in despising him knew that the one contemptible thing is contempt. For what he had done, she felt that no punishment could be too severe, yet in so feeling she knew that he was only the embodiment of past misdeeds of her own. Physically he had struck her. Spiritually, it was her own hand that had dealt the blow. He had loosed the dogs on Verplank and she had judged and condemned him for it, though she knew that not only she should not judge at all, but that never perhaps do useless events occur. Clearly these events were evil, but were not those which she planned evil too?
In this dilemma there was some slight consolation for her in the knowledge that it was not her fault, at least not her present fault, that she had been born with a nature so problematic. But the Vidya, in teaching her that whatever we suffer is derived from our past; that the people who wrong us﻿—or seem to﻿—are mere puppets come to claim karmic debts which we owe; the Vidya, in teaching her that taught her also that every life we lead here is but a day in school. Her schooling, she felt, had as yet been insufficient. No doubt she would know better when she came here again.
The thin gilt hope of that fortified her a little on this day when, to Barouffski’s surprise, she sent for him and then, her head raised, said distantly:
“The Helley-Quetgens have asked us to the Opéra. I am going. You are free to do as you like.”

===Chinese===
在他看来，某些荒僻之地也许能改变的，部分正是这种态度。说来也怪，蕾拉自己也希望它改变。但想做一回事，做出来另一回事，我们人人都如此，连最好的人也不例外。她鄙视巴罗夫斯基，可在鄙视他的同时又知道：唯一可鄙的东西就是鄙视本身。对于他的所作所为，她觉得无论怎样惩罚都不为过，可在这样想的同时她又知道，他不过是她自己过去罪孽的化身。肉体上，是他打了她。精神上，出手的是她自己的手。他对弗普兰克放出了狗，她为此审判他、定他的罪，尽管她知道，她不仅根本不该审判，而且也许从无无用之事发生。这些事显然是恶的，可她筹划的那些事难道不也是恶的吗？
在这进退两难之中，有一点小小的安慰：她生就如此成问题的天性，这不是她的错，至少不是她现在的错。但 the Vidya 教导她：我们遭受的一切都源自我们的过去；那些伤害我们的人——或看似伤害我们的人——不过是来讨我们还欠着的业债的傀儡；the Vidya 在这样教导她时，也教导她：我们在此间过的每一世，不过是学堂里的一天。她觉得自己的学业还不够。毫无疑问，等她再来时，她会懂得更多。
这一层薄薄镀金般的希望，在这一天给了她些许支撑。这一天，令巴罗夫斯基吃惊的是，她差人去叫他，随后抬起头，淡漠地说：
「埃利-凯特根一家邀我们去歌剧院。我去。你随意。」

===Original===
Here, obviously, was something new. At it and at her Barouffski looked with shifting eyes. Uncertainly he rubbed his hands.
“But how then! I am at your orders. It is a festival to be where you are.”
But as he did nothing without an object, he wondered what hers was. Obviously, there was a reason. Yet, what? Could it be an olive branch? He was too adroit to ask. Even otherwise, he lacked the opportunity. Leilah had gone from the room.
It was in these circumstances that, on this night, she appeared at the Opéra where Violet was complaining at having seen her with d’Arcy.

===Chinese===
这显然是件新鲜事。巴罗夫斯基闻声望望这话、又望望她，眼神游移不定。他迟疑地搓着双手。
「那还用说！听凭您的差遣。能到您所在的地方，就是节日。」
但他做事从无目的之外的动作，他纳闷她的目的是什么。显然，有个理由。可是，是什么呢？会是一枝橄榄枝吗？他太乖巧，不会去问。即便不然，他也没有机会。蕾拉已经离开了房间。
正是在这种种情形之下，这一夜她出现在歌剧院，而维奥莱特正在抱怨看见她和达尔西在一起。

===Original===
At the complaint, Silverstairs pulled at his moustache.
“I did not know that she had taken up with him.”
“I don’t know that she has either. But she was with him today in the Champs Élysées.”
“Oh, come now! Things haven’t got to such a pass that a woman can’t be seen with a man﻿—”

===Chinese===
听了这抱怨，西尔弗斯泰尔斯捻了捻胡须。
「我不知道她跟他好上了。」
「我也不知道她是不是跟他好上了。可她今天在香榭丽舍大街跟他在一起。」
「哦，得了吧！事情还没糟到一个女人不能跟一个男人在一起被看见——」

===Original===
“No, but no honest woman can be seen alone with d’Arcy. Leilah ought to know better. She ought to know better too than to go to the Ritz. As she does not appear to, I propose to tell her.”
“Do as you like,” replied Silverstairs who would have said the same thing no matter what his wife had suggested. The lady had not entirely Americanised this Englishman but she had at least made him realise the futility of argument.
“Do as you like,” he repeated. “There are the Orlonnas. There are the Zubaroffs.”
At once to the quick click of an *ouvreuse*’s key, the door opened and Tempest appeared, a foulard showing above his coat.

===Chinese===
「不，可是一个正经女人不能单独跟达尔西在一起被人看见。蕾拉应该更明白事理。她也该明白，别往里兹跑。既然她看来不明白，我打算去告诉她。」
「随你的便，」西尔弗斯泰尔斯答道——无论他妻子建议什么，他都会说同一句话。这位女士没有彻底把这个英国人美国化，但至少让他认识到了争辩的无益。
「随你的便，」他重复道。「奥隆娜家在那儿。祖巴罗夫家也在。」
应着女引座员（*ouvreuse*）钥匙一声利落的咔嗒，门开了，坦皮斯特走了进来，大衣领口上方露出一方丝巾。

===Original===
While he removed these things Violet called at him:
“You’re late.”
Silverstairs laughed. “He always is. At Christ Church he was known as the late Lord Howard.”
Tempest moved forward and sat down between them.

===Chinese===
他一边脱这些东西，维奥莱特一边冲他喊：
「你迟到了。」
西尔弗斯泰尔斯笑了。「他一向迟到。在基督堂学院（Christ Church）那会儿，大家就管他叫『迟到的霍华德勋爵』（the late Lord Howard）。」
坦皮斯特走上前，在他们两人中间坐下。

===Original===
But now, to the volatile sweetmeats of the score, the curtain was falling. In the stalls there was a movement. Men stood up, put their hats on, turned their back to the stage or set forth for a chat with the vestals in the green room.
Silverstairs also stood up.
Violet turned to him:
“I do wish you would look in on the Helley-Quetgens, and ask Leilah to come to luncheon tomorrow. Say I have a bone to pick with her. That may fetch her, if nothing else will.”

===Chinese===
但此刻，应和着乐谱那些轻飘飘的甜腻，大幕正在落下。池座里起了一阵骚动。男人们站起来，戴上帽子，转身背对舞台，或是动身到后台休息室去，找那些「灶神贞女」们闲聊。
西尔弗斯泰尔斯也站了起来。
维奥莱特转向他：
「真希望你去埃利-凯特根家的包厢看看，请蕾拉明天来吃午饭。就说我有一根骨头要跟她算账。别的请不动她的话，这总能请动她。」

===Original===
Tempest ran a hand through his vivid hair.
“A bone over what, if I may ask? You may not know it, but I greatly admire Madame Barouffska.”
Violet smiled.
“She’s a dear. But I saw her today with d’Arcy, and I propose to scold her for it.”
Tempest showed his teeth.

===Chinese===
坦皮斯特一只手拢过他鲜艳的头发。
「能问问是为哪桩事算账吗？你也许不知道，我非常钦佩巴罗夫斯卡夫人。」
维奥莱特微微一笑。
「她是个可人儿。可我今天看见她跟达尔西在一起，我打算为此说她一顿。」
坦皮斯特龇了龇牙。

===Original===
“D’Arcy is not a man’s man, though he certainly is a woman’s. Yet, when you come to that, not such a woman as Madame Barouffska. What an odd thing that was about her first husband!”
“You mean about the dogs?”
“Yes. I never got the rights of it. What was he doing there? Is she living in the past?”
Violet raised her opera-glass.

===Chinese===
「达尔西不是男人中的男人，倒确实是女人中的男人。不过，话又说回来，也不是巴罗夫斯卡夫人那样的女人。她那位前夫的事，多离奇呀！」
「你是说狗那件事？」
「对。我始终没弄清内情。他去那儿干什么？她还活在往事里吗？」
维奥莱特举起了观剧镜。

===Original===
“She would be very lucky if she could be; living in the present is so expensive, don’t you think?”
Again there was a quick click. The door opened. Silverstairs, filling the entrance with his tall stature, reappeared.
“Violet,” he began, “the Helley-Quetgens are going on to some dance in the Faubourg, and Leilah wants the three of us to sup with her at Paillard’s. What do you say?”
Violet laughed. “I say it will be just my chance.” She turned to Tempest. “You will come?”

===Chinese===
「她要是能那样，可就太走运了；活在当下开销太大，你说是不是？」
又是一声利落的咔嗒。门开了。西尔弗斯泰尔斯高大的身影塞满门口，重新出现。
「维奥莱特，」他开口道，「埃利-凯特根一家要去贵族区（Faubourg）参加一个舞会，蕾拉想请我们三个跟她一起去帕亚尔餐厅（Paillard's）吃夜宵。你说呢？」
维奥莱特笑了。「我说这正是我的机会。」她转向坦皮斯特。「你来吗？」

===Original===
“Thanks, yes. Isn’t that de Fresnoy with the Zubaroffs?”
Silverstairs, without sitting down, raised his glass.
“Yes, and I was just saying, this is the first time since his duel that I have seen him. But what an asinine affair that was! He lunged at Barouffski’s neck, Barouffski knocked the foil up and pricked himself on the chin with it. Then Barouffski’s surgeon stopped the fight on the ground that it might interfere with his breathing. Fancy that! Afterward, in the account given to the press, the surgeon described the prick as an incisive wound in the hyoidian region, accompanied by a notable flow of blood. Anyone who did not know would have thought that Barouffski had been nearly done for. But that’s a French duel for you﻿—a funeral at which everybody giggles.”
Tempest looked gravely up at his friend. “What did you have for dinner?”

===Chinese===
「谢谢，来。跟祖巴罗夫家在一起的不是德·弗雷努瓦吗？」
西尔弗斯泰尔斯没有坐下，举起了望远镜。
「对，我刚才还在说，这是他决斗以来我头一回见到他。可那是多蠢的一场事啊！他朝巴罗夫斯基的脖子刺过去，巴罗夫斯基把花剑往上一磕，剑尖戳破了自己的下巴。然后巴罗夫斯基的医生叫停了比剑，理由是那伤口可能妨碍他呼吸。你想想！事后在给报纸的通报里，那位医生把那一点戳伤描述成舌骨区域的切割伤，伴有可观的出血。不知道内情的人还以为巴罗夫斯基差点送了命呢。可这就是法国式决斗——一场人人都咯咯发笑的葬礼。」
坦皮斯特严肃地抬头看他的朋友。「你晚饭吃了什么？」

===Original===
Suspiciously, Silverstairs considered him.
“Why do you ask?”
“You are so expansive and brilliant.”
On the stage, the drama continued, poignantly, beatifically, in a unison of violins and voices that was interrupted at last by the usual stir in the stalls and boxes, by the haste to be going, to be elsewhere, and a defile began; a procession of silken robes, gorgeous cloaks, jeweled headgear, black coats, white ties; a procession that presently filled the subscribers’ rotunda, from which, at sight of it, grooms fled, then hurried back, touching their hats, eager and zealous.

===Chinese===
西尔弗斯泰尔斯狐疑地打量他。
「你问这个干什么？」
「你这么兴高采烈、神采飞扬。」
台上，戏剧在继续，惨恻而极乐，小提琴与人声交融成一片，终于被池座和包厢里惯常的骚动打断——人们急着要走，要到别处去——一场鱼贯而出的行列开始了：绸缎长裙、华美的斗篷、珠光宝气的头饰、黑礼服、白领结的行列；转眼间挤满了订户席的环形大厅，一见这阵仗，听差的先逃开去，又急忙赶回来，碰着帽檐，急切而殷勤。

===Original===
Between the columns groups loitered, regarding each other with indulgence, with indifference, at times with a loftiness that put isolating zones about them; and women assumed that attitude which women alone can assume, that attitude of being not only apart from the crowd, but of being unaware of the crowd’s existence.
In the centre, Mme. Orlonna, an Italian princess, with a slight moustache and an ancestry that extended to the super-Neronian days of Heliogabalus, stood, laughing and talking, lisping *Bonthoirs* to everybody.
Another princess, a Russian, Mme. Zubaroff, with a young girl at her side, and an escort of blond giants, passed, inclining her head to the left, to the right, bowing with a grace mechanical, but sovereign.
Beyond, Leilah appeared, d’Arcy on one side, Barouffski on the other. Her face, ordinarily pale, was flushed, and her manner, usually subdued, was animated. She was laughing, not loudly, but noticeably.

===Chinese===
柱子之间，一堆一堆的人闲立着，彼此打量，带着宽容，带着淡漠，有时带着一种在他们四周划出隔离地带的高傲；女人们摆出唯有女人才能摆出的那种姿态——不仅置身人群之外，而且浑然不觉人群存在的姿态。
在中央，奥隆娜夫人，一位意大利王妃，唇上有一点浅浅的胡髭，家谱可以上溯到埃拉加巴卢斯（Heliogabalus）那超尼禄时代的岁月，站在那里又说又笑，咬着舌头向每个人道着*Bonthoirs*（「日安」的嗲音）。
另一位王妃，俄国人祖巴罗夫夫人，身边伴着一个年轻姑娘，一队金发巨人护卫着，走了过去，向左点头，向右点头，行礼的姿势机械，却有君王气派。
再过去，蕾拉出现了，一侧是达尔西，另一侧是巴罗夫斯基。她的脸平日苍白，此刻泛着红晕；她的神态平日低回，此刻活泼。她在笑，笑声不高，却引人注目。

===Original===
Violet, accompanied by Tempest and Silverstairs, approached. As the men, after saluting the women, greeted each other, Violet tapped Leilah with her fan.
“My dear, I have a bone to pick with you.”
Leilah, with a levity that was rare with her, interrupted:
“It is just for that we are going to supper. How will you have it, grilled or deviled?”

===Chinese===
维奥莱特由坦皮斯特和西尔弗斯泰尔斯陪着走近。男人们向女士们行过礼、彼此寒暄时，维奥莱特用扇子轻拍蕾拉。
「亲爱的，我有一根骨头要跟你算账。」
蕾拉带着她少有的轻快，接口道：
「我们这不正要去吃夜宵嘛。你要怎么个做法，烤的还是辣烤的？」

===Original===
“Her ladyship’s carriage is at the door,” a groom announced, in English.
Another added, in French: “The motor of madame la comtesse is advanced.”
“Yes,” Violet retorted. “But my bone belongs to a different kettle of fish. Now, you come with me.” With a smile, she turned to the others. “We will go in the brougham, and you take the motor.”

===Chinese===
「夫人您的马车已候在门前，」一个听差用英语通报。
另一个用法语补充：「伯爵夫人的汽车已开上前来。」
「是呀，」维奥莱特回敬道。「可我要算的账属于另一码事。来，你跟我来。」她微笑着转向其他人。「我们坐马车，你们坐汽车。」

===Original===
Stooping, she lifted her train, and the two women, accompanied by the men, followed the groom to the carriage.
There, after seeing them in, Barouffski called:
“To Paillard’s, Chaussée d’Antin.”

===Chinese===
她俯身提起裙裾，两位女士由男士们陪着，跟着听差走向马车。
到了那里，看着她们上车之后，巴罗夫斯基喊道：
「去帕亚尔餐厅，安坦大道（Chaussée d’Antin）。」

## XII / 十二

===Original===
At the glass door, which a chasseur opened, Barouffski stopped, spoke to the man, gave him an order. As the others, conducted by a maître d’hôtel, approached a table, a fat woman in a pulpit charged them, before they were seated, with the use of the silver and the cloth.
Beyond, a band of Bohemians, costumed in crimson, were loosing, with nervous and dirty fingers, whirlwinds of notes. The atmosphere, filled with vibrations, fevered by the fury of the violins, dripped with the scent of flowers, with the bouquet of burgundies, the smell of champagne, the odour of tobacco and food.
At adjacent tables were demireps and foreigners, mondaines and clubmen, a sprinkle of the cream of the venal, the exotic and the ultra-chic, whom omnibuses and waiters, marshaled by maîtres d’hôtel, served with the same deference and zeal.
For the Barouffski party, these latter had turned two tables into one, at which Violet Silverstairs occupied one end, Leilah the other. Violet had Barouffski at her right, Tempest at her left, while Leilah had Silverstairs at her left and d’Arcy at her right, a disposition natural enough and otherwise fortuitous which placed Tempest next to d’Arcy, with Barouffski and Silverstairs opposite.
In the rising storm of the music, Leilah turned to d’Arcy. What she was saying the others could not hear and all, save Silverstairs, who was munching a hors d’oeuvre, addressed themselves to Violet.
Presently, in a lull of the gale, Tempest would have tried to talk to this woman who, in abandoning her Madonna air had now the merit of suggesting both the Chimera and the Sphinx, but something in her attitude to d’Arcy prevented. It was not, to employ a vulgarism, that she was making eyes at the man, but she was obviously permitting him to make eyes at her.
===Chinese===
在玻璃门前——一名 chasseur（门房侍者）拉开门——巴罗夫斯基停下脚，对那人说了一句，吩咐了一件事。其余的人由一位侍者领班引着走向餐桌，还没落座，一个坐在高账台（宛如讲坛）后的胖女人先就银器和台布的使用向他们收了费。
再里面，一队穿猩红衣裳的波希米亚乐师，用紧张而肮脏的手指，放出一阵一阵音符的旋风。空气里满是震颤，被小提琴的狂热烧得发烫，滴着花香、勃艮第酒的醇味、香槟、烟草和菜肴的气味。
邻桌坐着暗娼与外国人、交际花与俱乐部男士，还有几粒「脂粉场」的精华、异域客与顶时髦的人物；传菜小工和侍者在领班们的调遣下，以同样的恭敬和殷勤伺候着他们。
为巴罗夫斯基这一伙人，侍者们把两张桌子拼成了一张：维奥莱特·西尔弗斯泰尔斯坐一头，蕾拉坐另一头。维奥莱特右手是巴罗夫斯基，左手是坦皮斯特；蕾拉左手是西尔弗斯泰尔斯，右手是达尔西——一个够自然、其余纯属偶然的座次，坦皮斯特挨着达尔西，巴罗夫斯基与西尔弗斯泰尔斯对面。
乐声的风暴越涨越高，蕾拉转向达尔西。她说什么，别人听不见；于是所有人——只除了正嚼着一份餐前冷盘（hors d'oeuvre）的西尔弗斯泰尔斯——都同维奥莱特攀谈起来。
不多时，风势稍歇，坦皮斯特本想同这个女人聊聊——她丢开了那副圣母的神气之后，如今兼有喀迈拉（Chimera）与斯芬克斯（Sphinx）之妙——可她对着达尔西的那副姿态里有什么东西拦住了他。用一个俗话说，倒不是她在向那人抛媚眼，而是她明明白白容许那人向她抛媚眼。

===Original===
D’Arcy was seated, his arms on the table, talking in her face. His plate was empty. A chaudfroid had been served. He had refused it. A mousse had followed. He had refused that also. Over the glasses at his side he had put a hand. It seemed a pose of his not to eat or to drink that he might do nothing but talk.
Leilah herself had not eaten. But as soon as champagne was served she had drunk of it, she had drunk since and in her manner, in the way she held herself, in the inflection of her voice, there had entered a trace of the excessive which the mondaine avoids. It was this that had deterred Tempest. Moreover she had been laughing and that surprised Violet who, except a little earlier in the rotunda, never, since Leilah reached Paris, had seen her laugh before.
Now, her head drawn back, her eyes half closed, she was gratifying d’Arcy with that look with which a woman can appear not to listen merely but to drink the words, the appearance even, of the man by whom she is addressed. While perhaps flattering to him, it was too marked for good taste. The others noticed it, but, as is usual in such circumstances, they acted as though they had not.
Barouffski conscious of the impression produced, conscious also of the impressions of the afternoon, leaned forward and said in French:
“But, my dear! You eat nothing!”
Silverstairs, tugging at his moustache, laughed inanely and addressing himself to both Leilah and d’Arcy, threw in:
===Chinese===
达尔西坐着，双臂支在桌上，冲着她的脸说话。他的盘子空着。一道肖弗鲁瓦（chaudfroid，热制冷吃的奶油肉冻）端上来，他辞了。随后是一道慕斯（mousse），他也辞了。一只手罩在身旁的杯子上。不吃不喝似乎是他的一个姿态，好叫他一心只顾说话。
蕾拉自己也什么都没吃。可香槟一上来她就喝了，此后一直喝着；在她的举止里、在她持身的姿态里、在她嗓音的抑扬里，渗进了一丝交际花避之不及的「过火」。正是这个打消了坦皮斯特的念头。况且她还一直在笑——这让维奥莱特吃惊：自蕾拉到巴黎，除了方才在圆厅里那一小会儿，她从没见她笑过。
此刻她头向后仰，眼睛半阖，用那样一种眼神酬答着达尔西——女人用这种眼神，显得不只是在与男人交谈时倾听，简直是在畅饮他的话语，连他这个人本身也在畅饮。这于他或许受用，却太着痕迹，有失品味。旁人都看在眼里，但如这种场合的惯例，都只当没看见。
巴罗夫斯基意识到了这印象，也意识到下午的种种印象，俯身向前，用法语说：
“可是，亲爱的！您什么都没吃！”
西尔弗斯泰尔斯捻着髭，傻笑了一声，冲着蕾拉和达尔西两个人插进一句：

===Original===
“If this is a private conversation﻿—”
“What nonsense!” Leilah threw back.
“I was about to say,” Silverstairs resumed, “that if it is a private conversation, I’d like to hear it. If it is not, never mind.”
Barouffski, still leaning forward, continued:
“I pray you take a bit of the chaudfroid.”
With a movement of impatience, yet otherwise ignoring him completely, Leilah turned again to d’Arcy.
===Chinese===
“要是这是私房话——”
“胡说些什么！”蕾拉回敬。
“我正要说，”西尔弗斯泰尔斯续道，“要是是私房话，我倒想听听。要是不是，那就算了。”
巴罗夫斯基仍俯着身，继续：
“求您尝一点肖弗鲁瓦。”
蕾拉不耐烦地一动，除此之外全然当他不存在，重又转向达尔西。

===Original===
Barouffski was not in a mood to be ignored. The sight of d’Arcy in the afternoon, the man’s unawaited advent at the Opéra, his demeanour to Leilah, her attitude to him, the hazards which both seemed to suggest; yet chiefly the precariousness of his own position, the constant effort to appear other than what he was, the consciousness of danger ever present, the obligation to cover irritation with calm, anxiety with banter, these things and the tension of them, fevered and enraged. At the moment he felt like a fiend and looked it. A moment only. Reacting at once, he compressed his lips, parted them and summoning his ambiguous smile, called out:
“If the chaudfroid says nothing to you, will you not try the mousse?”
Leilah was raising a glass to her lips. She looked over it at him and, much as though he were a servant, said:
“Do me the favour to attend to your own affairs.”
Barouffski’s smile evaporated. A man with no sense of honour and some sense of humour may go far, provided that he keep his temper. Barouffski knew it but forgot it. With a tone of authority which in the Rue de la Pompe he would have ordinarily avoided, angrily he replied:
“Then do me the favour not to drink any more.”
===Chinese===
巴罗夫斯基可没心情被人无视。下午撞见达尔西，此人不请自来地现身歌剧院（the Opéra），他对蕾拉的态度，她对他的态度，两人身上似乎都在暗示的种种风险；更要紧的是他自己处境的岌岌可危——那份把自己装成另一个人的持续用力，那份对无处不在的危险的自觉，那份不得不用镇定盖住恼怒、用打趣盖住焦灼的义务——这一切，连同它们的紧张，烧得他又热又怒。那一刻他觉得自己像个恶鬼，看上去也像。只一刻。他立刻反应过来，抿紧嘴唇，再张开，唤出他那暧昧的笑，喊道：
“要是肖弗鲁瓦引不起您的兴致，不妨试试慕斯？”
蕾拉正把一杯酒举向唇边。她隔着杯沿看他，那样子就当他是个仆人似的，说：
“劳驾，管好你自己的事。”
巴罗夫斯基的笑蒸发了。一个没有荣誉感、尚有些幽默感的人，只要沉得住气，是可以走得很远的。这个道理巴罗夫斯基懂，却忘了。他用一种在蓬佩街他通常要避免的权威口气，怒冲冲地答道：
“那就劳驾，别再喝了。”

===Original===
Leilah, the glass at her lips, paused, looked over it again, and very gently, almost sweetly, with the pretty air of a spoiled child, nodded at him.
“Only one sip.”
She touched the glass with her lips, for a moment held it there, then, offering it to d’Arcy, rather languorously she said:
“Beau sire, will you drink the rest?”
Instantly Violet intervened. “Leilah! Behave yourself!”
“But with delight,” d’Arcy was saying.
===Chinese===
蕾拉酒杯还在唇边，顿了顿，又隔着杯沿看他，然后极温柔地、几乎是甜腻地，带着受宠孩子的娇态，朝他点了点头。
“就抿一小口。”
她用嘴唇碰了碰杯子，在那里停了一瞬，随后把杯子递向达尔西，娇慵地说：
“Beau sire（美爵爷），剩下的您替我喝了？”
维奥莱特立刻出面。“蕾拉！放规矩点！”
“不胜荣幸。”达尔西正说着。

===Original===
From Leilah’s extended hand he took the glass, raised it, drained it, put it down, looked at her.
Barouffski was looking at him. Quietly, without emphasis, he asked:
“Will you drink mine, too?”
Half rising as he spoke, he had taken his own glass in his hand and with a gesture which, even as he made it, he regretted, a gesture incited by vibrations which he was unable to resist, he flung the contents at him.
“Barouffski!” Violet indignantly exclaimed.
She glanced about her. At her elbow an omnibus, a lad undersized but stout, stood gaping. Beyond, the Bohemians were storming. At the adjacent table were demireps and South Americans. They had not noticed. At this table, Tempest, his teeth visible, was contemplating his host. Silverstairs, tugging at his moustache, was considering Leilah. The latter was looking﻿—and with what a look!﻿—at Barouffski. But no one spoke. A spell seemed to have settled on all. With the idea of doing or of saying something that would break it, Violet turned to d’Arcy.
===Chinese===
他从蕾拉伸着的手里接过杯子，举起，一饮而尽，放下，看着她。
巴罗夫斯基正看着他。他平静地、不动声色地问：
“我的这杯，您也喝吗？”
说话间他半站起来，一手抓起自己的酒杯，做了一个连他自己做出的一瞬就已后悔的手势——一个被他自己无力抗拒的震颤所煽起的手势——把满满一杯酒泼向了他。
“巴罗夫斯基！”维奥莱特愤然喝道。
她环顾四周。她肘边，一个矮胖的传菜小工张着嘴呆立。再那边，波希米亚乐师们正掀起风暴。邻桌上坐着暗娼和南美人。他们没有留意。这一桌上，坦皮斯特龇着牙，端详着这位主人。西尔弗斯泰尔斯捻着髭，端详着蕾拉。蕾拉本人正望着——好一副什么样的眼神啊！——巴罗夫斯基。可谁也没有说话。一道魔咒仿佛罩住了所有人。想找件事、说句话把咒破掉，维奥莱特转向达尔西。

===Original===
Delicately, with a coroneted handkerchief, he had wiped his face and was then mopping at his shirt.
Interrupting the operation, he looked up and laughed. “Oh, la, la! The dangers that may be avoided in remaining at home! These are the accidents of restaurant life!”
He laughed again. The laugh humanised and deformed the Pheidian beauty of his face. He bowed to Leilah, bowed to Violet and collectively added:
“Mesdames, I have ceased to be presentable. A thousand pardons. You will permit me?”
In a moment, after another bow, circular this time, a bow which while managing to omit Barouffski, included the rest of the table, he had gone.
“He looks like Keats,” said Silverstairs animated by an unconscious desire to second his wife and break the spell which still persisted. Ordinarily he would have taken her and gone. The assault had been as much of an affront to her as it had been to d’Arcy. But to have left the table would have been a reproof to Leilah, whom, in the ridiculous way in which society is organized, he was unable to disassociate from Barouffski.
===Chinese===
他正用一方绣着冠纹的手帕，细致地擦着脸，随后又去揩衬衫。
他停下手里的事，抬起头笑了。“Oh, la, la（哎哟哟）！待在家里能躲开多少危险啊！这就是餐馆生活的意外！”
他又笑。这一笑使他脸上菲狄亚斯式（Pheidian）的美变得有人气，也走了形。他向蕾拉鞠躬，向维奥莱特鞠躬，又冲全体补了一句：
“Mesdames（夫人们），我这副样子见不得人了。千罪万罪。容我告退？”
转眼间，他又鞠了一躬——这一回是环形的，一躬绕过全桌，独独设法绕开了巴罗夫斯基——人已去了。
“他长得像济慈（Keats）。”西尔弗斯泰尔斯说，浑然不觉自己是在给太太帮腔，想破掉那仍未散去的魔咒。换在平时，他早带她走了。那一泼对她是冒犯，正如对达尔西是冒犯一样。可离席便等于责备蕾拉，而按社会那套可笑的组织法，他又没法把蕾拉同巴罗夫斯基拆开。

===Original===
“Keats!” Tempest, coming to his aid, exclaimed. “I’ll lay a guinea you would not know his picture if you saw it.”
Amiably Silverstairs tugged at his moustache. “Well, perhaps not. What I meant was that he looks like a poet.”
“I don’t agree with you,” Tempest retorted. “To begin with, there are not any. Besides, latterly there have been but two﻿—Hugo, who looked like a greengrocer, and Swinburne who looked like a bookseller’s assistant. Moreover I hate poets, though, as someone said somewhere, an inability to write in verse can hardly be regarded as constituting a special talent. No, d’Arcy does not look like a poet, he looks like a poet’s creation.”
“Excuse me,” Silverstairs with affected meekness threw out. “And thanks for the lecture.”
Tempest nodded. “You’re entirely welcome.”
He turned to Violet. She was looking at Leilah who was looking at Barouffski. The latter was looking at the fingers of his right hand against which his thumb passed and repassed mechanically. But now, aroused from his reflections by the entire cessation of talk, he glanced about him, summoned a waiter, settled the score. The Bohemians who momentarily had been silent, abruptly striped the air with spangles from their bows.
===Chinese===
“济慈！”坦皮斯特赶来给他搭把手，叫道。“我赌一几尼，你见了济慈的画像也认不出来。”
西尔弗斯泰尔斯和气地捻着髭。“唔，也许认不出。我的意思是，他长得像位诗人。”
“这我可不赞同，”坦皮斯特反驳。“第一，根本就没有诗人。再说，晚近统共出过两位——雨果（Hugo），长得像菜贩子；斯温伯恩（Swinburne），长得像书店伙计。何况我讨厌诗人，虽然正如谁在哪儿说过的，不会写诗这件事，很难算得上一项特殊的才能。不，达尔西长得不像诗人，他像诗人的造物。”
“恕我冒昧，”西尔弗斯泰尔斯装出谦卑的样子，插话。“多谢指教。”
坦皮斯特点头。“不必客气。”
他转向维奥莱特。她正望着蕾拉，蕾拉正望着巴罗夫斯基。后者则望着自己右手的指头，拇指在上面机械地来回摩挲。可这时，四下里彻底的静默把他从沉思中惊醒，他环顾一眼，唤来侍者，结了账。方才静了一刻的波希米亚乐师，倏地又在琴弓起落间撒出金粉，给空气抽出一道道纹。

===Original===
Violet and Leilah stood up, resumed their wraps, passed on. The men, buttoning their coats, putting their gloves on, followed.
At the door were the eager grooms. As one of them touched his hat to Leilah, Violet turned to her.
“My dear, I cannot thank you for a very pleasant evening. But I will look in on you tomorrow. That bone isn’t picked and what’s more, now I’ve got sauce for it.”
With Silverstairs and Tempest at her heels, she went to her brougham. Leilah entered the motor.
At the door of the latter Barouffski stood. He raised his hat. Leilah looked at him. She had had, she thought, her last glimpse of the world and this was her last glimpse of him. The sight was so repugnant that she almost sickened and the nausea which she felt, her face expressed.
Barouffski tried to smile but the unconcealed candour of her abhorrence made his lips twitch. Now, though, the motor was starting. As it whirred away, he drew his coat closely about him, turned up the collar and stuck his hands deep in the pockets. There had come to him that odd sensation which homely fancy attributes to someone walking on your grave.
===Chinese===
维奥莱特和蕾拉站起身，重新披上披肩，走了出去。男人们扣着外衣、戴着手套，跟在后面。
门口是热切的司机们。其中一位向蕾拉碰了碰帽檐，维奥莱特转向她。
“亲爱的，这么一个愉快的夜晚，我实在没法谢你。不过我明天要来看你。那桩事还没跟你算完；更何况，如今我又给它添了作料。”
西尔弗斯泰尔斯和坦皮斯特跟在她身后，她朝她的布鲁厄姆马车（brougham）走去。蕾拉钻进了汽车。
汽车门边站着巴罗夫斯基。他举了举帽。蕾拉看着他。她想，她已与这世界诀别过了，而这便是与他的诀别。那景象令她如此作呕，她几乎呕出来，她心里的恶心，全写在了脸上。
巴罗夫斯基试图笑一笑，可她那毫不掩饰的、坦率的憎恶令他的嘴角直抽。不过这时汽车发动了。它嗡嗡地驶远，他紧了紧外衣，竖起领子，双手深深插进衣袋。他心里泛起了那种古怪的感觉——家常的说法里，把它归给有人正从你的坟头走过。

## XIII / 十三

===Original===
The next day, Violet, entering the brilliant room, gazed first about it and then at Leilah.
“Aurelia is not here? That’s odd. She is simply horrid but so reliable. You don’t mind my having told her to meet me?”
Leilah sighed. “I am getting so that soon I shan’t mind anything.”
Violet, seating herself, nodded vivaciously.

===Chinese===
第二天，维奥莱特走进那间明晃晃的房间，先环顾四壁，再看蕾拉。
「奥蕾莉亚没来？怪了。她这人实在可恶，可又这么可靠。我让她来见我，你不介意吧？」
蕾拉叹了口气。「我已经到了这步田地，很快就不会介意任何事了。」
维奥莱特坐下身来，活泼地点了点头。

===Original===
“I call that very fine. But there is something finer. Never mind anybody. Silverstairs now﻿—” and as the lady spoke she summoned a smile feline and Cheshire﻿—“he fancied I would be a good, obedient little wife. Instead of which he is a good, obedient big husband.” In entire self-appreciation she exhibited the tip of her tongue and moistened her lips with it. “It takes us, doesn’t it? But forgive me, dear, us is perhaps an exaggeration. I am afraid you have made rather a mess of things. Now what are you going to do?”
Without replying, Leilah looked away. During the night she had barely slept. The incident in the restaurant, events that had preceded it, anterior complications, subsequent developments, these things, like the Bohemians at Paillard’s, had stormed at her, attacked her fibres, wrenched her nerves, striating the darkness of her room with variations on the tragedy of her life.
In what manner the affair in the restaurant had terminated, she had no one to inform her but she could readily fancy that shortly d’Arcy and Barouffski would go somewhere and fight, or pretend to, and then return, none the worse and none the better, but with honour satisfied and their names in print.
The entire episode was shameful. But though provoked by her she had not premeditated it. In offering d’Arcy her glass, she had wished solely to display her independence. Subsequently, in going over the matter, she had realised that the wish, while human, had not been nice. Then, a bit conscience-stricken, she had wondered how she could have behaved as she had.

===Chinese===
「我说这很好。可还有更好的。谁都别放在心上。就说西尔弗斯泰尔斯吧——」说到这个名字，这位女士唤起一个猫一般、柴郡猫一般的微笑——「他原以为我会做个又乖又听话的小媳妇。结果呢，是他做了个又乖又听话的大丈夫。」她满心自得地露出舌尖，舔了舔嘴唇。「咱们女人吃亏就吃亏在这上头，是不是？不过原谅我，亲爱的，『咱们』也许说大了。恐怕你把事情弄得相当糟。现在你打算怎么办？」
蕾拉没有回答，把目光移开了。夜里她几乎没有合眼。餐馆里的那场事端、先前种种、前因的纠葛、后续的发展，这些东西，像帕亚尔餐厅里的那些波希米亚人一样，向她轮番猛攻，侵袭她的纤维，扭绞她的神经，把她房间的黑暗划出一道道条纹——全是她人生悲剧的种种变奏。
餐馆里那场事端是如何收场的，没有人来告诉她，但她不难想象：达尔西和巴罗夫斯基不久会找个地方打一架，或者装装打架的样子，然后回来，不好不坏，但荣誉得到了满足，名字也见了报。
整个事件是可耻的。虽然是她招惹的，她却并非蓄意。把酒杯递给达尔西时，她一心只想显示自己的独立。事后回想此事，她意识到那个愿望虽属人之常情，却并不光彩。于是她有点良心不安，纳闷自己怎么会那样行事。

===Original===
“I did it without thinking,” was her immediate excuse. But that, she told herself was untrue. Ever since the duel and the blow and the nightmare that followed, some such wish had been fermenting in her. The wish, reasonable in itself, though in her case unreasoned, persisted, as in certain natures a wish will persist, until, after the fashion of a constantly recurring idea, the individual becomes so saturated with it that, given the impulse, given less than that, given a vibration, some effect, perhaps wholly atmospheric, and suddenly the idea has solidified into an act, an act noble, degrading or merely banal, according to the influence that produced it, but an act which, whatever its character, has then become inevitable, even involuntary, its constant mental recurrence having exhausted the ability to choose between it and another.
Leilah had thought of this and, in search of comfort, had groped for another excuse. As an Oriental will say: “My body is tired.” “My body is hungry,” so Leilah decided: “It was my nerves that did it.”
But the sophistry displeased her. She knew that she had judged and condemned Barouffski and she knew also that she had no right to do either. The man was clearly a cad, a scoundrel and a brute, yet even though he were these things and worse and more besides, he so became in her eyes merely because her consciousness had evolved to a point higher than his own. Seen from a different angle, she might appear only a shade less reprehensible, and it might be, even equally vile. Moreover, according to the Upanishads, this consciousness of hers and that consciousness of his were fundamentally one. Both had come from the same source. To that source both would ultimately return. They would be fused there, as originally there they had been merged.
At the apperception of that, the curious lines which occur in the *Book of Dzyan* suggested themselves:

===Chinese===
「我是不假思索做的，」是她当下找的借口。但她告诉自己，这不是真话。自决斗、那一记耳光和随后的噩梦以来，某个这样的愿望一直在她心里发酵。这愿望本身合理，在她却未经推理，于是持续不散——正如在某些天性里愿望会持续不散——直到，按照一种不断重现的念头的规律，人被它浸透到如此地步，以致只要给一个冲动，给比冲动更少的东西，给一次振动，某种或许全然是气氛性的东西，那个念头便突然凝固成一个行为——高尚的、堕落的或仅仅平庸的行为，视促成它的影响而定——但无论其性质如何，这行为到那时已成必然，甚至不由自主，因为它在意识中的不断重现，已经耗尽了在它与另一个行为之间进行选择的能力。
蕾拉想到过这一层，为了寻安慰，又摸索出另一个借口。正如东方人会说：「我的身子累了。」「我的身子饿了。」蕾拉于是断定：「是我的神经干的。」
但这诡辩让她不快。她知道自己评判并定罪了巴罗夫斯基，她也知道自己无权做其中任何一样。这人显然是个下流坯、恶棍、畜生，然而即便他是这些东西，再加上更坏更多的，他在她眼里之所以如此，也仅仅因为她的意识演化到了比他更高的地步。从另一个角度看，她也许只比他少可责一分，甚至可能同样卑劣。何况，据《奥义书》所说，她的这个意识与他的那个意识本是一体。两者来自同一个源头。两者终将回到那源头去。他们将在那里融合，一如他们原本在那里曾经合一。
领会到这一点时，《德赞之书》（*Book of Dzyan*）里那几行奇特的文字自行浮现出来：

===Original===

> “Said the Flame to the Spark: ‘I have clothed myself in thee, thou art my image, my body, until that day when thou shalt rebecome myself and others, thyself and me.’ ”

In the penetrating beauty of the allegory Leilah told herself that she, Barouffski, Verplank, the Silverstairs, the Helley-Quetgens, everybody whom she knew, everyone whom she did not know, all the fillers of space, were sparks of one Flame, the Flame from which they had been formerly emitted and into which they would finally return. That, she felt she must believe, if she were to believe anything. But she felt too that though she did believe it, she believed also that for the present there were sparks better parted than united, as there were also others better united than apart.
On the table before her was a treatise on the Paramitas﻿—which are The Blessed Things. She looked at it, knowing that what she had done was wrong and what she was about to do was evil. But she felt also that she could not help herself. Whatever the penalty, it was impossible for her to live with Barouffski. Whatever the punishment, it was impossible for her not to live with Verplank. At the moment it seemed to her as though the high fates had set her in a circle from which she could not escape except by the door of death or that of further sinning. At this idea she wondered about Barouffski as, after Verplank’s eruption at the Joyeuses, she had wondered about him. She wondered whether in some former existence she had injured Barouffski and whether it were for that reason that she had been led into giving him the power to injure her now. It might be so, she told herself, in which case she ought to bear it. But how could she know that it was so? Yet even though it were and the fact were made clear to her, even then, even too if the certainty of a punishment more poignant than any were shaken in her eyes, still she could not bear it.
“But I ought to,” she found herself saying.

===Chinese===

> 「火焰对火花说：『我已以你为衣裳，你是我的形象、我的身躯，直到那一天——你将重新成为我，成为他人，成为你自己，也成为我。』」

在这寓言那透彻的美里，蕾拉对自己说：她、巴罗夫斯基、弗普兰克、西尔弗斯泰尔斯夫妇、埃利-凯特根一家、她认识的每一个人、她不认识的每一个人、一切充塞太空者，都是同一团火焰的火花——他们先前从那火焰中迸出，终将回到那火焰中去。她觉得，若要信点什么，就必须信这个。但她也觉得，尽管她信了它，她同时相信：就目前而言，有些火花分开比合一好，正如也有些火花合一比分开好。
她面前的桌上摊着一篇论「波罗蜜多」（Paramitas，即「诸福祉」）的文章。她望着它，知道自己所做的已是错，将要做的是恶。但她也觉得自己无可奈何。无论代价如何，与巴罗夫斯基共同生活已不可能。无论惩罚如何，不与弗普兰克共同生活也已不可能。此刻在她看来，高踞的命运仿佛把她安放在一个圈子里，除了死亡之门或再犯罪的门，她无路可逃。想到这里，她对巴罗夫斯基起了疑想，正如弗普兰克在茹瓦耶兹家发作之后她对他起过的那样。她纳闷，是否在某个前世她伤害过巴罗夫斯基，是否正因如此，她才被引导着给了他现在伤害她的权力。也许是这样，她对自己说，若是这样，她就该忍受。可她怎么知道是这样呢？然而即便是这样、即便事实向她挑明，甚至再加一层——即便比任何惩罚都更刺骨的惩罚的确定性在她眼里动摇——她仍旧忍受不了。
「可我应当忍受，」她发觉自己在说。

===Original===
These reflections Violet’s advent had interrupted.
“What are you going to do?” the lady was asking.
Leilah, unfit at the moment for battle, felt unable to tell. She looked away.
“Nothing, I suppose.”

===Chinese===
这些思绪被维奥莱特的到来打断了。
「你打算怎么办？」这位女士正问着。
蕾拉此刻无心应战，觉得说不出所以然。她把目光移开。
「不怎么办，我想。」

===Original===
Violet, cocking a belligerent eye, threw out:
“You carry moderation to excess.”
Leilah looked up. “What would you have me do?”
“Divorce Barouffski.”

===Chinese===
维奥莱特挑起一只好斗的眼睛，抛出一句：
「你把节制用过火了。」
蕾拉抬起头。「你要我怎么办？」
「跟巴罗夫斯基离婚。」

===Original===
“Because he threw champagne about? I hardly fancy I could get a divorce for that.”
Militantly Violet whipped off a glove. “Frankly that creature is a criminal. Never, in all my life, have I seen such an exhibition of bad taste. But then, as they say here, bad taste leads to crime﻿—to such vulgar forms of it at that.”
“Even so, I don’t see how it helps me.”
“But he struck you,” Violet, more bellicose than ever, exclaimed. “You told me so. What more would you have. But aren’t you difficile today!” Suspiciously she considered her friend. “You’ve got something up your sleeve.”

===Chinese===
「就因为他泼了香槟？我不大觉得凭这个就能离婚。」
维奥莱特斗志昂扬地刷地褪下一只手套。「坦白说，那家伙是个罪犯。我这辈子从没见过如此恶劣品味的表演。不过，正像这儿的人说的，坏品味会导向犯罪——还是犯罪里最俗的那几种。」
「即便如此，我看不出这对我有什么用。」
「可他打了你，」维奥莱特比刚才更好斗地嚷道。「你自己告诉我的。你还想要什么。可你今天真是难缠！」她狐疑地端详她的朋友。「你袖子里藏着东西呢。」

===Original===
“If I have,” Leilah, in an effort to parry the thrust, replied, “at least it is not witnesses.”
Cogently Violet nodded. “Come to me then. Divorce is the mother-in-law of invention. If you haven’t any witnesses, I’ll find something else. I make a specialty of finding things before they are lost.”
This programme hardly suited Leilah’s book. Again she parried. “Last night﻿—and what a night!﻿—I dreamed I was feasting with the dead. It was so peaceful. It is that that I want. It is peace the very fibres of my being crave.”
Here were heights﻿—or depths﻿—where Violet could not follow. With a smile she tacked.

===Chinese===
「就算有，」蕾拉竭力招架这一击，答道，「至少不是证人。」
维奥莱特中肯地点点头。「那就来找我这儿。离婚是发明的婆婆。你要是没有证人，我会找到别的。我的专长，就是在东西丢失之前把它们找到。」
这一方案很不合蕾拉的路数。她又招架了一次。「昨夜——怎样的一夜啊！——我梦见自己与死者共宴。那么安宁。我要的就是那个。是安宁，我生命的每一根纤维都在渴求安宁。」
这是些高处——或者深处——维奥莱特跟不上去了。她微笑着换了帆向。

===Original===
“Before you dreamed your dream I noticed that you wore a very ducky frock.”
“It is stained. So am I. The champagne Barouffski threw stained me within and without.”
Here were other heights. Readily Violet skirted them.
“I believe I got a drop or two myself. But then I don’t mind. It is true I am not a theosophist.”

===Chinese===
「在你做那个梦之前，我注意到你穿了件非常俏丽的连衣裙。」
「它染上污渍了。我也是。巴罗夫斯基泼的香槟把我里里外外都染污了。」
这又是别的高处。维奥莱特轻巧地绕了过去。
「我想我自己也沾了一两滴。不过我不在乎。的确，我不是神智学者。」

===Original===
At this, in indignation at herself, Leilah protested.
“Theosophy is primarily a school of good manners. In giving d’Arcy my glass, mine were detestable.”
Now they were on surer ground. Wickedly Violet winked.
“Nobody has any manners anymore or, when they do, they have them in plenty and all of them bad.”

===Chinese===
听了这话，蕾拉带着对自己的愤慨，提出抗议。
「神智学首先是一门礼仪学堂。把酒杯递给达尔西的时候，我的礼仪糟糕透顶。」
现在她们站到了较扎实的地面上。维奥莱特调皮地眨了眨眼。
「如今谁都没有礼仪了，或者，一旦有了，就多得要命，而且全是坏的。」

===Original===
“If I have an excuse,” Leilah continued, “it is that Gulian drove me nearly demented.”
Now, Violet felt, they were getting at it. Mentally she girded herself and with an engaging appearance of sympathy, exclaimed:
“A man never does that unless he loves a woman to distraction.”
The sympathy, however feigned, did its work. After all, Leilah reflected, why should she not tell what she was about to do? On the impulse she turned.

===Chinese===
「如果我有什么借口，」蕾拉继续道，「那就是格里安把我逼得几乎发疯。」
现在，维奥莱特觉得，她们说到正题了。她在心里束紧了腰带，带着一副动人的同情模样，喊道：
「男人绝不会那样，除非他爱一个女人爱得发狂。」
这同情，纵然是装的，也起了作用。说到底，蕾拉想，她为什么不能说出自己打算做的事呢？她凭着这股冲动转过身来。

===Original===
“Violet, I offered Barouffski half my fortune to free me. His reply was a blow. Apart from that I have no grounds for a divorce, none at least which I can show. Previously there was something between Gulian and me. It has gone. He cares for me still and I care for him. Shortly he is to be here. For a long time I hesitated. But after last night﻿—after the nights and days that preceded it!﻿—it does not seem to me that I need hesitate any more.”
Pausing, she drew breath. “Violet, when he comes I am to go with him. Is there a reason why I should not?”
Violet, who had long suspected as much, now at last had nailed it. Cautiously she buckled to.
“No. Not one. On the contrary. There is every reason why you should. Every reason. Particularly as to greet you there will be the disdainful eyes, the lifted skirts, the averted heads, every one of the very slight and very fiendish tortures that are visited on the woman who has gone and done it.”

===Chinese===
「维奥莱特，我向巴罗夫斯基提出用我一半家财换他放我自由。他的回答是一记耳光。除此之外我没有离婚的理由，至少没有拿得出手的。先前我和格里安之间有点什么。现在已经过去了。他仍然在乎我，我也在乎他。不久他就要到这里来。我很久都犹豫不决。但经过昨夜——经过此前的那些日日夜夜！——在我看来，我不需要再犹豫了。」
她顿了顿，吸了口气。「维奥莱特，他来的时候，我就跟他走。有什么理由说我不该吗？」
维奥莱特早就疑心是这么回事，此刻总算把它钉死了。她谨慎地着手干起来。
「没有。一条也没有。恰恰相反。你有千百条该走的理由。千百条。尤其是，去迎接你的，将是轻蔑的眼睛、提起的裙裾、扭开的头，凡是加在那个『做了那事』的女人身上的、顶轻巧也顶恶毒的种种刑罚，一样都不会少。」

===Original===
For a second she too paused, then with a war whoop added:
“But you can’t do anything of the kind. I won’t let you.”
“Would you turn from me also?”
“I! Merciful fathers! But I am not the world. No matter what is done in private, the world does not care. The world is very well bred. It never sees anything that was not intended for it. But on the open scandal of scandalous conduct instantly it turns its well bred back. It will turn it on you.”

===Chinese===
她也停了一秒钟，随即发出一声战斗的呐喊，补充道：
「可你不能干这种事。我不许你。」
「你也要背过身去不理我吗？」
「我！慈悲的老天！可我不是这个世界。私底下无论干了什么，这个世界都不在乎。这个世界教养极好。凡不是特意给它看的东西，它一概看不见。可一旦丑行闹到了明面上，它立刻转过它那教养良好的背。它会这样对你的。」

===Original===
Indifferently Leilah assented. She had examined this phase of the matter. It had not seemed very important.
“No doubt,” she replied. “Yet then destiny seldom closes a door without opening another. I told you of my dream. It was a dream of peace. Here there can be none. It may be my karma perhaps. But this,” she continued, motioning at the brilliant room, “this is my prison.”
She hesitated and a bit lamely concluded:
“It is horrible to be in such a place.”

===Chinese===
蕾拉淡漠地表示同意。这一层她早已掂量过，似乎不太要紧。
「毫无疑问，」她答道。「不过命运很少关上一扇门而不打开另一扇。我跟你说过我的梦。那是一个安宁的梦。这里不可能有安宁。这也许是我的业。可是这个，」她继续道，朝那明晃晃的房间摆了摆手，「这是我的监狱。」
她迟疑了一下，有点无力地收尾道：
「待在这样一个地方，真可怕。」

===Original===
“And worse to be nowhere at all,” Violet shot at her. In firing she had sat up. Now, lolling back on the cushions, she enigmatically resumed: “You may be right though. I daresay it is dreadful to be in prison. I daresay it is even worse than you think.”
Enigmatic still, she smiled. With another shot, with two at most, Leilah, she told herself, would be routed.
“It is as though I were in a vortex,” the latter was saying. “It is as though I were being torn from places where I do not wish to be to others I may not like. But can one argue with a vortex? It is idle even to struggle. Whether you will or you won’t, you have to let yourself go.”
Lightly, in her most worldly manner, Violet laughed.

===Chinese===
「哪儿都不在，更糟，」维奥莱特朝她开了一枪。开火时她坐直了身子。此刻她又懒洋洋地靠回垫子上，玄奥地接着说：「不过你也许是对的。我敢说坐牢是可怕的。我敢说它甚至比你以为的还糟。」
她依旧玄奥地微笑着。再一枪，至多两枪，蕾拉就会被击溃，她对自己说。
「我就像在一个漩涡里，」后者正说着。「就像被从我不愿待的地方撕走，抛向我未必喜欢的别处。可谁能跟漩涡讲理呢？连挣扎都是徒劳。不管你愿意不愿意，都只得随它去。」
维奥莱特以她最世俗的方式，轻轻地笑了。

===Original===
“Never in the world! A woman should never let herself go﻿—except in an aeroplane. At a pinch a four-in-hand might do, but﻿—”
At the moment the lady was thoroughly *en veine* and, sure of victory, might have bantered on, but this, Aurelia, ushered by Parker, prevented.
Sharply Violet threw at her: “Where have you been?”
Ignoring Violet, Aurelia nodded at Leilah.

===Chinese===
「万万不可！一个女人永远不该放任自己——除非在飞机上。万不得已，四驾马车也许行，可是——」
此刻这位女士正*en veine*（法语「兴头正浓」），稳操胜券，本可以继续打趣下去，可奥蕾莉亚由帕克（Parker）引进来，打断了这一切。
维奥莱特尖刻地向她抛去：「你上哪儿去了？」
奥蕾莉亚不理维奥莱特，朝蕾拉点了点头。

===Original===
“I was here the other day, did they tell you?”
She turned and with blithe impertinence looked her sister up and down.
“Don’t be so ordinary as to ask a girl where she has been. If you are inquisitive, ask where she is going, and, if you ask me, I’ll tell you. I am going to become﻿—”
“Yes, yes.” Violet impatiently interrupted. “We’ve heard all that. You’re going to become a star. I know what kind too﻿—a fallen star.”

===Chinese===
「前几天我来过这儿，她们告诉你了吗？」
她转过身，快活而无礼地把她姐姐上上下下打量了一番。
「别俗气到去问一个姑娘她去过哪儿。你要是好奇，就问她要去哪儿，你要是问我，我就告诉你。我要成为——」
「行了，行了。」维奥莱特不耐烦地打断。「这一套我们都听过了。你要成为一颗明星。连哪一种我都知道——一颗陨落的星。」

===Original===
A fresher smile bubbled about Aurelia’s delicious mouth.
“It pleases your ladyship to jest. I am to become the Princess Farnese!”
“Merciful heavens!” Violet exclaimed, turning as she spoke to Leilah. “She’ll go in to dinner before me!”
But Leilah who, after a word of greeting to Aurelia, had, from the table before her, taken and reopened the Paramitas, perhaps did not hear.

===Chinese===
一个更清新的微笑在奥蕾莉亚那可人的嘴边泛起。
「夫人您说笑取乐。我可是要成为法尔内塞王妃（Princess Farnese）的！」
「老天爷！」维奥莱特惊呼，一边说一边转向蕾拉。「她要排在我前头入席了！」
可蕾拉——向奥蕾莉亚招呼过一句之后，就从面前的桌上拿起那篇论波罗蜜多的文章重新翻开——多半没有听见。

===Original===
Violet turned again to her sister. “When did he propose?”
With an air of amused contempt, again Aurelia looked her up and down.
“How antiquated you are! Don’t you know that all that sort of thing has gone out?”
“Do you mean that he didn’t propose?”

===Chinese===
维奥莱特又转向她妹妹。「他什么时候求的婚？」
奥蕾莉亚带着好笑的轻蔑，又把她上上下下打量了一遍。
「你多老派呀！你不知道那一套都过时了吗？」
「你是说他没有求婚？」

===Original===
“Of course not. I proposed to him.”
“What?”
“He was so coy about it, too,” Aurelia, quite as though she were eating sweetmeats, resumed. “He asked me all about my finances, what settlements I would make and whether I would object if he kept a separate establishment. You can’t really fancy how coy he was. He quite blushed and stammered, the poor thing.”
“I should think he might.”

===Chinese===
「当然没有。是我向他求的婚。」
「什么？」
「他还在那事儿上扭扭捏捏的呢，」奥蕾莉亚续道，那口气就像在吃蜜饯。「他问了我全部的财务状况，问我打算立怎样的婚约财产，还问我若他另置外宅我介不介意。你真想象不出他有多羞怯。他还脸红结巴来着，可怜的东西。」
「我想他也该如此。」

===Original===
“Yes, indeed. He asked me if I had spoken to his mamma, and I told him that while it was perhaps incorrect of me to speak to him in the first instance, yet that there could be no real love without a mutual misunderstanding, and now that we had one I would make a formal demand of the old lady today.”
Aurelia, executing a little *pas*, added: “I have just seen her.”
“Well?”
“She said: ‘And so you want my little boy for your husband.’ Partly that I told her, but partly also I want you for mother-in-law. You should have seen her lick her chops over that. Then she asked me about my affections. I told her they were just like the fashions. They came and went.”

===Chinese===
「可不是嘛。他还问我是否跟他妈妈说过，我告诉他，虽然由我先开口也许不合规矩，可是没有相互的误解就没有真正的爱情，如今我们既已有了一场误解，我今天就要向老太太正式提亲。」
奥蕾莉亚做了个小小的*pas*（舞步），补了一句：「我方才见过她了。」
「怎么样？」
「她说：『这么说，你想要我的小宝贝做丈夫。』我告诉她，一部分是，可另一部分，我想要你做我的婆婆。她听了那副咂嘴舔唇的样子你真该看看。随后她问起我的感情。我告诉她，我的感情就跟时装一样，来了又去。」

===Original===
“She must have been delighted.”
“She hugged me. Then, of course, I asked about him. She told me that he has just been named as corespondent in the Kincardine divorce suit, and I said that that was perfectly satisfactory.”
“What!”
“Perfectly satisfactory. It appears that the poor boy can’t remember whether the charge is true or false and that I think is so dear of him.”

===Chinese===
「她一定高兴坏了。」
「她抱了我。当然，随后我问起他。她告诉我，他刚在金卡丁（Kincardine）离婚案里被列为共同被告，我说那再满意不过了。」
「什么！」
「再满意不过。看来那可怜的男孩记不清那指控是真是假，我觉得他这一点真是可爱。」

===Original===
Violet, rising, extended her arms. “Aurelia, I have been wrong, I have misjudged you. Come to me.”
But Aurelia, moving back, waved her away. “You’ll only muss me. Now I must run. I must break the news to Parsnips. Will you come, Vi?”
“In a moment.” Violet answered. “Wait for me in the brougham. There is something I want to say to Leilah.”
Endearingly the ingénue smiled. “Something I ought not to hear, I suppose.” In speaking she made for the door. “The amount of things I ought not to hear and do hear is simply amazing.”

===Chinese===
维奥莱特站起来，张开双臂。「奥蕾莉亚，我一直错了，我看错你了。到我这里来。」
可奥蕾莉亚退后一步，挥手让她走开。「你只会把我弄皱。现在我得跑了。我得去把这个消息告诉欧防风（Parsnips）。你来吗，维？」
「一会儿就来。」维奥莱特答道。「在马车里等我。我有话要跟蕾拉说。」
那位天真少女动人地笑了笑。「想来是我不该听的话喽。」她一边说一边向门口走去。「我不该听见却听见了的事情，那数量简直惊人。」

===Original===
As the portières fell on her gracile back, Violet, with a gesture which, for so warlike a lady, resembled defeat, reseating herself, exclaimed:
“Frankly, she is impossible.”
At once though, buckling anew for the fray, she aimed at Leilah and fired.
“So are you.”

===Chinese===
门帘落在她纤细的背影后，维奥莱特做了一个姿势——对这么好战的一位女士来说，那姿势近乎认输——重新坐下，感叹道：
「坦白说，她简直没法要。」
不过她立刻重新束甲上阵，瞄准蕾拉开了火。
「你也一样。」

===Original===
Leilah, who had been considering the Paramitas, started at the attack which instantly was followed up by another.
“Do you know anything about the code here, the law code, I mean. Do you?”
Leilah, a bit bewildered, shook her head. “No, that is, very little.”
Violet’s eyes fairly snapped. “I congratulate you. A little is sometimes a great deal. I also know a little and that little is, well, immense, so enormous even that because of it I am in a position to tell you that if you go with Verplank, Barouffski can, if he catches you, have you jailed.”

===Chinese===
正在思忖波罗蜜多的蕾拉被这一击惊了一下，而紧跟着又是一击。
「你知道这儿的法典吗？我是说法律法典。你知道吗？」
蕾拉有点茫然，摇了摇头。「不知道，就是说，知之甚少。」
维奥莱特的眼睛简直啪啪作响。「那我得恭喜你。知一点点，有时就是知一大截。我也知道一点点，而那一点点，唔，浩瀚无边，大到大到——正因为它，我才有资格告诉你：你要是跟弗普兰克走，巴罗夫斯基只要逮住你，就能把你送进监狱。」

===Original===
Gravely, with curious calm, Leilah looked at her.
Annoyed that the shot had fallen short, Violet aimed again.
“Didn’t you hear me? I said jailed. *J*-*a*-*i*-*l*-*e*-*d*! Now I may add for two years. That is what I meant when you spoke of your prison. I agreed with you that it must be dreadful to be in one. I said I daresay it is even worse than you think.”
Leilah half raised a hand. “But﻿—”

===Chinese===
蕾拉神情凝重，带着一种奇异的平静，望着她。
这一枪没有打中，维奥莱特恼了，重新瞄准。
「你没听见吗？我说的是把你关起来。监——狱！现在我还可以补一句：两年。你说到你的监狱时，我就是这个意思。我同意你的话，坐牢一定可怕。我说了，我敢说它甚至比你以为的还糟。」
蕾拉半举起一只手。「可是——」

===Original===
Unheedingly Violet continued. “Now, as for Verplank, if Barouffski were to surprise him here with you he could kill you both, yes, and be acquitted. But you﻿—I have told you what he can do. Moreover, if he so much as suspects you, he can call the police. But excuse me. You were saying?”
“Nothing of any moment.”
But Violet persisted. “What was it?”
“Merely that during the Terror a woman went to the guillotine smelling a rose.”

===Chinese===
维奥莱特不理会，继续说。「再说弗普兰克。要是巴罗夫斯基在这里撞见他和你在一起，他可以把你们两个都杀了，是啊，还能无罪开释。可你——他能对你做什么，我已经告诉过你了。此外，他哪怕只是疑心你，也可以叫警察。不过对不起，你方才想说什么？」
「没什么要紧的。」
可维奥莱特不依不饶。「是什么？」
「只是想说，大恐怖时期，有个女人闻着一朵玫瑰走上了断头台。」

===Original===
At this flank movement Violet winced, but she rallied.
“Yes and rehearsed the act beforehand. They all did. The great ladies of the period rehearsed for the guillotine that they might die, as they had lived, with grace. Those were the good old days. These are the bad new ones. Anything of the kind would be ridiculous now.”
To this, gravely as before, Leilah assented. “No doubt. But aren’t you rather rambling about the grounds? It seems to me that you also have something up your sleeve.”
Violet rose to the challenge. “Something! I have the entire pharmacopoeia. In it is a remedy heroic but sovereign. If you’ll take it, it may maim but it will cure.”
Leilah, well entrenched, braved her. “I may not need it.”

===Chinese===
这一记侧翼回击让维奥莱特缩了一下，但她重整旗鼓。
「是啊，还事先排练过呢。她们都排练。那个时代的大贵妇们为断头台排练，好死得像活着时一样优雅。那是过去的好时光。如今是眼前的坏时光。这一套现在再来就可笑了。」
蕾拉像先前一样郑重地表示同意。「毫无疑问。不过你这不是在论据的园子里乱转吗？依我看，你袖子里也藏着东西呢。」
维奥莱特奋起应战。「什么东西！我有整整一部药典。里面有一剂药，峻猛却是灵药。你要肯服，它可能致残，但能除病。」
蕾拉深沟高垒，毫不畏惧。「我未必需要它。」

===Original===
With a rush, then, the attack began.
“That’s because you don’t know. You think you are sane and sound. Don’t you now? Yes, and you are neither. You have a high temperature complicated with nausea. The temperature is due to exposure, the nausea to indigestion. You have been exposed to Verplank and you have supped on Barouffski. You fancy you can’t be rid of the incubus. That is an hallucination which the fever has caused. You can be rid of it. You can throw it up. I have an emetic that will do the trick. It may not be tasty but I’ll warrant it will make him gag far more than you.”
At the ferocity of the assault, Leilah quailed. “Really, Violet, your language﻿—”
But the lady was not to be denied. “Aha! You want me to be butterfingered, don’t you? Not a bit of it. You shall have the dose whether you like it or not and here it is for you. You called this place a prison. Now who runs it? You do. And on what? Your money. Supposing you hadn’t any? Would Barouffski supply it? He would pack in a jiffy. Couldn’t you then sue for divorce? Of course you could. Then there is the emetic. Verplank has enough for you both, enough for an army. All you have to do is to hold your nose, open your mouth, give your money away and vomit Barouffski. Will you?”

===Chinese===
于是，攻击排山倒海地开始了。
「那是因为你不知道。你以为自己又健康又正常。是不是？哼，你两样都不是。你发着高烧，并发呕吐。发烧是因为受了风寒，呕吐是因为消化不良。你受的风寒叫弗普兰克，你吃下的夜宵叫巴罗夫斯基。你幻想自己摆脱不了这个梦魇。那是高烧引起的幻觉。你摆脱得了。你能把它吐出来。我有一剂催吐药，药到病除。它也许不好吃，可我保证，它让他作呕的程度，远胜过你。」
这猛攻的凶悍让蕾拉胆寒。「真的，维奥莱特，你这口气——」
可这位女士不容拒绝。「啊哈！你想让我手滑放过你，是不是？没门。这剂药你爱服不服，都得服，喏，就在这儿给你。你管这地方叫监狱。那么是谁在维持它？是你。靠什么？靠你的钱。假如你一个子儿都没有呢？巴罗夫斯基会供给吗？他立马卷铺盖。那时你还不能起诉离婚吗？当然能。这就是那剂催吐药。弗普兰克的钱够你们俩用，够一支军队用。你只需捏住鼻子，张开嘴，把钱散出去，把巴罗夫斯基呕吐干净。干不干？」

===Original===
It was Violet’s last shot. With it she had expected to bowl Leilah completely over. Anxiously she looked to see the result.
“Will you?” she repeated.
With manifest exaltation Leilah answered: “I had thought of it.”
Surprisedly Violet stared, wondering could all her ammunition have gone for nothing, wondering too at the almost rapt expression that had come into Leilah’s face.

===Chinese===
这是维奥莱特的最后一枪。她指望这一枪把蕾拉彻底打翻。她焦急地看着战果。
「干不干？」她重复道。
蕾拉带着明显的昂扬答道：「我想过这个办法。」
维奥莱特吃惊地瞪着眼，纳闷自己的弹药难道全部落了空，又纳闷蕾拉脸上浮现的那近乎出神的表情。

===Original===
“What?”
“Yes, Violet, I had thought of it﻿—and of other things also. While Aurelia was here, I was reading a little book. It told me what I once knew and had since forgotten. It told me that if we have ideals we should live for them, that it is only by living for them and suffering for them that we can lift ourselves above the brutes. Violet, it was sinful of me to think of going with Gulian.”
Dispassionately the now mollified lady toned that phrase.
“It was not perhaps quite nice.”

===Chinese===
「什么？」
「是的，维奥莱特，我想过它——也想过别的事情。奥蕾莉亚在这儿的时候，我在读一本小书。它告诉了我一些我曾经知道、后来忘掉的东西。它告诉我，我们若有理想，就该为理想而活，唯有为理想而活、为理想而受苦，我们才能把自己提升到畜生之上。维奥莱特，我竟想跟格里安走，这是罪过。」
这位如今软下来的女士不动声色地把这句话调了调色。
「这或许确实不太体面。」

===Original===
“But I had not seen,” Leilah continued. “No, not that,” she interrupted herself to explain. “I had forgotten that this prison, all I have endured, all I shall endure, these are my debts to the Lords of Karma.”
But that was a bit too strong. Violet laughed.
“You mean the Lords of Gammon.”
“I mean,” Leilah, with heightening fervour, replied, “that I have looked upon my prison as a cross. It is not a cross, it is a boon﻿—one of which I have not been worthy. For I forgot that any sorrow should be welcomed. I forgot that it has been sent to make us nobler than we are. But my sorrows I have not welcomed. I have rebelled against them. I shall rebel no more. They were my masters. They shall be my servants now.”

===Chinese===
「可我当时没有看明白，」蕾拉继续道。「不，不是那件事，」她插话解释，「是我忘了：这座监狱，我受过的一切苦，我将受的一切苦，都是我欠业主们（Lords of Karma，业之王）的债。」
可这就有点过火了。维奥莱特笑了。
「你的意思是『鬼话之主』（Lords of Gammon）吧。」
「我的意思是，」蕾拉答道，热忱愈发高涨，「我一直把我的监狱看作一副十字架。它不是十字架，它是一件恩赐——一件我不配领受的恩赐。因为我忘了，任何忧愁都应受到欢迎。我忘了，忧愁之降临，本是为使我们变得比现在更高尚。可我的忧愁，我从未欢迎过。我一直反抗它们。我再也不反抗了。它们曾是我的主人。从今往后，它们要做我的仆人。」

===Original===
At these fine sentiments Violet sniffed.
